32. **Camera-reference cached state goes stale — the reference is the ONE node that can skip `update()` (`ModularBody.cpp:247-261`, sole writer of `axisRotation`) while its consumers read it with no drawn gate (`Camera.cpp:92,476,591-599,510,909`). `Camera.hpp:425`'s `boundToSurface = true` is a persistent default, not the altitude invariant §11.93(b)'s dismissal assumed. Sharpest: `Camera.cpp:591/599` writes stale spin into PERSISTENT observer longitude (does not round-trip). Same node latches `areaOfInfluence` ⇒ AoI is less fresh than the `eclipticPos` it derives from.** — **OPEN (found 2026-07-24, §11.101(d); one fix — recompute the reference's spin+reach at the top of `Camera::update` — closes spin, longitude and the reference half of the B15 residual; existing gates blind by construction)** **[ANNOTATED 2026-07-25, F4 §11.108(e) — FIRST MEASURED OBSERVABLE OF THIS CLASS, and it adds a consumer the row did not list.** `Camera::descend` (`Camera.cpp:836-877`) reads the reference through `getObservedPosition()` = `mat.getTranslation()` (`ModularBody.hpp:1075-1077`), the cached eye-frame matrix refreshed only by the per-frame body update ⇒ **N uses inside ONE frame all see the same geometry and compound LINEARLY**: ten `descend(0.99)` with no intervening frame gave alt ×**0.899997** (measured 179.999429 km) against the geometric ×**0.9043821** (measured 180.876177 km with frames between); the linear prediction from the mechanism is ×0.9 exactly = 180.000019 km, so the residual is **0.59 m**. User-reachable: a script or the TCP channel can issue several `camera action descend` in one frame — a **D8 use-site-barrier** breach, not just a staleness nit. Same fix as the row's (recompute the reference's state at the top of `Camera::update`).]** **[CLOSED 2026-08-01, F20 §11.128(a)(a2), code `b4105a83`. The row's own fix landed as written — the reference's spin and reach are refreshed at the top of `Camera::update`, through the same single authorities `update()` calls (`ModularBody::refreshFrameState`). The ANNOTATION's claim that it is *"the same fix"* as the descend observable's is CORRECTED: a fix that fires once per frame cannot change what ten uses inside ONE frame do. The descend breach has its own root in the same sentence — the reference's eye-frame position is a COPY of the camera's own view matrix, republished once per frame — and is closed by making that composition a function (`Camera::viewMat`), which every consumer needing the reference in the eye frame now asks. MEASURED BOTH WAYS against the pre-fix binary, same scene, same commands: ten `descend 0.99` in one socket write give **179.999429 km pre-fix / 180.876177 km post**, against ten spread one per frame at 180.876177 either side — the two cadences **876.748 m apart before and 0.000 m after**; and the bound placement of a CULLED reference over 0.1 d of a 24 h body moves **0.000000° pre-fix / 36.000016° post** against 36.000000 predicted. **RESIDUAL, same class, recorded not fixed**: the FAR branch of `descend` reads `sel->getObservedPosition()` for the SELECTED body, which the camera cannot derive from its own state; the exact transport is `viewMat() · lastDispatchedMat⁻¹ · cached` and it is not taken because a matrix inverse in the common path perturbs a currently-exact value with float noise and the far case has no measured observable. **Also recorded**: the row's `setBoundToSurface` longitude conversion has NO runtime channel (B35's config-only class, sole caller `ssystem_factory.cpp:155`), so that pair is not traversable from any §2(c) channel — what is measurable, and what the conversion consumes, is the spin value itself.]**


34. **`Object::operator=` never releases the previous rep — both overloads (`tools/object.cpp:105-126`) overwrite `rep` after retaining the new one, so every reassignment of an `Object` leaks one reference.** Pre-existing and already live on the OLD path: a STAR selection leaks its refcounted `StarWrapperBase` on every subsequent selection change (the wrapper self-deletes at zero and never reaches zero). B24-select adds a second instance of the class — each composed-body selection leaks one `ModularObject` (~24 B) **plus a permanent entry in the static `ModularBodyPtr::ref` vector**, which is scanned on every body destruction. Bodies whose `retain`/`release` are no-ops (old `Body`, owned by `ProtoSystem`) are unaffected, which is why the defect stayed invisible. — **FIXED 2026-08-09 (F32, §11.142; code `71be9145`).** The row's own condition was paid first: **`Object::rep` is the ONLY declared `ObjectBase*` in `src/`** — every other long-lived holder is a counted `Object` (four members) or an `ObjectBaseP`, the only escape is `as<T>()` whose **six** call sites are all intra-expression, and the selection sets keep names rather than reps, so **no holder depends on the wrapper outliving its last retaining `Object`** and the stop boundary was not hit. Both overloads now retain-new / release-old (an order that also covers two Objects sharing one rep, and the null branch retains so the invariant is uniform); the enumeration is now the class's header documentation. **Measured both ways on one drive under ASan/LSan, per allocation site:** pre **11 `StarWrapper1` (440 B) + 8 `ModularObject` (192 B)** unreleased, post **0 + 0**, predictions written before the launch, all 17 selection readouts identical, heap delta exactly the defect (214→195 allocations, 632 B). Use-after-free churn on both paths (mixed types, tracking, `mode jump`, `body action reload`, reversible pairs twice): **0 ASan errors**, against **3 `heap-buffer-overflow`** from the same parser on a control launch. Parity: `b40_parity` 0 divergent fields on 120 bodies pre-vs-post binary, `b32`/`b24_equivalence` green, screen A/B inside its own A/A envelope. `[measured: harness/f32_object_leak.py, artifacts/f32/]`

35. **`day_key_mode` is an INERT capability end to end — a control surface that changes nothing.** `App::setDayKeyMode` (`app.hpp:129-130`) declares `std::string DayKeyMode = _value;` — a **local that shadows the member** — so the setter is a no-op; its only caller is the TUI's "Day keys" item (`ui_tuiconf.cpp:1036`, calendar/sidereal). And the member itself has **no behavioural consumer anywhere**: read from config (`app.cpp:628`), displayed in the TUI (`ui_tuiconf.cpp:688`), written back on save (`app.cpp:931`) — while the day keys emit `date sidereal ±1` / `date relative ±1` as hardcoded strings (`ui.cpp:1506,1523,1689,1705,1716,1731,1857,1874`). So the operator can select a mode that neither sticks nor would do anything if it did. — **OPEN (found by the S6 capability audit 2026-07-25, §11.108(f); NOT fixed: un-shadowing alone makes an inert value stick and get persisted to config, and giving it a consumer changes what the day keys DO — a user-visible semantics decision, not an implementation step)**

36. **`media subtitle toggle` turns subtitles OFF instead of toggling — a wrong-variable test, one word from correct.** `app_command_interface.cpp:3329` tests `argAction == W_TOGGLE` inside the branch guarded by `!argSubtitle.empty()`; the same command family's other implementation, 150 lines above at `:3178`, tests `argSubtitle == W_TOGGLE` and is right. So `media subtitle toggle` leaves `argAction` empty, falls through to `Utility::isTrue("toggle") == false` and calls `playerSetShowSubtitles(false)`. — **OPEN (found by the F4 command-grammar census 2026-07-25, §11.108(k) / B38; NOT fixed although the fix is one word: its counterfactual is not observable on this host — `~/.spacecrafter/videos` is empty and the subtitle state has no readout on any control channel — and an unverified user-visible behaviour change is exactly what the S-class rule excludes. Lands trivially in any wave that carries a media asset; the sibling at `:3178` is the specification.)** **[CONVERGENCE 2026-08-04: the scedit sweep (unit 3), reading blind of this row, rediscovered the identical wrong-variable test — line drifted :3329 → :3764 at HEAD; the standalone-`subtitle` branch reads `argAction` where `action play`'s reading of the same key (:3613) is correct. Independent rediscovery confirms the row; scedit's doc line for `media subtitle` blocks on the same fix-or-document answer.]**





41. **`camera action save` cannot succeed as shipped (doubled path prefix), and destroys the date when it can.** `CoreLink::cameraSave` prepends `"anchors/"` (`coreLink.cpp:57-59`) and `SSystemFactory::cameraSave` prepends `"anchors/"` **again** (`ssystem_factory.hpp:871-873`), so `AnchorManager::saveCameraPosition` (`anchor_manager.cpp:696-739`) opens `anchors/anchors/<name>`; the load leg doubles identically (`coreLink.cpp:62-64`, `ssystem_factory.hpp:875-877`). `~/.spacecrafter/anchors` does not exist on this install ⇒ the command always fails. **MEASURED, ladder one variable at a time, predictions first** (2026-07-25, §11.110(d)): no `anchors/` → FAILS with `AnchorManager::saveCameraPosition couldn't create a file anchors/anchors/f6test`; `anchors/` created → **still FAILS, same path**; `anchors/anchors/` created → **succeeds**. Two further properties of the file it then writes: it carries **old-path authorities only** (Observer lat/lon/alt, `Navigator::getLocalVision`, TimeMgr jd — no heading, fov, mount, reference or selection), and it is written with **default ostream precision**, so the session's JD **2461247.34443** came back as `time 2.46125e+06` = **2461250** — a **2.66-day error** with a **10-day** round-trip resolution. — **OPEN (found by the B31 design pass 2026-07-25, §11.110(d); NOT fixed: the prefix is a one-line fix but the surface is a user-visible command whose repair makes a previously-dead path live, and the precision half changes the file's format; both belong in a wave that can verify them. B31's design (§3.4(d)) requires this surface to be re-expressed on the session serializer rather than extended.) **[CONVERGENCE 2026-08-04: the scedit sweep (unit 4), reading blind of this row, rediscovered the doubled `"anchors/"` prefix — lines drifted to coreLink.cpp:65/:70 + ssystem_factory.hpp:946/:950 at HEAD. Row confirmed, nothing new; recorded so the rediscovery is not re-registered later as a fresh finding.]****

42. **`configuration action save` rewrites the user's `config.ini` destroying every comment, and `InitParser::load` aborts the process on one malformed line.** `App::saveCurrentConfig` (`app.cpp:907-941`) → `InitParser::save` (`init_parser.cpp:75-86`) → `iniparser_dump_ini` (`iniparser.c:258-281`), which re-emits the parsed dictionary as `%-30s = %s` and has no representation for comments. **MEASURED on a farm copy** (2026-07-25, §11.110(c), 8 predictions committed first, all matched): comment lines **3 → 0** (file-level AND in-section); an inline `; …` trailing comment stripped out of its value; `F6_MixedCase_Key` → **`f6_mixedcase_key`** (`strlwc`, `iniparser.c:657-688`); unknown key and unknown section preserved; key/section order preserved; and **9 keys the user never authored materialized** (`nautical_alt`, `nautical_ra`, `object_coordinates`, `mouse_coordinates`, `angular_distance`, `loxodromy`, `orthodromy`, `vertical_line`, `flag_star_pick`), 325 → 330 lines. Two source-certain riders: the save opens the **target itself** with `fopen(…, "wt")` (`init_parser.cpp:79`) — truncate in place, no sibling temp, no rename ⇒ **non-atomic**, the failure mode §11.52(a) clause 2 exists to prevent; and `iniparser_load` returns NULL if ANY line is a syntax error (`iniparser.c:812-818`), whereupon `InitParser::load` calls **`exit(-1)`** (`:62-72`) ⇒ **a single malformed line in `config.ini` kills the app at startup**. So one user-editable file is governed by refusal-on-read and destruction-on-write — the exact inverse of the §11.66(b) preservation contract mandated for the other user-editable format. — **OPEN (found by the B31 design pass 2026-07-25, §11.110(c); NOT fixed: making the config writer comment-preserving is the §11.66(b) writer's job on a second format, and making the reader survive a bad line changes startup semantics on frozen field configs — both are B31-adjacent work with their own verification. The immediate design consequence is recorded instead: the session file is NOT `config.ini`, and B31 must not extend this writer's reach.)**

43. **`ModularBodyPtr`'s implicitly generated copy constructor produced an UNREGISTERED pointer — its destructor then walked the tracking list off the end.** The class registers each instance in the static `ref` vector and increments the body's `pointerCount` in its raw-pointer constructor; the implicit COPY constructor did neither, while the destructor unconditionally runs `while (ref[++i] != this);` [observed: `ModularBodyPtr.cpp:14-24`] — so destroying a copy scanned past the end of `ref` (SIGSEGV), and while the copy lived the body's pin count was one short of its actual holders (the very I5 guarantee this pointer exists to provide). Latent as long as every ModularBodyPtr lived at a stable address; **MEASURED the first time one lived inside a value a container RELOCATES** (2026-07-25, the B4 anchor registry): SIGSEGV at `ModularBodyPtr.cpp:19` inside `__relocate_object_a`, on the 6th anchor of the shipped `anchor.ini`, first run (backtrace in §11.111(f)). — **FIXED 2026-07-25 (§11.111(f), code `2d1387b5`): the copy constructor delegates to the raw-pointer constructor, so registration keeps ONE authority (I2) and a copy is exactly a second holder.** Rider recorded, NOT fixed: `ModularBodySelector` inherits that constructor and its own destructor deselects, so copying a SELECTOR remains a hazard of a different kind (no crash — a spurious deselect when the copy dies); nothing copies one today.

44. **OLD PATH: unhiding a body RE-SHOWS descendants that were hidden independently — `showBodyRecursive` has no memory of why a child was hidden.** `ProtoSystem::setPlanetHidden` routes both directions through the recursive pair (`protosystem.cpp:271-298`): `hideBodyRecursive` hides every descendant, `showBodyRecursive` **shows** every descendant unconditionally — while `hideBody`/`showBody` are pure membership edits on `renderedBodies`/`sortedRenderedBodies` (`protosystem.hpp:196-203`) that record nothing about the origin of the state they change. So a body authored `hidden = true` (`initialHidden`, `:947-966`) or hidden by a script, sitting under a parent the operator hides and then unhides, comes back VISIBLE — the authored/commanded state is destroyed by an unrelated operation on an ancestor. Reachable from the shipped command surface (`body name <parent> hidden true|false`); the shipped `[Solar_System]`/`[Goldilocks_Zone]` hidden bodies hang off `Sun`, so `body name Sun hidden true` then `false` is the two-command reproduction. — **OPEN, OLD PATH ONLY, NOT TO BE FIXED THERE (found 2026-07-29 by the D23 propagation's corpus check, §11.113(b)(v); the old path is the frozen comparison baseline and this is a defect in it, tracked not reproduced per §11.52(b)).** The NEW path is already correct **by construction, not by a guard**: `hide()` parks the node in its parent's `hiddenBodies` with ownership and relation intact (`ModularBody.cpp:445-481`), so the subtree leaves every walk as a side effect of nesting and no descendant's own state is touched — which is exactly what [vixy, D23] requires (*"mustn't change the exposed hidden attribute/flag"*). The row exists so B39 does not "port" the old behaviour on the assumption that old is the spec, and so the divergence is named before it is measured.


46. **`dispatchUpdate`'s UP-CHAIN never writes `matLocalToBodyPos`, though the member's own contract says it is *"Set on EVERY position update (visible or not)"*.** [observed 2026-07-30, §11.117(k)(2): the `while (body->isNotIsolated)` loop assigns `body->mat = parentTilted` and calls `preUpdate`/`update`, and nothing writes the flat position frame; every DOWNWARD path does write it — `recursiveUpdate`, `selectiveUpdate`'s else-branch and `recursiveTranslationUpdate` all set it explicitly and say why.] So for every ancestor ABOVE the camera's reference body the cached flat frame is whatever it was when the walk last descended through it. Consequence beyond the finding: `ModularSystem::drawOrbits`/`drawTrails`/`drawTails` build each body's parent frame from `body.getMatLocalToBodyPos()`, so an up-chain ancestor's OWN orbit/trail/tail line is placed in a stale frame — reachable on today's corpus (observer on the Moon ⇒ Earth is up-chain and carries a TRAIL module), which is exactly the class §11.39 believed it had closed (*"the cached frame is correct for EVERY body, visible or not"* — true of the descent, false of the climb). — **OPEN (found 2026-07-30 by B39; NOT fixed there: the one-line fix CHANGES RENDERED OUTPUT for a body B39 does not otherwise touch, and B39 does not need it — `publishParkedFrame` captures the frame at the six sites that provably have it. Fix shape: assign `matLocalToBodyPos = flat` in the up-chain loop where `mat = parentTilted` is assigned, and A/B the orbit/trail of an up-chain ancestor to see what moves.)** — **FIXED 2026-08-09 (F29, §11.139; code `bd3f7117`). The recorded fix shape held; the row's ENUMERATION did not — the class is TWO sites, not one.** A whole-file enumeration of the five writers of `mat` found the same omission in `dispatchUpdate`'s INVISIBLE-REFERENCE branch (`ModularBody.cpp:343`), reachable by looking away from a distant reference rather than by standing on a moon (§5.32's own branch); `flat` is that body's own flat position frame at BOTH sites by construction, so one line each. **Measured both ways on the pre/post pair, prediction committed first (harness `2af2ee1`, before the instrument existed):** the invariant `eclRoot == mat.translation` — exact, because `mat` is always this frame times a pure rotation — is violated by EXACTLY the up-chain (`Earth`,`Sun`,`SolarSystem` with the observer on the Moon; `Sun`,`SolarSystem` in the shipped Earth scene) pre-fix and by **nobody, in five scenes, over 120 bodies** post-fix. At the render: the subject's TRAIL passed **550.39 px** from Earth and **0.70 px** from `project(cached frame)` pre-fix, and **9.2e-05 px** from Earth post-fix, against a descent body at **0.69 px on both binaries** — with the pre-fix miss PREDICTED (549.7 px) from the freeze leg's measured 37.58° off-axis angle, not fitted. Both control scenes bit-identical (max channel diff 0); the two ancestors of the shipped Earth scene have no rendered consequence structurally (they carry `orbit:0 trail:0 tail:0`). **The reversible pair DESCENT⇄UP-CHAIN driven twice** (`Earth→Moon→Earth→Moon→Earth`, each entry from the previous exit's state) gives the defect in one number: pre-fix, standing on the Moon, Earth's own cached frame says Earth is **6378.240 km** away — its own radius plus the observer's 100 m, the value the last Earth-standing frame left — instead of **359 624 km**; post-fix it tracks the state at every entry, the two entries agreeing to 0.032 km. A user-visible line for the pre-fix behaviour: the stale line went straight from a frozen matrix into eye coordinates, so it did NOT move when the observer turned their head. Residues recorded not fixed: `parkedChildFrame` is now a provable duplicate of this member (§11.139(k)(1), collapsing it is B39-barrier work), **§5.75**, and the orbit pass's depth-bucket clipping.


47. **`get status position` produces NO REPLY on the client connection.** [observed+measured 2026-07-30, §11.118(i)] `AppCommandInterface::commandGet` resolves the `status position` argument and calls `tcp->setOutput(coreLink->tcpGetPosition())`; `ServerSocket::setOutput` pushes the string onto `outputQueue` under its lock and returns. Nothing arrived on the driving socket in a **6 s poll, twice**, in a session where `timerate`, `date`, `flag`, `select`, `body action dual_dump` and `body action screenshot` all worked on that same connection — the app's own log carries no "No tcp : i can't send" warning either, so the command ran and the queue was fed. Consequence beyond the missing string: `get status position` is the ONLY side-effect-free read of the observer position AND of the heading readout, so its silence forces every heading instrument through `heading delta_azimuth`, which WRITES both authorities after it reads (that is why §11.118(f)'s two `experimental_path` pins needed two launches). — **FIXED 2026-08-02 (F27, §11.135; code `d13681eb`). The three hypotheses, answered `[observed: src/tools/io.cpp @ d9de42ac]` and then MEASURED on the wire:** (1) *drained at all* — **yes, on every pass** of `ServerSocket::run` (`checkDataToSend`, io.cpp:388; the loop's own wait is a 1 ms `SDLNet_CheckSockets`), emptying the whole queue each time (io.cpp:630-639); (2) *drained to a different socket* — **yes**: through `broadcast` (io.cpp:641-655), which addresses the clients whose `clientBroadcastTab` entry is true, a table set true in exactly ONE place, the **`$LOGON`** command (io.cpp:614), so an answer reached the issuer only if the issuer had subscribed to the LOG feed, and with nobody subscribed it was popped and lost in silence; (3) *only when a second client is connected* — **REFUTED**, the client count is irrelevant (one subscribed client receives; sixteen unsubscribed receive nothing). **The root is one hop earlier**: the origin of a request was known at `computeNormalString(client, string)` and dropped on the next line (`inputQueue.push(string)`, io.cpp:625), and `setOutput` had no addressee to name — the structure did not carry what was asked of it (I6). Delivered: request and answer are a `ClientMessage` naming the slot AND the connection id that slot held (I5 — slots are reused); `getInput` latches the request being served and clears the latch when the batch is drained; `deliver` sends to that connection **and**, unchanged, to the `$LOGON` subscribers minus the double copy. **Measured both ways** (`harness/f27_reply.py`, twelve fresh launches): the 6 s poll reproduces at **6.007 s / 6.006 s of silence** pre-fix and answers in **0.002 s / 0.006 s** delivered; the same socket after `$LOGON` answers in **0.002 s on the PRE-FIX binary** (the positive map: the drain works, the subscription was the condition); listener + issuer go from 0/1 to 1/1, before and after the issuer's connection is replaced in the same slot. The consequence this row recorded is discharged: the heading pin is now readable **3/3 in ONE launch** across two `experimental_path` toggles, against 0/3 pre-fix. Two residues, both recorded not fixed: **§5.72** (the `$LOGON` channel still carries other clients' answers — preserved deliberately, semantics = Vixy's) and **§5.73** (the `strcpy` size arithmetic on the same path).

48. **The §11.18 `ASmooth` NaN still fires on a cold `$HOME`, on whichever body the config scales at init.** [measured 2026-07-30, §11.118(i)] On a freshly built temp-HOME farm the app intermittently leaves `ModularBody::scaling` non-finite, so `boundingRadius`, `scaledDatumRadius` and `scaledGroundRadius` all dump as `nan` — observed on the **Moon** once and on the **Sun** once, which is exactly the pair `SSystemFactory::setPlanetSizeScale` targets by name from `moon_scale`/`sun_scale` at startup. `EntityCore/Executor/ASmooth.hpp` already carries a guard for the same shape, with the same observable in its own comment (*"double setScaling at init → Moon scaledRadius NaN → frozen main loop once the Moon became the camera reference"*), so this is a residue of that class rather than a new one. **Attributed to NEITHER binary**: 2 fires in the first 6 cold launches across two binaries, then **0 of 4 vs 0 of 4** in a controlled alternating repeat of the same corpus. — **OPEN (found 2026-07-30 by F12; NOT fixed: `EntityCore/` is read-only for this work, and the trigger is a startup race whose reproduction rate is ~15 % at best. Instrument consequence CLOSED at the root in the same wave: `b24_equivalence.load_dump` used to drop a NaN-carrying body silently — C++ prints `nan`, Python's json rejects it — so the body read as ABSENT to every gate built on that loader; it is now parsed and visible, and fails a value check loudly instead of disappearing.)**

49. **The OBSERVER's longitude origin is the mesh x̂ column (texture u = 0.75), not the map's centre column (u = 0.5) — the convention §5.28's fix has just made authoritative for the IAU meridian. On Earth, whose map is Greenwich-centred at u = 0.5, `moveto lon 0` stands above the column 90° away.** [derived from source 2026-07-30, §11.120(j); **NOT measured at the render — veto point stated with the finding**] Composition `[observed]`: `Camera.cpp:481` builds `… · xrotation(latitude − π/2) · zrotation(−longitude)` and `:484` appends `computeSurfaceToBody()` = `zrotation(−getAxisRotation())` (`ModularBody.hpp:495`) ⇒ the observer's zenith sits at body azimuth `axisRotation + π/2 + longitude`, while texture column u is drawn at `axisRotation + 180° + 360°·u` (`SphereObjL.cpp:153` + `ModularBody.hpp:487`) ⇒ **u = longitude/360 − 0.25**. Why it has never been seen: a surface observer does not look at their own body's map, so no shipped scene puts the two conventions in one frame — the same reachability shape §5.28 had. **Untouched by F14** (the fix spins the mesh and the observer's surface frame together, so a point at (lat, lon) keeps its texture column). **OPEN** — a user-visible-semantics change, so not fixed opportunistically. Discriminating test it owes: observer at (0, 0) on a body with a registered map, looking down, texture column under the sub-observer point vs the map's own centre — or `f14_meridian.py`'s `u_sub` against a commanded longitude. — **SHARPENED 2026-08-26 (F40, §11.153(k)), AND THE ROW'S OWN CONCLUSION IS IN DOUBT; nothing acted on.** The observer half of this derivation is now **MEASURED**, twice and independently (the rover angles of `f40_disc.py` — law of cosines on dumped distances AND the closed form off the drawn matrix, agreeing to < 0.0001° in 24 states — and `b24_screen`'s rover distances, 5096 km vs 5995 km at a 5737 km observer radius): **the observer's pose azimuth in the reference's SURFACE frame is `longitude − π/2`**, because `viewMat`'s anchored branch solved for the eye is `d·(cosφ sinλ, −cosφ cosλ, sinφ)` whose azimuth is `λ − π/2`. This row's chain says the zenith sits at `axisRotation + π/2 + longitude`; the composition gives `axisRot + π/2 + (lon − π/2) = axisRot + lon` — **the row's arithmetic omits exactly that −π/2**. Its TEXTURE half is confirmed at source and unchanged: `tex[0] = i/10 − 0.25` beside `pos = (cos, sin)(i·2π/10)` `[observed: SphereObjL.cpp:151-153]` puts column `u` at mesh azimuth `90° + 360u`, and `getAxisRotation() = axisRotation + π/2` `[observed: ModularBody.hpp:575]` puts it at body azimuth `axisRot + 180° + 360u` — this row's own formula. Composing the corrected observer half gives **`u = longitude/360 − 0.5`**, i.e. `moveto lon 0` stands above `u = 0.5`, **the map's CENTRE column — the opposite of this row's headline**. Both sides are still `[derived]`; the render measurement this row already says it owes (`u_sub`) is what settles it. F40 changed NOTHING here: the 90° between `moveto lon` and `orbit_lon` (§11.152(o)) is unchanged across F40's two binaries in ANCHORED mode, by construction and by measurement.

50. **A script that pushes a body with `coord_func = surface_point` KILLS THE APP.** [measured 2026-07-30, §11.121(j): the process died mid-session (socket broken, no error line after the load) on `body action load name ProbeA parent Earth type Artificial radius 50 coord_func surface_point orbit_lon 5.3667 orbit_lat 43.3 orbit_alt 400000 tex_map bodies/generic.png`.] **Mechanism, at source**: `surface_point` is a NEW-path orbit family (`SurfacePointOrbitLoader`); the OLD path's `orbitCreator` chain does not know it, logs *"something went wrong when creating orbit from <name>"* — **and then carries on with `orb == nullptr`**, building a `Body` around the null orbit (`protosystem.cpp:612-622`; the very next branch, `coord_func == "ell_orbit"`, would dereference it outright). Both paths receive the same push map (`SSystemFactory::addBody`), so any script using the new path's own orbit family through the push channel takes the app down; the same value in a COMPOSED FILE is fine (only the new path reads it), which is why `b24_screen`'s surface_point rovers have never fired it. **Attributed by source + log, NOT by a counterfactual binary** (no pre-F15 binary was kept — the weaker evidence is recorded as such; F15 touches neither `protosystem.cpp` nor the orbit creators). — **FIXED 2026-07-31 (§11.124(h), code `ebb41ab2`)**: the guard sits after the WHOLE orbit-determination block, not inside the branch that failed — every path either builds an orbit or returns, and the next statement dereferences it for `ell_orbit` while `Body`'s update path dereferences it every frame, so the class is *no orbit, no body*. Both ways on the row's verbatim reproduction: **pre-fix rc=139 SIGSEGV** (which also sharpens this row's *"died mid-session, socket broken, no error line"* into a signal), post-fix **rc=0** with the §2(f) line naming the body, the value, the valid values and the fix action. The skip is WITNESSED, not asserted: in the same launch's `dual_dump`, ProbeA carries a **null old half and a live new one** (skipped here, created there — `SSystemFactory::addBody` feeds both paths from one map and the old path returning is what lets the new one run), while the control ProbeB (`still_orbit`) carries **both halves**; the composed-file rover control (`b24_screen`) stays green A1–A5. Rider recorded, not fixed: the valid-value list is spelled out in the message because no `OrbitCreator` exposes the keyword it handles — an I2 duplication named in the code comment (the same one `CameraAnchors`' anchor diagnostic carries).

51. **`BodyModule` has no virtual destructor, so NO module destructor has ever run — 373 of them are skipped on every shutdown.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.122(h), AddressSanitizer on `build-asan`: `new-delete-type-mismatch`, *size of the allocated type 200 bytes, size of the deallocated type 16 bytes*, at `ModularBody.cpp:227` (`components.clear()`); **373 reports in one shutdown, 746 on a `qda` cycle** where `body action reload` doubles the population.] **At source**: `class BodyModule` (`BodyModule.hpp:149`) declares a pure-virtual `draw` and **no destructor at all**, so the implicit one is non-virtual; its **13 subclasses** (`OjmModule`, `LayeredMesh`, `BasicMesh`, `RingModule`, `TailModule`, `TrailModule`, `StarModule`, `OrbitModule`, `OortModule`, `PlanetGridModule`, `AtmExtModule`, `AxisModule`, `HintModule`) are owned polymorphically by `std::vector<std::unique_ptr<BodyModule>> components` (`ModularBody.hpp:1671`) and deleted through the base ⇒ UB by [expr.delete]/3, and every module's own cleanup — GPU resources included — is silently skipped. Invisible without a sanitizer: glibc's `free` ignores the size hint, so the wrong-size deallocation survives, and a leak that the process exit reclaims has no native observable. Note it also **masks measurement**: any teardown result is currently taken through 373 destructors that did not run. — **FIXED 2026-07-31 (§11.124(a)–(g), code `0dd0a938`), and the owed check paid in full.** The one line landed with the header stating what BOUNDS a module destructor, since that is not visible from the class: bodies die before `Context` and its managers (`App` declares `context` before `core`; `~App` waits idle first), but ALSO **mid-session on `body action reload`**, between frames, with no `waitIdle` — so the release channels were checked at source first (registry-pool `Set`s never reach `vkFreeDescriptorSets`; `SharedBuffer`/`VertexBuffer` return to LIVE `BufferMgr`s; `s_texture` goes through the 3-deep deferred ring). **Subclass count corrected: 14, not 13** — F9 added `PhotosphereModule` after this row was written; the 373 counts instances, not classes, so it is unchanged. **Measured, same cycle, three teardown entries, both ways**: `new-delete-type-mismatch` **373 → 0** (shutdown command), **746 → 0** (`qda` reload cycle), **373 → 0** (old path); heap-use-after-free stays **0**; rc=0 throughout; and **no report of ANY other class appears** — the check that mattered, since this is where a first-ever-run destructor would have fired. Re-measured on the delivered binary (`ebb41ab2`), not inherited. **Instrument rider the next hunt needs**: those counts require `ASAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=0`; at ASan's default the process prints one report and exits 1, cutting the shutdown short. **Validation**: layer positively loaded, its message channel located (`~/.spacecrafter/log/vulkan.log`, never stdout) and shown able to fire (27 BestPractices messages), then **0 VUID / 0 validation messages** and rc=0 on BOTH teardown entries after a completed mid-session reload. **Native quiet batch, delivered binary, full §11.95 9-variant mix: N = 54 — 54 CLEAN, 0 fires, 0 hangs, 0 invalid** (≤ 5.5 % per teardown, rule of three).

52. **In the DEPTH-LESS MID BAND the new path draws no body surface at all — the old path draws it.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.123(g): the Sun tracked at `zoom fov 100`, its big halo suppressed (`body name Sun color halo r 0 g 0 b 0`) so the disc is what the pixels show. `screenSize = 0.00524`, i.e. inside `0.0015 < screenSize ≤ 0.008` where `ModularBody::draw` calls `nearComponents → drawNoDepth()`. Row through the disc centre, old path: `178 720 725 722 723 720 717 716 720 723 725 270` (sum-of-RGB, max 765) over a ~11 px disc. Same row, new path: **all zero**; the only non-zero pixels within 30 px of the centre lie at r ≥ 9 px (the far components' hint ring). Identical in the pre-B12 and post-B12 binaries — the module that fills the MESH slot is not the variable.] The band is not a corner: it is every body between roughly 3 and 16 px across at a 2048-wide render — the regime §2.0 D3 calls the *common* case for anything the observer is not looking at. **Mechanism [derived, NOT instrumented]**: `drawNoDepth` is the one draw branch that issues **no `clearDepth`**, so the geometry is projected with whatever depth slice the last `clearDepth` established; `custom_project.glsl` maps `depth = (|eye| - clipping_fov.x) / (clipping_fov.y - clipping_fov.x)`, so a body outside that slice lands outside `[0,1]` and is depth-clipped whole. The old path draws its mid-band bodies inside their own command buffer with their own depth handling (`Sun::drawGL`'s `needClearDepthBuffer`), which is why the divergence is one-sided. A second candidate — `renderer.bind(family, VARIANT_NO_DEPTH)` returning no layout, i.e. the early return in every mesh module's `drawNoDepth` — is NOT excluded by these measurements. — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F9 while measuring the star surface across regimes; record-don't-fix by that task's stop boundary — it is a MESH-family/regime defect, not a star one, and it predates B12. It also **corrects a recorded conclusion**: §11.44's *"at native fisheye (mid-band, drawNoDepth) the disc is bright in both"* was reading the additive big halo, not the disc; with the halo suppressed the new path's mid-band disc is empty. Discriminating check the fix owes: this same row, halo suppressed, non-zero in both paths — and the same on a planet, since nothing here is star-specific.) — **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by F18, §11.127(a).** The two candidates were DISCRIMINATED BY INSTRUMENT before anything was fixed: the second is **REFUTED** (`layout` non-null and the NO_DEPTH bit present at every mesh family — the early return never fires), the first **CONFIRMED** (NDC z = 11574 Sun / 23588 Mars / 71345 Jupiter / 29.9 Moon with the previous body's range still in place; the rasterizer's depth CLIP is a fixed-function stage `VARIANT_NO_DEPTH` does not disable — it turns off the TEST and WRITE only). Fixed at the root by separating the depth MAPPING from the slice ENTRY: `Renderer::enterDepthlessSlice` sets the range and nothing else — no attachment clear, no command-buffer boundary, which is precisely the cost the band exists to avoid (D3) — and `clearDepth` now re-establishes its bucket's range on every call, so a mid-band body sorting between two same-bucket bodies cannot leave its override behind. Owed check MET, on the Sun AND on planets: disc-integrated ratio vs old **0.84 / 0.88 / 0.99** post-fix against **0.00 / 0.00 / 0.11** on the pre-fix binary; z = 0.5 exactly, as predicted before the run; b12's own mid-band scene 0.0000 → 0.9917.

53. **The reduced and the full level of one colour map do not render alike — a body visibly changes colour when it crosses the big-texture threshold, and the two paths cross it at different sizes.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.123(g), disc-centre RGB on the Sun, both paths: reduced level `[253, 246, 218]` (near-white), full level `[250, 199, 107]` (the map's own orange; the file's mean is `[250.7, 195.9, 94.5]` over 8192×4096, so the FULL level is the faithful one). Ring medians over the whole disc: 720 vs 540 on the sum-of-RGB scale — a **33 %** step in luminance and a large step in saturation.] Both paths ladder the same `s_texture`, so this is not a parity divergence; what differs is *when*: the old path swaps at `screen_sz ≥ 180` px diameter (`Sun::getSet`), the new one at `screenSize > 0.2`, i.e. 409 px at a 2048-wide render — so between those two sizes an A/B compares two different textures, which is a trap for any future disc measurement (F9 hit it: a near-approach ratio read 1.34 instead of 1.00 until the scene was moved above both thresholds). — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F9; record-don't-fix. Two independent questions inside it: (a) why the reduced level is washed out — a downscale preserves the mean, so the cause is in the reduction/cache path (`s_texture`, `txcache`), not in mipmapping; (b) whether the two paths' switch sizes should agree, which is a §11.52(b) parity question only for as long as both paths exist. Discriminating check: the same disc-centre RGB either side of the threshold, on a body whose map has a known mean.) — **(a) ROOT-CAUSED 2026-07-31 by F18, §11.127(c), and it is DATA, not the reduction path.** This row's own premise is what proves it: **Jupiter, which has no preview file, reads a level ratio of 0.998 ≈ 1.000** [measured] — a downscale does preserve the mean, and the reduction path is faithful. What happens instead is that the loader prefers an authored `<name>-preview.<ext>` over any downscale (`tools/s_texture.cpp:157-163`), and the two shipped previews are photometrically inconsistent with their full-resolution partners: `sun-preview.jpg` mean **[254.0,246.2,223.5]** vs `sun.jpg` **[250.7,195.9,94.5]**, `moon-preview.jpg` **[184.1,183.3,181.5]** vs `moon.jpg` **[150.5,147.0,146.1]**. The Sun's rendered step reproduces its two files' mean ratio to **0.5 %** ([1.016,1.257,2.352] measured vs [1.013,1.257,2.365] predicted); the Moon's is neutral (1.46 on all three channels) as its own files predict in SHAPE, with an achromatic ×1.13 excess named to its normal/height maps swapping level at the same gate. Exactly TWO bodies are affected tree-wide — they are the only `tex_map` values with previews — and **the Moon is the second, previously unnoticed**. NOT decision-free, so NOT fixed: correcting the assets is a §2.0 D9 forward propagation through `spacecrafter-data` (**Vixy's, and the recommendation**); making the loader reject an inconsistent preview is rejected (it would silently override authored data, and an authored low level legitimately may differ); the decision-free rider — a D12 log of the substitution — was BUILT and **WITHDRAWN** because it never fired while the preview's pixels were demonstrably on screen (the string is in the binary), and a diagnostic that cannot be shown to fire is a silent no-op probe. Its correct placement needs the texture-cache path understood and is its own task; a naive unconditional probe at `asyncLoad` entry ABORTED the app at startup. **(b) MEASURED 2026-07-31, §11.127(d), NOT decided**: old **180 px** diameter [observed: `body_bigbody.cpp:441`, `body_smallbody.cpp:113`], new **409.6 px**, ratio 2.28. Recommendation: align to 180 while both paths exist — between the two sizes an A/B compares two different textures, and (a) has just shown the step is a real colour change on two shipped bodies. Left to Vixy because engaging the big texture earlier means more VRAM resident sooner, a D5/D6 residency and D10 headroom question; the two gates were deliberately kept as separate constants so the change is a one-token edit. — **OPEN** (on (a)'s asset correction and (b)'s decision; the renderer side is settled).

54. **Two spellings of the G4 threshold family, and the named one is dead.** [observed 2026-07-31, §11.123(g): `BODY_EARLY_VISIBILITY_BOUNDING_SIZE = 2` and `BODY_FULL_VISIBILITY_BOUNDING_SIZE = 16` are declared in PIXELS at `ModularBody.hpp:193-196` and have **zero consumers** tree-wide (whole-`src` grep: declarations only); `ModularBody::draw` gates on the raw literals `0.0015 / 0.008 / 0.2` (`ModularBody.hpp:510-540`).] At `viewportRadius = 1024` the literals reproduce the named values almost exactly (0.0015 → 3.1 px, 0.008 → 16.4 px diameter), so the literals ARE those constants frozen at a 2048-wide render — but `viewportRadius = screenWidth/2` (`ModularBody.cpp:639`), so what ships is a resolution-INDEPENDENT fraction of the half-FOV: at 8k the "16 px" gate is 65 px. Two authorities for one threshold family (I2), one of them dead, and a §2.0 D5 (1k–8k) question underneath — G4's own evidence line cites the named constants as if they were the gates. — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F9 while locating the regime boundary for B12; record-don't-fix — touching any of those literals moves every body's regime in every scene, which needs its own dispatch and its own before/after scene battery. Decide it as one question: are the gates px-intent (then derive the fractions from `viewportRadius` and delete the literals) or fraction-intent (then delete the px constants and rewrite G4's evidence line)?) — **CLOSED 2026-07-31 by F18, §11.127(b): PX-INTENT, derived not chosen.** Four independent lines agree and none supports fraction-intent: G4's own wording (*what the observer can distinguish* is pixels); `b12-design.md`'s *smallest added structure ≥ 1 px*; every other member of this family already converts (`SYSTEM_VISIBILITY_SUBSYSTEM_SIZE` at `ModularSystem.cpp:748-752`, the orbit gate at `Renderer.cpp:50-52` commented *absolute*, `AtmExtModule.cpp:116`, `RingModule.cpp:150`, and `drawHalo` — whose comment records that the unconverted form WAS a bug — plus the old path's own `screen_sz < 180` / 10 px); and D5, under which fraction-intent makes the 16 px gate 65 px at 8k, i.e. more resolution drawing less. The census was wider than this row recorded: **0.2 had six spellings, 0.0015 three**, half of them `double` compared against `float`. The px constants are now the authority and the screenSize forms are derived by `ModularBody::setViewportRadius`, the viewport's ONLY writer (I3; `viewportRadius` is no longer public data). The notable/depth-bucket gate was FORCED to join the family: `clearDepth`'s stated precondition that it sits below full-visibility **inverts above ~4k** as fractions. Two constants where there was one literal at 0.2 (close-range vs big-texture), because §5.53(b) has an open question about the texture gate alone. At 2048 the derived gates are **bit-identical** to the old float literals (power-of-two scaling; verified arithmetically and in the app's own reported gates), so the only screenSize whose branch can change is one exactly equal to a gate. Measured at a SECOND width against a prediction fixed before the run: delivered **0.049 / 0.000** (Mars residual attributed to satellite halos by Venus reading 0.000), pre-change binary **0.729 / 0.644** in the same scene = the surface drawing where px-intent says it must not. **VETO POINT for Vixy**: the named EARLY constant said 2 px, the shipped gate is 3.07 px; shipped behaviour was preserved (D8 as-if) and the value is not mine — reversing it is a one-token edit. Also for that decision: `RAYMARCH_MIN_SCREEN_SIZE` (0.025 ≈ 51 px) is the one screenSize threshold still spelled as a fraction, deliberately (single-site, a capability gate rather than a G4 regime boundary), and is named in-source at its declaration.

55. **Drawing the rotation axis ONCE kills the process at exit: a file-static holds a buffer sub-allocation and returns it to a `BufferMgr` that was destroyed with the App.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.124(e); **deterministic, one flag, both ways**: identical drive but for one command — `flag planets_axis on` ⇒ `terminate called after throwing an instance of 'std::bad_alloc'`, **rc=134 (core dumped)**; `flag planets_axis off` ⇒ **rc=0**. Reproduced 2/2 on the pre-§5.51 binary (`a958c05e`) and 2/2 on the post-fix one (`0dd0a938`), so it is INDEPENDENT of §5.51 — `harness/artifacts/f16_axis_axis`, `f16_axis_noaxis`, `f16_axis_prefix`, `f16_noaxis_prefix`.] **Backtrace, app launched under gdb** (`f16_validation_gdb/app.log:2841` + POST-RUN STACK): `#0 BufferMgr::releaseBuffer` (`BufferMgr.cpp:113`) ← `#1 SharedBuffer<Vec3f>::~SharedBuffer` (`SharedBuffer.hpp:17`) ← `#4 ~unique_ptr` on `(anonymous namespace)::axisFamily()::data+16` ← `#5 AxisFamilyData::~AxisFamilyData` (`AxisModule.cpp:26`) ← **`#6 __run_exit_handlers`** — i.e. STATIC destruction, after `main` returned. **The same site presents as two different fires depending on what the freed block happens to hold**: natively `std::bad_alloc` out of a destructor ⇒ `terminate` ⇒ **SIGABRT**; under gdb *"Thread 1 received signal SIGSEGV, Segmentation fault"* at that same frame #0. So this is a **member of the §11.15d/B7 shutdown-crash class that reproduces on one command** — the first one that does. **Mechanism at source**: `AxisFamilyData` is a function-local static (`AxisModule.cpp:26-31`, constructed on the axis family's first use) holding `std::unique_ptr<SharedBuffer<Vec3f>> uColor`; `~SharedBuffer` calls `mgr.releaseBuffer(buffer)` on the `BufferMgr &` captured at construction (`SharedBuffer.hpp:15-18`), and that manager is a `Context` member destroyed inside `app.reset()` (`main.cpp:367`) — so the release walks the free-list of a destroyed object (`BufferMgr.cpp:101-131`) and the garbage it reads throws out of a destructor, which is `terminate`. **Why 266 hunted teardowns never saw it**: the static only exists once an `AxisModule` has actually drawn, and neither the §11.95 mix nor the shipped default scene ever turns `flag planets_axis` on — this is a shipped, one-command user action, not a corner. **Same-class survey** [observed]: the three sibling family statics (`OrbitFamilyData`, `TrailFamilyData`, `GridFamilyData`) hold only a `unique_ptr<VertexArray>` (trivial destructor, no manager reference) and a `PipelineFamily` handle whose `release()` is null-guarded on the already-reset registry (`PipelineRegistry.cpp:627-631`), so `uColor` is the only manager-touching member among them **today**. The invariant broken here is one the code already states for every Renderer-owned resource — *"released by releaseRegistry() while the buffer managers are alive"* (`Renderer.hpp:383,391`; `PipelineRegistry.cpp:1399-1440`, and the same reasoning in `Registry::~Registry`, `:212-220`). **ANNOTATION 2026-07-31 [measured, §11.125(b)]**: the *"SIGABRT natively / SIGSEGV under gdb"* reading is a coincidence of four runs, not a rule — driven identically through the hunt's own classifier on `ebb41ab2`, the NATIVE face was **SIGSEGV, rc=139** (`f17_pc_native`, cycle 3, no `terminate called` line at all). Which face appears depends on what the freed block happens to hold, exactly as this row's own mechanism says; both faces are FIRE to the classifier, and the ASan tree shows a third face — `heap-buffer-overflow` READ at `BufferMgr::releaseBuffer`, nearest freed region attributed to `~VulkanMgr` (`main.cpp:376`) (`f17_pc_asan/ASAN_POSCONTROL.txt`). The site, the mechanism and the determinism are unchanged. **Second site of the same class found 2026-07-31: §5.57** (`s_texture::forceUnload` draining the texture ring into destroyed `BufferMgr`s) — fix the class, not the instance. — **FIXED 2026-07-31 (§11.126(b)(c), code `83324455`)**: the fix is the CLASS, not this site. `Context` now takes teardown hooks and runs them at the START of `~Context`, where every manager and the pipeline registry are alive (contract in `context.hpp` `onManagerTeardown`; I3 — the owner of the managers notifies its dependents, I5 — that notification is what makes a captured manager reference legal); `AxisModule.cpp` registers one hook that hands `uColor` back. **The owed audit was run and is a deliverable (§11.126(a))**: of the ten new-path family-data file-statics, this is the ONLY one that captures a manager — the other nine hold a `VertexArray` whose `= default` destructor releases nothing (`VertexArray.hpp:27,54`) and a `PipelineFamily` handle whose `release()` is GUARDED on the reset registry (`PipelineRegistry.cpp:626-630` against `:226`), i.e. safe by an explicit guard rather than by luck. The audit also found the OLD path already carries exactly this fix (`Axis::destroySC_context()` ← `Body::deleteShader` ← `~SolarSystem`, `axis.cpp:139`/`body.cpp:315`/`solarsystem.cpp:67`), so this was a **generality dropped in the port**, not a new class. **Both ways, both entries, same classifier, same session** (`f19_axis_prefix` / `f19_axis_delivered`, the `pcaxis`/`pcaxissig` pair): pre-fix **4/4 FIRE** (rc=134 ×3, rc=139 ×1 — the garbage-dependent face this row's own annotation predicts), delivered **4/4 CLEAN rc=0**, with the injected `pcsegv`/`pcabrt` controls still firing so the detector is live, and `axisshot_*.png` written so the family's resources really were built. Consequence for the hunt: the §11.124(k) coverage hole can close — `planets_axis` is now safe in a hunted mix, and the F16 validation drive no longer needs `AXIS=off`.)

56. **`ModuleLoader` and `OrbitLoader` carry §5.51's exact shape without (yet) its consequence.** [observed 2026-07-31, §11.124(f)] `class ModuleLoader` (`ModuleLoader.hpp:14`) and `class OrbitLoader` (`OrbitLoader.hpp:8`) declare pure-virtual members and **no destructor**, and both are owned polymorphically by `ModuleLoaderMgr` (`std::vector<std::unique_ptr<ModuleLoader>> loaders[…]`, `std::map<std::string, std::unique_ptr<OrbitLoader>>`, `std::unique_ptr<OrbitLoader> defaultOrbitLoader` — `ModuleLoaderMgr.hpp:44-46`), deleted through the base ⇒ the same [expr.delete]/3 UB §5.51 fixed. Nothing is lost today because all 23 registered loaders (`modules.cpp:72-97`) are **stateless**, so `sizeof(subclass) == sizeof(base)` and there is nothing for a destructor to do — which is also why the instrument that found §5.51 is blind here: ASan's `new-delete-type-mismatch` fires on a SIZE difference, and the same cycles that reported 373 from `ModularBody::components` reported **0** from this path. The compile-time channel is blind too [measured, with positive control 2026-07-31]: `-Wdelete-non-virtual-dtor` fires on a direct `delete p` but **not** through `std::unique_ptr`, and the build passes no `-Wall` at all (`build-claude/…/flags.make`: `-O2 -g -DNDEBUG -std=gnu++23`). — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F16 while checking whether §5.51 was an instance or a class; NOT fixed with it, and the asymmetry is the reason: these objects die at STATIC teardown, i.e. **after `vulkan.reset()`**, so making their destructors run for the first time is exactly the regime §5.55 fires in — free only for as long as every loader stays stateless, and nothing states that invariant. Fix it together with a place that says loaders hold no resources, or with the ownership move that makes them die before the device does. **ANNOTATION 2026-07-31 [observed, §11.126(f)]: re-derived under F19's reordering rather than inherited, and UNCHANGED.** F19 moved the deferred texture drain and the file-static release into `~Context`; none of that touches these objects, because `ModuleLoaderMgr::instance` is a class-static object (`ModuleLoaderMgr.cpp:22`) whose members still die at `__run_exit_handlers`, after `vulkan.reset()`. The 23 registered loaders are still stateless (`modules.cpp:72-97`), so the base-pointer delete still has nothing to fail to destroy. This row is now the ONLY member of §11.126(a)'s file-static audit that the `Context::onManagerTeardown` mechanism does not cover — its objects are not reachable from `Context` at all, which is the ownership question this row already names.)

57. **`body action reload` then quit = SIGSEGV: the deferred texture ring is drained AFTER the buffer managers are destroyed.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.125(g)(h); **reproducible on demand**, which no member of this class except §5.55 has ever been: composed scene of 8 grounded OJM rovers, `body action reload`, teardown signal 0.05 s later — delivered binary `ebb41ab2` **25 FIRE / 26 cycles under load** (13 of 14 in the N=54 arm, 6/6 plain, 6/6 gdb) and **2/6 on a QUIET host**; artifacts `f17_hung_P`, `f17_bisect_P`, `f17_hung_P_gdb`, `f17_scope_quiet`.] **Backtrace** (`f17_hung_P_gdb/gdb_001_qda.log`): `#0 BufferMgr::releaseBuffer` (`EntityCore/Core/BufferMgr.cpp:113`) ← `#1 Texture::detach` (`Texture.cpp:299`) ← `#3 ~Texture` (`:229`) ← `#11 vector<unique_ptr<Texture>>::clear` on `s_texture::releaseTexture` ← `#13 s_texture::forceUnload` (`tools/s_texture.cpp:570`) ← `#14 main` (`main.cpp:375`). **Mechanism at source**: `main` calls `app.reset()` at `main.cpp:371`, destroying `Context` and every `BufferMgr` in it, and only THEN drains the 3-deep deferred texture ring at `main.cpp:375`; `~Texture` calls `detach()`, which is `mgr->releaseBuffer(staging)` on a `BufferMgr&` captured at construction — a release into a destroyed manager. **This is §5.55's shape at a second site**, which is what makes it a class and not an instance. **Attributed SINGLE-VARIABLE to §5.51's fix** [measured]: the delivered source with only `BodyModule.hpp` reverted to `a958c05e` (the 14 lines `0dd0a938` added) is **6/6 CLEAN** where the delivered binary is 6/6 FIRE, same mix file, same load, same session (p = 0.0011); the §11.122 pre-fix reference is **0 FIRE / 54**, its own 14 reload-race cycles all clean (p = 3.7e-07 on that subset). §5.51 did not create the ordering error — it made it REACHABLE: before it, no module destructor ran, so nothing was ever pushed into the ring (`~texRecap`, §11.124(d)) and the ring was empty at `forceUnload`. **Why F16's fallout battery missed it**: that battery ran the shipped scene (K=0), and K=0 under the same load is **6/6 CLEAN** — the trigger needs bodies whose modules own OJM/texture resources. — **FIXED 2026-07-31 (§11.126(b)(e), code `83324455`)**: `s_texture::forceUnload()` moved out of `main()` into `~Context`, as the **terminal** drain — placed AFTER `renderer.releaseRegistry()` because that call itself feeds the ring (it drops the pointer and sun-halo textures) — and the `main.cpp:375` call site was DELETED rather than kept as a safety net (two authorities for one drain is the next silent desync, I2). **The owed CLASS audit was run (§11.126(a)B)** and found seven deferred-release containers, of which **five were not in this row's record**: `texCache`'s still-live entries (`forceUnload` sets `tex->texture = nullptr` on 0–2 textures per shutdown), `bigTextures`, and — never drained at ALL, dying at `__run_exit_handlers` after even `vulkan.reset()` — `droppedBigTextures` (`s_texture.cpp:60`, which really can hold a `Texture` via the splice at `:794`), `droppedTextureQueue` and `textureQueue`. All are now drained in the alive-manager window. `Context::transientBuffer[3]`/`transientAsyncBuffer[3]` were checked and cleared: a leak into a manager that dies immediately after, no use-after-free. **RED→GREEN on this row's own reproducer** [measured, `f19_repro_R` vs `f19_repro_T`, interleaved, load pre-warmed, 30 cycles per arm, the pre-fix binary rebuilt BIT-IDENTICAL to §11.125(m)'s stated md5]: pre-fix **27 FIRE / 3 HUNG / 0 CLEAN**, delivered **0 FIRE / 1 HUNG / 29 CLEAN**, p ≈ 1e-14. ASan over the same regime (K=8 reload cycles, which F17's N=54 arm never ran): **0 reports of any class**, leak baseline improved to 17626 B / 194.)

58. **The drawing thread records commands from resources a mid-session reload has already released.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.125(h), Face B — 4 of the 6 gdb-arm fires] `#0-#3` inside `libVkLayer_khronos_validation.so` ← `#4 VertexArray::bindGlobal` (`EntityCore/Resource/VertexArray.hpp:39`) ← `#5 Ojm::drawShadow` (`ojmModule/ojm.cpp:185`) ← `#6 ShadowService::record` (`experimentalModule/ShadowService.cpp:412`) ← `#8 DrawHelper::submit` (`tools/draw_helper.cpp:483`) ← `#9 DrawHelper::mainloop` (`:100`). The main thread destroys bodies and modules on `body action reload` — between frames, with the device live and no `waitIdle` — while `DrawHelper::mainloop` is still recording shadow geometry from an `Ojm` whose vertex buffers that release returned. Same trigger and same session as §5.57, and the same single-variable attribution (the two faces appear in one 6-cycle gdb arm on the delivered binary and neither appears on the §5.51-reverted build); recorded separately because the FIX is different in kind: §5.57 is an ordering error at shutdown, this is a missing synchronisation between the release path and frames in flight. The regime was named in advance by §11.124(a) (*"ALSO mid-session on `body action reload`, where the release happens between frames rather than after a `waitIdle`"*) as the thing that bounds what a module destructor may do; nothing enforces that bound. Note the layer is only the messenger — it dereferences the destroyed handle first; with `debug_layer=false` the same use-after-free would land elsewhere. — **FIXED 2026-07-31 (§11.126(d)(e), code `83324455` + `071b817a`)**, by the row's own fix shape: `Context::quiesceFrames()` states the precondition once — the worker has recorded everything queued (`DrawHelper::waitAllFrames`) and the device is idle — and the destruction entry points call it: `ModularSystem::reloadSystem()` before `clearChildren()`, and `ModularBody::remove()`, which is the funnel every commanded removal passes through (`ssystem_factory.hpp:729`, `CameraAnchors.cpp:184,274,278`, `ModularBody.cpp:100`). Placed at the entry points rather than at each caller, because that is the class. **A THIRD site of the same race was found by the verification campaign and fixed with it** (`071b817a`): leaving the main loop ALWAYS left one frame in flight — `waitFrame` only ever waited for the frame BEFORE the current one — so `~App` destroyed bodies, modules, media and fonts while the worker was still recording commands referencing them; `~App` now stops the worker first (idempotent, `~Context` stops it again). Same measurement as §5.57 (the two faces share one reproducer): **27 FIRE / 30 → 0 FIRE / 30**, and the delivered binary logs `Loading body Rover…` 16 times per cycle (load + reload) where the pre-fix one logs 8, i.e. it COMPLETES the reload the pre-fix one dies inside. NOTE for anyone touching `waitAllFrames`: it waits for REAL completion on purpose — a frame merely given up on does not satisfy it (§11.126(g)).)

59. **A teardown request cannot be serviced while the main loop sits in an UNTIMED frame wait — the app simply does not quit.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.125(i); 4/108 cycles across both binaries at loadavg 16.4–19.8, plus §11.122(i)'s 2] `App::draw` begins with `context.helper->waitFrame(...)` (`appModule/app.cpp:795`), and `DrawHelper::waitFrame` (`tools/draw_helper.cpp:405-418`) ends in `drawer[i].hasCompleted.wait(0, std::memory_order_acquire)` — a `std::atomic` wait with **no timeout and no cancellation**. Teardown (SIGINT/SIGTERM/`shutdown action now`) is serviced by that same main loop (`main.cpp:340-343` → `NSSigTERM` → `App::flag(ALIVE,false)`), so for as long as the drawing thread has not marked the frame complete the process cannot begin to exit. **Positively identified, not inferred**: every one of this campaign's 4 hangs carries the app's own §11.5 watchdog stack — `__platform_wait` (`atomic_wait.h:114`) ← `App::draw` (`app.cpp:795`) ← `App::startMainLoop` (`:995`) ← `main` (`main.cpp:348`) — and the log ends there; the same stack appears when the same path is starved a completely different way (the app on a software Vulkan ICD, `f17_tsan_smoke/lvp_native.log`). This is what every "HUNG (no exit in 45 s)" in the B7 record has been, on BOTH binaries: a frame-pacing stall upstream of teardown, NOT a teardown fault — which is also what §11.74 concluded about B17's mis-filed draw-stall. It **supersedes §11.122(i)'s unattributed residual** (the binary theory it named is refuted: 3/54 post-fix vs 1/54 pre-fix, p = 0.31, and the pre-fix binary — 0 HUNG in 122 before — hung here). — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F17. **The fix was BUILT, MEASURED and WITHDRAWN by F19, 2026-07-31, §11.126(g)** — the most useful thing this row now has is the negative and the reproducer.) **A DETERMINISTIC REPRODUCER EXISTS**: `harness/f19_stall.sh` + one scratch binary (delivered source + one injection, never committed) parks the worker inside `submit()` with the frame's `hasCompleted` never set; three arms differing by a runtime variable, each traversed twice — pre-fix **HUNG 2/2** (no exit in 45 s), cancellation-without-the-shutdown-site-worker-stop **rc=139 2/2 @~2.0 s**, cancellation-with-it **rc=0 2/2 @~1.0 s**. So the mechanism attributed here is confirmed from the other side: ending that wait DOES let the teardown proceed. **WHY IT IS NOT SHIPPED** [measured, `f19_repro_P`/`Q`/`S`, 3 fixed arms, 60 loaded cycles]: the correlation is exact — **every** cycle whose log carries the app's own `This frame stall is very long`, i.e. every cycle that entered the cancellation path, **died with SIGSEGV (3 of 3)**, while all 57 cycles that never entered it were clean; two successive attributed fixes (the shutdown-site worker stop, then making the mid-session quiesce refuse a cancellation as completion) each removed a mechanism and left the correlation intact. The bar this fix had was I7 as-if — identical on every channel except the previously-hung teardown — and turning that teardown into a crash is not it. The honest reading is this batch's own shape: **the cancellation makes a pre-existing teardown-after-stall fault REACHABLE** (the untouched binary hangs those cycles, so it never reaches teardown), exactly as §5.51 made §5.57 reachable. **THE FORK, SUSPENDED FOR VIXY (§11.126(l))**: when the worker has not completed the frame the main loop waits for and the user asks to quit, should the app (i) keep waiting — today, the process does not exit at all — or (ii) stop waiting, which measurably reaches a teardown fault that waiting was hiding? Both are user-visible; it is a product-risk judgement, not an implementation choice. Rate on the delivered binary, unchanged as intended: **1 HUNG / 30** under load (3/30 pre-fix, p = 0.30). See also **§5.61**, a lost wakeup in the queue this wait rides, which is a candidate mechanism for the stall itself.)

60. **The app requests a single 2.68 GB device allocation and does not survive where the limit is enforced.** [measured 2026-07-31, §11.125(e), on the software Vulkan ICD (llvmpipe), NATIVE binary] The validation layer names it: `vkAllocateMemory(): pAllocateInfo->allocationSize (2684360960) is larger than maxMemoryAllocationSize (2147483648)`; the app carries on regardless, then dies **rc=139**, its draw loop having stalled in the §5.59 wait. The causal chain from the oversized allocation to the SEGV is **NOT established** here — it was found while trying to run TSan without the proprietary driver, and both facts are reported as measured rather than joined by argument. Why it matters anyway: this is the **§2.0 D13 downgrade line** — `maxMemoryAllocationSize` is a device limit a conformant implementation may set at 2 GiB (the Vulkan spec's own advice is not to exceed it), and on this host the NVIDIA driver's limit simply hides the request. — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F17, out of that task's scope, record-don't-fix. Owed before it can be judged: WHICH allocation this is and whether it is one buffer or one pool — the answer decides whether the fix is a policy on pool sizing or a fallback path, and D13 says a downgrade must stay possible.) — **EXTENDED 2026-08-09 (F30, §11.140(a)): the owed datum is IN. It is ONE BUFFER, and it is not anything the memory manager sizes.** Identified at its callsite `[measured: harness/f30_alloc.{sh,gdb}, artifacts/f30/lvp1/gdb.log]` by a breakpoint at the ENTRY of `vkAllocateMemory` reading `allocationSize` out of the caller's `VkMemoryAllocateInfo` — 434 allocations recorded on the software ICD, 2 above 1 GiB, **exactly one at 2 684 360 960**: the **`"Staging video buffer"`** of `VideoPlayer::createTextures` `[observed: video_player.cpp:147]` ← `Media::createSC_context` `[media.cpp:302]` ← `App::firstInit` `[main.cpp:346]`. **Not a pool**, on three independent grounds: the pool's `chunkSize` is **256 MiB** `[observed: main.cpp:319 .chunkSize=256 → VulkanMgr.cpp:81]` and `VulkanMgrCreateInfo::allowOverrides` is set at `main.cpp:322` but **read nowhere in `src/`**, so nothing overrides it; the frame carries `registerChunk=false`, the branch reserved for a RESOURCE's own `memRequirements.size`, which is never entered in `memoryChunks`, has `size = (VkDeviceSize)-1` and is freed by direct `vkFreeMemory` `[observed: MemoryManager.cpp:140,157,75-76,245-257]`; and the size is exact arithmetic from the video player's own two constants — `computeTextureResolutionLimit()` = `32*MIBI + MAX_CACHED_FRAMES` on a host with ≥15 GiB `[video_player.cpp:38-45]` × `MAX_CACHED_FRAMES` = 80 `[video_player.hpp:52]` = **(33 554 432 + 80) × 80 = 2 684 360 960**, this row's number **to the byte** (the 6 400 B over 2 560 MiB is the helper adding a frame COUNT to a per-frame BYTE size, then the caller multiplying by the frame count again). **Regime control on the proprietary driver:** the same buffer, the same size, the same callsite, **0** `maxMemoryAllocationSize` warnings, only the route inside the manager differing (`dmalloc`, since dedicated is preferred there) `[measured: artifacts/f30/native1/gdb.log]` — so the request is **unconditional and ICD-independent** (it is computed from HOST RAM and a compile-time frame count) and only the ENFORCEMENT differs. **The fix domain, which is what D13 needs: NOT a policy on pool sizing.** It is (i) the video player's own single-buffer sizing policy — a host-RAM-tiered per-frame cap × a fixed 80-frame cache, taken **unconditionally at startup** whether or not any video is ever played — and/or (ii) a fallback path, which does not exist at either end: `maxMemoryAllocationSize` is queried **nowhere in `src/`** (0 occurrences), and `BufferMgr`'s failure branch logs *"Failed to create buffer bloc"* and RETURNS `[observed: BufferMgr.cpp:8-11]`, leaving a manager with no buffer, no free space and no mapping, on which `createTextures` then builds four `Texture`s — nothing thrown, nothing signalled. Runner-up allocation on both ICDs: **1 610 612 736** = `3*512 MiB`, the `"Texture staging BufferMgr"` of `app.cpp:480`, at 75 % of the limit. **Correction to this row's implied chain:** on the measured ICD the oversized allocation **SUCCEEDED** (the layer only warns; the app logs its own *"Allocate chunk of 2560 MiB"* success line), so it is not what fails — the rc=139 remains unexplained and unclaimed.

61. **A lost wakeup in the work queue the frame pacing rides: the consumer can sleep with work pending, and nothing wakes it again.** [observed 2026-07-31, §11.126(h), at source; NOT measured against any particular hang, and stated as a candidate mechanism rather than an attribution] `WorkQueue::pop` (`EntityCore/Tools/SafeQueue.hpp:174-187`) tests `count` and then calls `cv.wait(lock)`; producers (`:143-162`) never take the mutex and notify only on the **0→1** transition (`if (!count++) cv.notify_one()`). A push landing between the consumer's test and its wait is therefore a lost wakeup — and every subsequent push is silent, because `count != 0` from then on. The consumer then sleeps with a non-empty queue until something calls `flush()` or `close()`. Why it matters here: `DrawHelper::waitFrame` flushes **once** before its untimed atomic wait (`draw_helper.cpp:409`), so a lost wakeup in that window parks the worker AND the main thread for good — which is exactly the state §5.59 describes, and would make §5.59's "alive and correct, just late" reading wrong for at least some of its cases. The queue's own `waitIdle()` (`SafeQueue.hpp:232-241`) re-notifies every 100 µs until the queue drains and therefore heals the class, which is why `DrawHelper::waitAllFrames` uses it rather than a single flush. — **OPEN** (found 2026-07-31 by F19 while reading the §5.59 path; record-don't-fix: `EntityCore` is a submodule, read-only for that task, and the fix is a change to the queue's synchronisation contract — the honest shapes are to notify under the mutex, or to have the consumer re-test under it. Owed before it is judged: a measurement that attributes at least one observed stall to this window, which nothing yet does.)
