It blocks b31-design §6.2's T1 and the next B31 slice inherits it.)** **[EXTENDED 2026-08-01 by F21's bounded attribution attempt (§11.129(b); budget stated and spent: 3 measurement waves / 9 launches, in-scene A/A floor 0 at every stage of every wave, frozen md5 in == out on all nine). NOT attributed; what the attempt EXCLUDED, each with its evidence:** **(1) the row's own characterisation is CORRECTED — the residual is NOT photometry-without-displacement**: of the saved frame's 400 lit>32 pixels, **0 are lit in the restored frame**, and the restored frame's 907 lit>32 pixels read ≤ 8 (mean 1.9) in the saved one; median nearest-neighbour distance saved-bright → restored-bright is **50.2 px ≈ 1.1°** at fov 45 on a 2048 dome. The lit sets are positionally DISJOINT, which a mean/bbox statistic cannot see. **(2) content**: the whole residual is old-path sky — stars 2418 px, milky way 744 px, nebulae 2493 px, and with those three off the two frames are **identical (0 px>8)**, so landscape, atmosphere, planets and the dome itself are exact. **(3) transience EXCLUDED**: the restored scene, untouched, gives the identical number at t0, t+15 s and t+40 s ⇒ not an adaptation/settling term. **(4) rotation about the dome centre EXCLUDED**: best-fit rotation over ±180° (2° then 0.25° steps) reaches overlap 0.007. **The milky way is in the SAME place in both** while the star/DSO field is not — so it is not a view-frame rotation of the old path as a whole. **(5) re-asserted to the same value on BOTH sides, none of which moved the difference**: `flag atmosphere off` (7834→7834), `set light_pollution_limiting_magnitude 6.5` (both sides brightened, 401→749 / 1013→1703 lit, difference did NOT converge — so the `ToneReproductor` world-adaptation term, whose only inputs are the atmosphere fader and the pollution luminance at `atmosphere.cpp:167`, is excluded both ways), `zoom fov 45 duration 0`, `moveto lat 12 lon 34 alt 500000 duration 0`, `set star_scale 1`, `set star_mag_scale 1`, `flag lock_sky_position off` then `on`, `set heading 0`. **(6) `view_offset` EXCLUDED BY CONSTRUCTION, and it is a harness defect**: `set view_offset 0.25` — the B10 line of f20_session.py's scene A — is **not a registered `set` name**; the app rejects it (*"view_offset is unknown. Did you mean zoom_offset ?"*, `f21s_saved.applog:2159`), so scene A never exercised §2 row B10 at all and both sides run at offset 0. **(7) the magnitude is NOT stable across restores**: 3185 px (§11.128(h)), then 5655 / 7834 / 7830 on the same binary, same scene, same commands — while the SAVED side's lit count is stable (400/401/401) and the floor is 0 every time. The divergent term is on the RESTORED side and varies from restore to restore. **Sharpened hypothesis, unverified**: what `hip_stars` and `nebulas` consume BEYOND the navigator (the equatorial-frame transform / geodesic grid / the refraction pre-pass) diverges on a restore, while the milky way — same navigator, same frame — agrees. **Owed before it is judged**: a readback of the old path's own view state (`Navigator::getLocalVision` / `getPrecEquVision` / `mat_local_to_earth_equ`) on both sides — which does not exist today and is the same READ-HALF gap this row's own slice is about. Harness: `harness/f21_s563.py` (content ladder + transience), `f21_s563b.py` (mechanism ladder), `f21_s563c.py` (orientation ladder); artifacts `harness/artifacts/f21s563{,b,c}`.]** **[CLOSED 2026-08-01 by F22 (§11.130), attributed and fixed, both ways.** The owed readback was built (`oldView` on the dual dump: navigator + observer + projector + star pipeline, all `const`) and answered in one run: **the restored session reproduced every field of `Camera::dumpTrace` to the digit and drew its sky from a direction 107.634° away**, against an in-scene A/A floor of **1e-6°**, with **392 stars drawn against 689**, while the observer's place agreed to 1.4e-6° and `mat_local_to_earth_equ` to 1.9e-8. **TWO facts.** (1) `Navigator::local_vision` is STATE and the file did not carry it: §2 row B19's clause *"setters are dual so it follows"* is measurably false here — nothing ties that vector to the camera, and there is no seam to route it through (driving the shipped `look_at` dual seam with the camera's own alt/az lands **94.4°** from where the old navigator ends up; recorded, not kept → §5.66). (2) A sky lock froze a STALE pair: the whole restore runs inside ONE command with no frame between its steps, so the lock latches the transforms and the equatorial vision vector the PREVIOUS frame computed — on the launch body, at the launch place, **at the launch date, which is the system clock** — which is precisely why exclusion (7)'s magnitude moved on every restore. **Fix**: `[observer] sky_vision` + `Core::restoreSkyVision` (recompute the transforms from the restored place and date, then put the direction back — the same two calls `Core::init` makes after it moves the observer). **Measured both ways on one ladder**: pre-fix 3294 px>8 / floor 48 / 107.634° / 392-vs-689; delivered **38 px>8 / floor 44 / 0.0° / 392-vs-392**; and on an AIMED direction (a scene that issued `look_at` before saving) **0.00e+00° against a fresh-launch control 101.999° away**, which no stale-pair-refresh alone could pass. **THREE of this row's own statements are corrected in §11.130(h)**: the milky way was NOT *"in the same place in both"* (a smooth full-dome layer is not a witness for displacement — 107° of view change crosses the >8 threshold on only 744 of its pixels while point sources go disjoint), the rotation fit excluded a ROLL and not a change of direction, and the in-scene A/A floor of a sky-LOCKED scene is **not 0** (26–51 px>8 measured across six runs; the lock captures at whatever frame the command lands on). **b31-design §6.2 T1 is MET** (f20_session 39 px>8 against an in-scene floor of 51) and the f21_s563 ladder on the delivered binary reads 29 px against a floor of 26 at EVERY stage, the content signature gone. Harness: `harness/f22_s563_view.py`, `f22_b10_offset.py`; artifacts `harness/artifacts/f22view{,_fix}`, `f22b10`.]**

64. **`timerate action pause` does not stop the simulation clock.** `TimeMgr::update` advances `JDay` by the RAW `time_speed` unconditionally (`JDay += time_speed*(double)delta_time/1000.`, `time_mgr.cpp:42`), while `FlagTimePause`/`timeLockCount` gate only the `getTimeSpeed()` READOUT (`time_mgr.hpp:59`, `(timeLockCount) ? 0 : time_speed`). Measured as a **4.5e-5 d (3.9 s of simulated time) drift over ~1.3 s of wall clock at rate 3**, in a scene whose pause had been set. Found because a session records `paused` and a restored pause did not hold the date; the session records what the operator SET and is correct either way (§11.128(d)). — **OPEN (found 2026-08-01, §11.128(j); record-don't-fix: making the pause hold the clock changes what a shipped script's `timerate action pause` does mid-show, which is a user-visible semantics decision, and §2(b) makes the authored corpus immutable. Owed before it is judged: which consumers read `getTimeSpeed()` and therefore already behave as if paused, since the readout and the clock disagreeing is the actual defect shape.)** — **EXTENDED 2026-08-09 (F30, §11.140(b)): the enumeration is IN and it is CLOSED — the answer is ALL of them.** Whole-`src` sweep `[observed: @ bd3f7117]`, every hit of `getTimeSpeed`/`getTimeSpeedRaw`/`timeGetSpeed`/`time_speed` classified: the gated readout has **two** direct callers, `CoreLink::timeGetSpeed` `[coreLink.cpp:502]` — a pure forwarder whose only consumers are `commandTimerate`'s **four** branches `[app_command_interface.cpp:3327 increment / :3345 sincrement / :3360 decrement / :3376 sdecrement]` — and `MeteorMgr::update` `[meteor_mgr.cpp:90]`, where `tspeed = 0` fails the `tspeed <= 0` test so no new meteors spawn. **6 of 6 gated call sites behave as if paused.** The only two raw readers are deliberate and carry their reason at the site: the `SessionFile` host adapter `[coreLink.cpp:1295 → SessionFile.cpp:179,529]` and `dual_dump`'s header `[ssystem_factory.cpp:983]`. **Nothing else in the tree reads `time_speed` — except `TimeMgr::update` `[time_mgr.cpp:42]`, which reads the member directly, neither accessor.** So the readout/clock disagreement is **TOTAL, not partial**: exactly one line is on the wrong side of the gate, and **no gated consumer's behaviour would change if the pause were made to hold the clock** — which is the datum this decision was starved of. **Measured on top of the enumeration, 5 predictions written into the instrument before the run and 5 hit** `[measured: harness/f30_timerate.py, artifacts/f30/f30_timerate.json]`: the four `timerate` sites are worse than behaves-as-paused — each reads the GATED value as if it were *the rate the operator set*, drops the pause and recomputes from it, so a held pause makes the operator's rate not slower but **lost**. From `timerate rate 3`: with a pause held, `timerate action decrement` → rate **−1.000000** (the zero branch `s > -JD_SECOND && s <= 0.` landing on `-JD_SECOND`, `[:3366-3369]`) i.e. **time runs backward at real time**, JDay falling 2461261.522283792 → 2461261.522272217; the control, same command and same set rate with no pause held, → **+1.500000**. This row's own headline re-measured on that drive: **+0.000041703 d = +3.603 simulated seconds elapsed across the HELD pause**. Both halves sit on the live key surface (`ui.cpp:795` pause, **`SDL_SCANCODE_J`** `ui.cpp:1547` decrement, `ui.cpp:922-924` the held-key ramp), and the script engine issues `timerate action pause` for its own pause `[script_mgr.cpp:133]`, while **no shipped script issues any `timerate action` at all** (0 of 408). → minted as its own row **§5.76**, since it survives whichever way this row's pause semantics are decided.

65. **Turning the sky lock on in the same frame as a move latches the view the PREVIOUS frame had.** [derived from the measurement that closed §5.63, §11.130(c); the shipped-command instance is observed at source, not separately measured] `Core::setFlagLockSkyPosition` sets `flag_lock_equ_pos`, after which `Navigator::updateVisionVector` HOLDS `equ_vision` and re-derives `local_vision` from it every frame `[observed: navigator.cpp:128-134]`. `equ_vision` is only written by that per-frame update, and the transform matrices it was built from are only refreshed by the per-frame `updateTransformMatrices` `[observed: solarSystemModule.cpp:114-116]`. So any caller that moves the observer (body, place, or date) and engages the lock **inside one frame-less batch** freezes a pair computed at the OLD place — and the shipped surface has exactly that shape: a script block issuing `moveto …` and then `flag lock_sky_position on` executes as one command batch. Measured in the session restore, which is the same shape: the frozen pair put the sky 107.634° from where it belonged. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-01 by F22 while attributing §5.63; the session path is fixed at its own root — it now refreshes and re-asserts the direction before locking — but the general seam is NOT, because making `setFlagLockSkyPosition` refresh first changes what a shipped `flag lock_sky_position on` does after a move in the same block, which is user-visible semantics. Owed before it is judged: whether any shipped script actually issues the pair in one block, and whether the operator's meaning is *"lock what I am looking at now"* — which is what refreshing would give them.) — **EXTENDED 2026-08-09 (F30, §11.140(c)): NO — 0 in-block pairs in the shipped corpus, and the two scripts that come closest wait a full second on purpose.** Corpus 434 files, spellings verified at the parser not from this row's prose (`ACP_CN_MOVETO` = `"moveto"` `[base_command_interface.hpp:359]`, `ACP_FN_LOCK_SKY_POSITION` = `"lock_sky_position"` `[:468]`) `[measured: harness/f30_corpus.py, artifacts/f30/corpus_report.txt]`: `lock_sky_position` **7 hits in 7 files**, `moveto` **181 hits in 74 files**, files carrying both **7**, **in-block pairs 0**. Controls on the same channel: `^\s*flag` = 1364 hits in 170 files, and the block analyser finds exactly 1 pair in a synthetic file holding one adjacent pair and one `wait`-separated pair `[artifacts/f30/control_5_65.sts]`. **This row's one-frame premise is VERIFIED at source**, not assumed: `ScriptMgr::update` executes in a `while (wait_time == 0)` loop `[observed: script_mgr.cpp:303]`, so a run of zero-wait commands runs inside ONE frame, and only `wait` `[app_command_interface.cpp:1361]`, `script` `[:2797]`, `zoom` with a duration `[:3268]` and `camera` with a time `[:4186]` ever set a non-zero wait — **`moveto` never does**. Only **two** of the seven sites turn the lock ON (`fscripts/07g.sts:30`, `fscripts/07isc.sts:31`); the other five turn it off. Both ON sites are the same shape — `moveto` … `date utc` … `zoom fov 2xx duration 1` … `wait duration 1` … `flag lock_sky_position on` — i.e. **two yielding commands and a full second of frames** between the move and the lock, so the lock engages on a freshly updated `equ_vision`. **Scope of the negative, stated:** it covers SCRIPTS. The session-restore path had exactly this shape and is already fixed at its own root (§11.130); the TCP channel drains a command batch per pass, so a client can still issue the pair in one frame — that route was not measured here and this row did not ask for it. (The comment at `script_mgr.cpp:283`, *"runs maximum of one command per update"*, contradicts the loop three lines below it — recorded, not fixed.)

66. **`look_at`'s two halves do not land in the same direction.** [measured 2026-08-01, §11.130(c): `CoreLink::lookAt` aims the camera (`Camera::lookTo(alt,az)`) and the old navigator (`Navigator::lookAt(az,alt)` → `moveTo`) from ONE pair of angles `[observed: coreLink.hpp:375-382]`; driven with the camera's own current alt/az — i.e. an aim that is a no-op for the camera by construction — the old navigator settled **94.4° away from the vector that same convention predicts** and **102.0° from where the old path had been**, against an in-scene A/A floor of 1e-6°.] The two paths therefore do not share the alt/az convention this seam feeds them, which is the B9 az-convention family (§11.4/§11.60) seen from the WRITE side rather than the readout side. Consequence, and the reason it is recorded here: a restore cannot put the old path's view back by replaying the camera's angles through this seam — §5.63's fix carries the old direction as its own datum instead. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-01 by F22 as a failed intervention; record-don't-fix: the convention question rides §11.92(d)/§11.102(b2), which is Vixy's, and changing what `look_at` does to either path is user-visible. Owed before it is judged: which of the two landings is the one an operator typing `look_at azimuth X altitude Y` means — the drawn bodies' or the drawn sky's.)

67. **After any `look_at` the old path runs with vision vectors of length 2.** [observed 2026-08-01, §11.130(i): `Navigator::moveTo` normalises its aim and then multiplies it by 2 (`navigator.cpp:353-355`), and the end-of-move branch assigns that vector straight into `local_vision`, whence `equ_vision` and `prec_equ_vision` inherit the norm (`:112-117`)] Most consumers are safe because they normalise (`SphereGrid::intersect` does, `tools/SphereGrid.hpp:467-471`, so the nebula and illuminate grids are), and `updateViewMat` normalises what it uses. **`constellation.cpp:180` does not**: `nav->getPrecEquVision().dot(art_vertex[i]) > 0.9` is a fixed-threshold test on a vector whose length silently doubles, i.e. the art-visibility test becomes twice as permissive after any commanded look. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-01 by F22 while reading the aim path for §5.63; record-don't-fix: old-path render behaviour, unchanged by construction (§11.52(b)), and the visible effect is constellation art appearing slightly earlier. Owed before it is judged: a screen A/B with constellation art on, before and after a `look_at`, which nothing has run.)

68. **The place setters are dual but NOT equivalent: old clamps, the camera does not.** [measured 2026-08-01, §11.131(j), in F23's own run] `Observer::setLatitude` forces the latitude into [-90, 90] and maps exactly 0 to **1e-6** `[observed: observer.hpp:102-111]`; `Observer::setAltitude` floors at **0.1 m** `[observed: observer.cpp:61-63]`. `Camera::setLatitude`/`setAltitude` and `Camera::moveTo`'s absolute branch assign what they are given `[observed: Camera.hpp:312-318, Camera.cpp:586-612]`. So the ONE seam that writes both (`CoreLink::observerMoveTo`, and the three `observatorySet*`) lands the two paths on different states for any value either clamp touches: after `moveto lat 0 lon 0 alt 200000` the old observer reads latitude **1.000e-6°** and the camera **0.000000°** — measured, on the delivered binary, in a dump whose whole purpose is to show the two side by side. Consequence: a write-half divergence channel that no readout fix can close, and the sharper version of it — `moveto alt 0.05` puts the old observer at 0.1 m and the camera at 0.05 m — is a REAL divergence of the drawn place from the readable one on the OLD side of the pin. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-01 by F23 while closing B33's read half; record-don't-fix: which clamp is right is a product question — the ±90° one is a coordinate-system fact the camera arguably needs too, the 0.1 m altitude floor and the 0→1e-6 latitude nudge are old-path defensive habits whose removal or adoption changes where a shipped `moveto` lands. Owed before it is judged: whether any shipped script or config hits a clamped value — the shipped `config.ini` does not (43.3°, 75 m).)

69. **`body action preload keep_time` is truncated to 8 bits, so every value above ~1.8 s is wrong and wrong non-monotonically — the command's OWN default included.** [measured 2026-08-01, §11.132(g), in F24's own run] The operator gives SECONDS; the command multiplies by the target fps and hands FRAMES to `s_texture::setBigTextureLifetime(int)` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp, the W_PRELOAD branch]`; `bigTexRecap::lifetime` is an **`unsigned char`** counted down once per frame `[observed: s_texture.hpp:103, s_texture.cpp getBigTexture/acquireBigTexture]`. So `keep_time 3` at the shipped `maximum_fps = 144` asks for 432 frames and the record carries **176** — read directly out of the engine's own table by this wave's new `bigTextures` dump — and the command's own default of 10 s asks for 1440 and carries **160**, i.e. LESS than the 3 s request. Old-path and engine-wide (old's `Body::preload` passes the identical value), and identical on both paths since §11.132 mirrored the seam, so the two paths agree on the wrong number rather than disagreeing. Same argument, second observation: `ProtoSystem::preloadBody` reads the key with `std::stoi(param[W_KEEPTIME])` `[observed: protosystem.cpp:1016]`, which THROWS on an absent key — unreachable through the shipped command (which always writes one) but reachable by any future caller of `Core::preloadSolarSystemBody`. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-01 by F24 while wiring the preload mirror; record-don't-fix: widening the field is an s_texture change with a memory-layout and a release-policy question attached — how long a preloaded texture MAY be pinned is a product answer, not a type answer — and clamping instead would silently change what a shipped `keep_time 10` does. Owed before it is judged: what the intended maximum residency is, and whether any shipped show relies on today's truncated value.) — **EXTENDED 2026-08-09 (F30, §11.140(d)): the scannable half is IN, and the negative is not the whole answer.** Corpus 434 files, spellings at the parser (`W_PRELOAD` = `"preload"` `[base_command_interface.hpp:312]`, `W_KEEPTIME` = `"keep_time"` `[:288]`) `[measured: artifacts/f30/corpus_report.txt]`: **`keep_time` — 0 hits in 0 files**, control on the same channel `^\s*body` = 5520 hits in 43 files, so it is a mapped negative and **no shipped show sets a `keep_time` value it could rely on**. But `preload` has **4 hits in 3 files**, three of them real: `~/.spacecrafter/scripts/internal/clear_mess.sts:31` (`purge all`, not a load), `~/.spacecrafter/scripts/internal/fly_to_selected.sts:13` (`body action preload name selected`) and the documented example `doc/superscript.sts:130-131` (`body action preload name Earth`). **None passes `keep_time`, so all of them ride the command's OWN DEFAULT — which is the worst-truncated value in this row.** `kt = std::to_string(Utility::strToInt(kt, 10) * stapp->getTargetFps())` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:4028-4029]`: the `10` is the default seconds, so at the shipped `maximum_fps = 144` it asks for 1440 frames and the `unsigned char` carries **1440 − 5×256 = 160** — confirming §11.132(g)'s measured 160 by arithmetic, i.e. **1.11 s of residency for a documented 10 s request**, less than a 3 s request's 176. So: nothing relies on a SET value; one shipped internal script and the documented example rely on the DEFAULT, and any change to what the default means reaches them. (The intended-maximum half remains Vixy's and was deliberately not answered.)

70. **The interactive turn ramp RECORDS an action no channel can replay — the command name and both argument keys are unregistered.** [observed 2026-08-02, §11.133(j), found while mapping the ramp's terms] `Core::updateMove` emits `look delta_az <v> delta_alt <v>` through `recordActionCallback` `[observed: core.cpp, the deltaAz||deltaAlt branch]`. There is no `look` command: `m_commands` registers `ACP_CN_LOOK_AT` = **`look_at`** and nothing else `[observed: app_command_init.cpp:66, base_command_interface.hpp:356]`; and the delta keys are **`delta_azimuth`/`delta_altitude`** `[observed: base_command_interface.hpp:154,195]`, which is what `commandLook` reads before calling `Core::panView` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:3192-3197]`. So every recorded show replays an operator's arrow-key pan as an unknown command — an R2 live-show channel loss of the same class as §11.108(k)'s `script speed`, one layer out. Its ZOOM sibling on the adjacent line is correct on both counts (`zoom` is registered, `delta_fov` is a real key) AND lands on both paths (`CoreLink::setFov` is dual), which is what makes this a defect rather than a design. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-02 by F25; record-don't-fix: the one-word fix is `look_at delta_azimuth … delta_altitude …`, but what a recording CONTAINS is show-facing, the recorded value would then execute `Core::panView` — which is §5.71, old-only — and B28 owns command spellings. Owed before it is judged: whether any archived recording already carries the broken line, since fixing the emitter does not fix a recorded file.) — **EXTENDED 2026-08-09 (F30, §11.140(e)): NO — and the reason is that this field has no archived recordings at all.** The recorder's own default output directory is `AppSettings::getConfigDir()` = `~/.spacecrafter/`, name `record_<date>.sts` `[observed: script_mgr.cpp:212-214]`; it holds **0** `.sts` files, and all 408 `.sts` on this field live under `scripts/` `[measured: find]`. Over the 431-file recording+script corpus: **`look delta_az` 0 hits; `delta_az` in any position 0 hits** `[measured: artifacts/f30/corpus_report.txt]`. Controls on the same channel: 9682 command-shaped lines in 274 files, and the ramp's CORRECT sibling **`zoom delta_fov` returns 1 hit** (`doc/superscript.sts:1396`) — the sharpest control available, since it proves a line of exactly that shape is findable by this instrument. **So fixing the emitter would orphan nothing here.** Qualifier that belongs to the decision: this is ONE installed field, §2.0 D9 freezes delivered fields individually, and an operator's own recordings are precisely the files this scan cannot see elsewhere — the negative is *"no recording exists on the reference field"*, which is weaker than *"no recording exists"*. **Precision correction to this row:** `delta_az` is indeed unregistered (0 definitions in the tree), but **`delta_alt` IS a registered word** — `W_DELTA_ALT` = `"delta_alt"` `[observed: base_command_interface.hpp:211]` — read by `commandMoveto` as an observer ALTITUDE delta in metres `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:3404]`. It does not merely fail to resolve; it collides with a different meaning on a different command. This changes no behaviour (the line dies at the command name, which does not exist, so the keys are never reached) and it does bear on how the replacement spelling should be chosen.

71. **`Core::panView` — the turn ramp's own COMMAND twin — still moves the old navigator alone.** [observed 2026-08-02, §11.133(i)] `look_at delta_azimuth <d> delta_altitude <d>` reaches `Core::panView`, which calls `setFlagTracking(0)` (dual) and then `navigation->updateMove(delta_az, delta_alt, projection->getFov(), duration)` `[observed: core.hpp:264-267, app_command_interface.cpp:3197]` — the OLD path only, exactly as the key ramp did before §11.133. It is the last member of B34's class and the fix is one line at the same seam, `Camera::lookRel(delta_alt, delta_az, duration)`, now that `lookRel` carries old's convention. NOT taken by F25 for two stated reasons: it is inside the `look_at` family, whose two halves already land 94.4° apart (§5.66) behind §11.92(d)'s suspended heading-coupling question; and unlike the ramp it uses `duration > 0`, where old runs an atan-eased `auto_move` (which also arms the view offset) against the camera's constant-min-acceleration law — **perceptual parity, not exact**, i.e. precisely the class the B34 mandate says to record rather than approximate. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-02 by F25; blocked on §5.66/§11.92(d) rather than on effort. Owed before it is judged: whether the duration branch should ride the camera's own law or whether `look_at delta_*` should mirror with duration 0 like the ramp, which is a feel answer.)

72. **The `$LOGON` feedback channel is documented as the LOG feed and carries other clients' command answers.** [observed 2026-08-02, §11.135(c)] A client subscribes with `$LOGON` and is answered *"Vous receverez maintenant les logs"* `[observed: io.cpp:616]`; the same subscription is what `broadcast` addresses when the application produces command output `[observed: io.cpp:641-655]`, so every subscriber sees every other client's `get`/`search` answer — and, before §11.135, was the ONLY thing that saw them. F27 left this delivery exactly as it was and ADDED the addressed copy, so nothing a shipped client may depend on was removed; the conflation itself is untouched. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-02 by F27 while fixing §5.47; record-don't-fix: removing the broadcast copy of an addressed answer is user-visible semantics — a control room subscribed to watch what operators ask would go quiet — and the channel's two meanings (logs, answers) may be one product feature or two. Recorded as a VETO POINT in §11.135(c): one condition in `ServerSocket::deliver` reverses it. Owed before it is judged: whether any shipped client subscribes with `$LOGON` and parses command answers off that stream.) — **EXTENDED 2026-08-09 (F30, §11.140(f)): YES — and the two shipped clients fall on OPPOSITE sides of the decision.** Scan over 1158 files (repo `util/`+`www/`+`sts-extension/`, installed client surfaces, whole `src/`, shipped scripts and data): **`$LOGON` 8 hits in 4 files**, control on the same channel `socket|tcp` = 235 hits in 26 files `[measured: harness/f30_corpus.py, artifacts/f30/corpus_report.txt]`. The server advertises it at `io.cpp:644` (`$NOTICE $LOGON $LOGOFF`); the other three `src` hits are §11.135's own comments. The two clients: **`util/server_standalone/send_recev_client.c:53`** writes `"$LOGON\n"`, reads the subscription confirmation, and then issues `get status constellation`, `get status position` and `search name m1`, **reading the answer off that same socket after each** — so it does consume command answers from the feedback stream, and it matches them **POSITIONALLY**, one `read()` per command, which means it depends on that stream's ordering and an interleaved answer from another client desynchronises it. **`util/server_standalone/recever_client.c:46`** writes `"$LOGON"` and then only loops `read()` + `printf`: **it issues no command at all, so the feedback stream is its ONLY source of anything.** Neither *parses* in the sense of branching on content — both print what arrives. **What this gives the decision:** removing the broadcast copy of an addressed answer would make `recever_client.c` go **silent entirely**, while `send_recev_client.c` would keep working, because §11.135 now delivers the addressed copy it actually wanted. Both are demonstrators rather than control rooms, and that is part of the weighing too. Observation recorded but NOT minted as a row (client-side, `util/`, not the application): `recever_client.c` reads up to `SIZEBUFFER` = 128 B into a 128-byte buffer and `printf("%s")`s it, so a full read leaves no terminator — and post-§5.73 the server sends answers up to 1024 B.

73. **An answer of 1023 bytes or more overflows the server's shared buffer.** [derived 2026-08-02 from the source, §11.135(j); not reproduced] `setOutput` clamps the answer to `MAX_BUFFER` = **1024** `[observed: io.cpp:63, 344-348]`; the send path then copies `data + '\n'` with `strcpy`, i.e. **L + 2** bytes for an answer of length L, into `buffer = new char[bufferSize]` `[observed: io.cpp:175, the strcpy in broadcast/deliver]`, where `bufferSize` is `tcp_buffer_in_size` = **1024** in the loaded config `[observed: ~/.spacecrafter/config.ini:18]`. So L >= 1023 writes past the end — up to 2 bytes at the clamp. Pre-existing on both binaries and untouched by §11.135's routing change (the `strcpy` ran even when nobody received the string); the answers with no length bound between producer and clamp are `get status object` (`getSelectedObjectInfo`) and `search` (`getListMatchingObjects`). — **FIXED 2026-08-08 (F28, §11.138; code `5b86be0f`). The owed measurement was taken first, on the wire, on the shipped corpus** `[measured: harness/f28_send_buffer.py --mode census]`: `get status object` = **114 / 117 / 117 / 118 / 116 B** for Mars / Saturn / Earth / Sun / Moon and **142 B** for a nebula — structurally unbounded, in fact a fixed five-line form, so it is NOT the reachable half; `search`, swept over all 26 first letters at `maxobject 320`, answers **53–1024 B**, and **`search name n maxobject 320` = 1024 B, CLAMPED** — the overflow is reachable today by typing one shipped command, with no data added. A producer this row did not name is next in line: **`get status planets_position` = 854 B**, 83 % of the buffer, growing with the loaded system (measured ladder 854 → 916 → 978 → 1024; ~5 `body action load`s cross the clamp). **The fix is sizing-only and was decision-free for a reason this row did not carry: the shared buffer never TRUNCATED anything** — a too-long message was not cut to `bufferSize`, it was written past the end — so its size expressed no policy that removing it could change. `setOutput`'s 1024 clamp is untouched, `tcp_buffer_in_size` still sizes exactly the receive buffer, and the wire rule is still `strlen + 1`. Root (I6): `send()` did not carry what it sends; it now takes the message (`send(TCPsocket, const char *)`), all nine call sites pass their own, and every `strcpy(buffer, …)` on the send path is gone. **Discriminated both ways on one drive** (854/854/916/1022/1023/1024, four fresh launches): ASan pre-fix reports `WRITE of size 1025 … 0 bytes after 1024-byte region` at `io.cpp:703` plus the read-back at 740 and the transmission of the overrun byte at 741, with 1022 SILENT and 1023 firing — one byte wide — and **0 reports post-fix on the same drive**; all four binaries deliver byte-identical answers at all six lengths. **What is still Vixy's, unchanged: the truncation POLICY.** The clamp now has a second, closer case — `get status planets_position` is 170 B from silently truncating a shipped readout, and a truncated answer ends mid-token with no marker (measured at step 5: `…NGC3190(N);NGC319`).)

74. **`search` returns no star and no constellation on the shipped corpus.** [measured 2026-08-08, §11.138(h)] `Core::listMatchingObjectsI18n` aggregates four catalogues — planets, constellations, nebulae, stars `[observed: coreModule/core.cpp:2367-2399]` — and tags each entry `(P)`/`(C)`/`(N)`/`(S)`. Over a 26-letter sweep at `maxobject 320` (where the per-catalogue quota is not the binding constraint), the two answers captured in full — `m` (854 B, 118 entries) and `n` (1024 B, 93 entries) — carry `(P)` and `(N)` only, and 233 entries sampled from the head and tail of all 26 add zero `(S)` and zero `(C)` `[measured: harness/artifacts/f28/f28_census.json]`. So `search` addresses half the catalogue surface it is written for. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-08 by F28 while measuring §5.73's producers; record-don't-fix: outside that task's boundary, and the cause is not established — the star/constellation name catalogues may simply not be loaded in a default launch, or the prefix match may never fire. Owed before it is judged: which of the two it is, on one launch, with the catalogue's own count as the positive control.) — **EXTENSION 2026-08-09 (F31, §11.141): THE OWED DISCRIMINATION IS PAID — it is NOT LOADED, and the load never ran.** One launch under gdb, each catalogue's own count read at the entry the aggregation itself calls, with the answering catalogues on the SAME channel in the SAME call `[measured: harness/f31_search_drive.py, artifacts/f31/f31_result.json]`: planets **90**, nebulae **407** — constellations **0**, star name-index **0**, sci index **0**, english star map **0**; the 36-prefix sweep against those counts gives **P 84 · C 0 · N 243 · S 0** (F28 reproduced: `m` 118 entries, `n` 93). `ConstellationMgr::loadLinesAndArt` and `HipStarMgr::loadCommonNames` are entered **0 times** in the whole startup, while `Core::setSkyCultureDir`'s reject branch fires **once** on the configured `western-spacecrafter`: the gate is `skyloc->directoryToSkyCultureEnglish(cultureDir) == ""` `[observed: core.cpp:1429-1432]`, that map is built only from each culture's `info.ini` `info:name` `[observed: sky_localizer.cpp:57-66]`, and **all 2922 files under `~/.spacecrafter/sky_cultures` are 0 bytes**, `western-spacecrafter/info.ini` included `[measured 2026-08-09]` — the same field-content class as the 13 zero-byte `stellar_systems/*.ini` at §11.109/§11.115, and not a blanket truncation (`landscapes` 0/147, `textures` 0/678 are zero). **THE MATCH IS SOUND**: on the same launch, loading a culture from outside the frozen field (`star_names.fab` a verbatim md5-asserted copy of the installed `stars/name.fab`; the constellation pair synthetic and labelled) took the catalogues to **3** and **3183** and the SAME 36 commands to **P 84 · C 3 · N 241 · S 1085** — the prefix taken from the LIVE index (`1`, from its own key `"1 AND"`) going **0 → 104 `(S)`** — with `(P)`/`(N)` unchanged on all 27 unclamped prefixes. **Still OPEN, and now Vixy's, because the cause is catalogue content and not code** (record-don't-fix honoured). **The question for Vixy:** does the delivered `spacecrafter-data` carry the sky-culture files, or ship them empty as this install has them? If it ships content, this row closes as an install artifact of this workstation; if it ships them empty, every install draws no constellation and knows no star name, and §5.77 is why nobody is told. Two code-level consequences were split off rather than folded in: **§5.77** (the rejection never reaches the app's own log) and **§5.78** (`loadSciNames` has no caller, so the sci half cannot answer whatever is installed).

75. **`TrailModule::accumulate` silently DROPS a sample when a step lands a hair short of `DeltaTrail` — a date-stepping show records a trail that does not reach its own body.** [measured 2026-08-09, §11.139(k)(2)] The gate is `dt = std::abs(static_cast<int>((date - lastJD) / deltaTrail))` with `date = body->getLastJD()` = the LIGHT-RETARDED jd, and `deltaTrail` is 1.0 sim-day for every body (`TrailLoader.cpp:28`, "always 1 sim-day (old never data-set)"). So a commanded 1.0-day step arrives as 0.9999998, truncates to **0**, and records nothing — and the miss is not recovered, because the next step measures from the same `lastJD`. Measured on a frozen-clock drive that stepped `date jday` by exactly 1.0 day thirty times: **21 of 30 samples taken on Earth, 16 of 30 on Mars**, with the newest sample left a full sim-day behind the body, i.e. the drawn trail visibly does not reach the body it belongs to (it was 366 px short in the F29 scene). Pre-existing and identical on both F29 binaries; ported arithmetic from old `trail.cpp`. Under a RUNNING clock the elapsed time keeps growing, so a sample is only LATE, not lost — the loss is specific to date JUMPS, which is what every scripted show does. Stepping 1.5 days makes the truncation unconditional (31 of 31, lag 0.0 d), which is how F29's instrument works around it. — **OPEN (found 2026-08-09 by F29; NOT fixed: the honest shapes — round instead of truncate, or accumulate the remainder — change how many samples a shipped show's trail carries, which is trail-sampling POLICY and user-visible; the fix itself is one expression.)**

76. **Slowing down a PAUSED clock makes time run BACKWARD at real time — the `timerate` ladder reads the gated readout as if it were the rate the operator set.** [measured 2026-08-09, §11.140(b), 5 predictions written before the run and 5 hit] All four `timerate action` ×crement branches open the same way `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:3327/3345/3360/3376]`: `double s = coreLink->timeGetSpeed();` — the **gated** readout, which is 0 while any lock is held (§5.64) — then `coreLink->timeSetFlagPause(false)`, then a ladder that recomputes the new rate **from `s`**. So while a pause is held the operator's rate is not slowed or doubled, it is **lost**, and the ladders' zero branches decide what replaces it: `increment` takes `s >= 0. && s < JD_SECOND` → `s = JD_SECOND`, and **`decrement` takes `s > -JD_SECOND && s <= 0.` → `s = -JD_SECOND`** `[:3366-3369]`. Measured from `timerate rate 3` `[measured: harness/f30_timerate.py, artifacts/f30/f30_timerate.json]`: with a pause held, `timerate action decrement` leaves rate **−1.000000** and the simulation date FALLING (2461261.522283792 → 2461261.522272217); the control — same command, same set rate, no pause held — leaves **+1.500000**. One command, one difference, and the discriminator is the sign. Both halves are on the live free-operator key surface, each guarded by "no script playing": the pause key reaches `timerate action pause` `[observed: ui.cpp:795]`, **`SDL_SCANCODE_J`** reaches `timerate action decrement` `[observed: ui.cpp:1547]`, and the held-key ramp reaches `sincrement`/`sdecrement` `[observed: ui.cpp:922-924]`; the script engine issues `timerate action pause` for its own pause `[observed: script_mgr.cpp:133]`. No shipped script issues any `timerate action` (0 of 408 `[measured: §11.140(g)]`), so the reachable surface is the operator's, not the corpus's. Each branch also RECORDS `timerate rate <s/JD_SECOND>` `[observed: :3341,:3358,:3374,:3390]`, so a recording replays the wrong rate — the same R2 live-show channel loss class as §5.70. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-09 by F30 while discharging §5.64's owed enumeration; record-don't-fix: what *"slow down"* should mean when the clock is held is user-visible semantics, and it is the same decision family as §5.64 without being the same defect — it survives whichever way the pause/clock question is answered, since the ladder would still read a readout of 0. The honest shapes are to read the raw rate here, or to make the zero branches direction-preserving; both change what a shipped key does. Owed before it is judged: nothing scannable — it is a semantics call.) — **ANNOTATED 2026-08-23 (§11.147, divergence-resolution validation).** The corpus clause is OVER-BROAD: *"No shipped script issues any `timerate action` (0 of 408)"* measured S1 (`~/.spacecrafter/scripts/**`) only, and `doc/superscript.sts:1302-1309` issues **six** — `pause`, `resume`, `increment step 2`, `sincrement step 1.05`, `decrement step 2`, `sdecrement step 1.05` [verified 2026-08-23 at the byte]. The row's CONCLUSION survives, and for a reason the row can now state instead of assume: the defect is not triggered there, because `resume` at `:1303` releases the pause before the increment/decrement block — so "the reachable surface is the operator's, not the corpus's" now rests on the trigger rather than on a count that was wrong. Citation drift, pre-existing: the zero-branch `[:3366-3369]` is the code at **:3370-3371**. And this row's `commandline` rider records THE SAME FOUR LINES as §5.96(d) (`app_command_interface.cpp:3341/3358/3374/3390`, four identical `commandline = "timerate rate " + std::to_string(s/JD_SECOND)`) — one real behaviour written up twice by the two diverged trees. ONE FACET, cited from both rows; not two findings.

Of the **39** startup failure-report sites, the app log carries **nothing** for 18 and **only the ATTEMPT** for 19 — the second group is this row's own shape, where the log implies success; **the class is 37**, and just **2** of the 39 already do what this row wants (`protosystem.cpp:616`, `ui_tui.cpp:813`). **The channel in this row's title is too narrow**: the class spans `std::cerr`, `std::cout`, `printf`, `fprintf(stderr)` and `SDL_Log`, and its **largest single cluster is 15 `printf`/`fprintf` sites in `ZoneArray::create`**. **No uniform additive routing exists**: 2 sites execute BEFORE `openLog` (`main.cpp:199` vs `:209`) where `cLog::write`'s `logFile.at(INTERNAL)` would throw; 13 are newline-less sentence FRAGMENTS whose composing head `printf` is commented out, so routing them requires new wording (§2(f)); 1 IS the log failing to open; 4 are not compiled on this platform; and **17 would change the console**, because `print_log = true` in the installed `config.ini` and `cLog` at `L_ERROR` already writes `std::cerr` `[observed: log.cpp:122-123,206-210; main.cpp:262]` — **measured: 654 cLog lines on the console of this run**, so this row's proposed one-liner prints the message TWICE. **This row's own site was re-measured at `04ae1d3e` and is unchanged** (console yes, `spacecrafter.log` **0**, line 39 still `Check sky_cultures subdirectory ok`), **and it is not alone on a default launch**: two `ZoneArray::create(...): fopen failed` fire the same way, leaving the app on **26 561 stars** at `max_geodesic_level: 1` with only `Loading catalog X` in the log (→ §5.90), and a fourth failure reports **nowhere at all** (→ §5.88). **Still OPEN, and the owed item is now a QUESTION FOR VIXY rather than a measurement:** should a startup failure appear on the console twice when `print_log = true`? Yes ⇒ the additive call lands at 20 sites, 13 needing new wording first. 80. **Entering free flight TELEPORTS the observer — `camera action free_mode state on` swings it ~125° around its reference at constant distance.** [measured 2026-08-09, F33 §11.143(k)] `Camera::setFreeMode` converts the pose with `spheToRect(-longitude, latitude)·distance`, which is NOT the inverse of the parametrization `viewMat()`'s anchored branch composes (`p = Z(lon)·X(π/2−lat)·(0,0,distance)`); the two differ by a rotation, so the DRAWN eye position moves. **MEASURED on the shipped config, three ways:** standing on Earth the observer moves **7.5526e-05 AU (11 300 km)** on `state on` and returns to **1.14e-11 AU** on `state off`; standing 5.5e+04 km from Mars it moves **6.6479e-04 AU (99 450 km)**, exactly the chord of a **124.8°** swing at constant radius (`selDist` is therefore BLIND to it — the distance to the reference is preserved by construction, which is why this survived); and the composed SCREEN differs by **6354 px >8** across the toggle against an **A/A control of 0 px**. New-path only (the old path has no free mode). Reversible, so a toggle pair is inert — which is why `switchToBody`'s internal round trip is unaffected. — **OPEN (recorded by F33, out of its scope: the fix is a choice between the two parametrizations and the losing one is used by `moveTo`'s free branch, `descend`, `moveEyeRel` and `setFreeMode(false)` alike, so it is a free-flight semantics change, not a sign flip. Owed before it is judged: which of the two conventions the free-flight movers were BUILT against — B10/B21 measured their behaviour, not their frame.)** — **EXTENSION 2026-08-09 (F34, §11.144; code UNCHANGED at `204d402e`): THE OWED DATUM IS ANSWERED, and the row's own text is corrected on one point.** **(A) is used by exactly the members that CONVERT between the anchored spherical triple and the free cartesian member — `setFreeMode` in both directions and `moveTo`'s free branch — and by nothing else.** `moveEyeRel` and `descend` perform NO spherical↔cartesian conversion anywhere: they add a vector to `position` in the frame `viewMat`'s free branch defines, and they are exact against the COMPOSER (`ΔE == −Rotᵀ·v` measured **5.514e-12** and **3.431e-12 AU** against **6.685e-06 AU** for the flipped sign), so the clause above naming them among (A)'s users is superseded — measured, not re-argued. Per-mover, winner vs loser on the DRAWN matrix, predictions committed at harness `bb4809b` before the run: `setFreeMode(true)` → `−Z(θ)·pA` **8.609e-11 AU** vs no-move **9.180e-05**; `moveTo` free → `−Z(θ)·pA` of the COMMANDED triple **1.263e-10** vs `Z(θ)·pB` **1.116e-04**; `setFreeMode(false)` → `Z(θ)·pB` of A⁻¹ **1.207e-10** vs no-move **1.143e-04**; anchored calibration `Z(θ)·pB` **8.483e-11** vs the (A) reading **3.610e-05 AU**. **THIS ROW NAMES ONLY HALF THE DEFECT:** (B) is the EYE's position while (A) is written into `position`, and `viewMat`'s free branch `mat = R·T(position)` makes that member MINUS the eye — so the converter is wrong by a SIGN as well as an azimuth handedness, the two composing into exactly one rotation, **180° about the horizontal axis at azimuth 45° in the pose frame** (the observer's LATITUDE flips sign). That is discriminating, because an azimuth-only defect gives the SUPPLEMENTARY swing: **measured 137.0620°, predicted 137.0620°, azimuth-only rival 42.9380°**; and at THIS ROW's own shipped place the closed form predicts **124.6797° / 11298.6 km** and measures **124.6797° / 11298.6 km** (the row was minted on 124.8° / 11 300 km). **WHICH SIDE OF THE SEAM: the CONVERTER** — the composer is what every anchored place, every anchored `moveto` and both free-flight movers already agree with, and the three converter sites are the only expressions that disagree with it; re-expressing the converter leaves `moveEyeRel`/`descend` and every anchored place untouched. **That does NOT make the repair decision-free and none is proposed here**: what `moveto lat/lon` MEANS in free flight and what `get status position` should answer there are user-visible semantics, so the fix stays Vixy's exactly as this row says. **Two riders, measured:** `get status position` is blind to the teleport too — `getPlace()`'s free branch is A⁻¹ of what the converter just wrote, so it returns the ENTRY TRIPLE (`lat 30.0 lon 70.0 alt 999999.83`, identical across a 13 732 km move), i.e. NEITHER shipped observable can see this; and the same command lands **16 700.0 km / 170.604° apart** depending only on whether free flight is on. The reversible pair was traversed TWICE with a `descend` between the traverses and stays inert (**2.477e-12 AU**, **0 px**). — **[THE CONVERTER HALF READS AS AUTHORIZED 2026-08-26 — D15(b) ANSWERED [vixy 2026-08-26, harness `2b24a1b`], reading recorded at §11.149(b); NOTHING IMPLEMENTED HERE, the row stays OPEN.]** Verbatim: *"freeMode swapping in both ways must be transparent - holding view continuity, under a dome where the visual cues is the projection, has the same structure as VR helmet. Brutal changes are disorienting … "*. This row's blocker was that the repair is user-visible free-flight semantics and therefore Vixy's; D15(b) is Vixy stating those semantics. Why the answer reaches a POSITION defect [derived]: *"in both ways must be transparent"* names the SWAP, not a channel, and the swap's largest observable is this row's 11 300 km / 124.6797° teleport at **6354 px>8** against a **0 px** A/A floor — a criterion whose whole justification is perceptual (dome audience, VR-helmet structure, *"brutal changes are disorienting"*) cannot exclude it. ⇒ the converter-as-composer's-inverse repair is authorized. **VETO POINT: D15(b)'s sentence is about VIEW CONTINUITY and may have been meant about orientation only** — in which case this row stays suspended and nothing is lost. **The unblock is PARTIAL and the remainder is NOT decided by it**: what `moveto lat/lon` MEANS in free flight, and what `get status position` should answer there (measured blind — it returns the ENTRY TRIPLE), stay Vixy's; whoever lands the converter may not settle those two on the way. **[VETO CLOSED — RATIFIED vixy 2026-08-26, §11.151(a): transparency includes the position channel ⇒ the converter-half fix is DECISION-FREE (task F40); the two riders stay open and untouched by it.]** — **FIXED AND CLOSED 2026-08-26 (F40, §11.153; code `7ef11aca → 18b6f13f`).** The converter is now the composer's EXACT inverse through ONE authority, `Camera::posePart` / `posePartToPose` — and **the site count in this row is corrected: there are FOUR, not three**. `getPlace()` is the fourth (`Camera.cpp:41-54`), and its own header doc already promised *"the SAME conversion setFreeMode(false) uses"* — a promise kept by copy, the §11.19 drift class; `placeAt` reads the pair too rather than restating the algebra a fifth time. Licensed before the code moved (`f40_probe.cpp`, **84/84**, 8 poses × boundToSurface off/on; the surface fold CANCELS, so the repair is fold-independent). Measured with one instrument scoring both conventions, on two binaries (`89d66cd3` / `98d09488`): **29/29 pre, 32/32 post**. This row's own numbers, both ways — entry toggle **13 732.4 km → 0.0009 km** and **174 px>8 → 0 px** against a 0 px A/A control; the shipped Earth place **124.6797° / 11 298.6 km → 0.312 m** (null control 0.207 m; float32 ulp at that radius is 0.38 m) with **4905 px>8 → 1 px** at flags-off; **Mars 39 945.9 km / 5714 px → 0.0031 km / 0 px**; `moveto` free-vs-anchored **16 700.0 km → 1.631 m** (3.3 ulp); the reversible pair entered twice with a flight between and the second entry from the first exit's state, **every teleport ≤ 2.3 m**. `descend`/`moveEyeRel` untouched and measured so (**2.252e-12 / 2.011e-12 AU** against F34's own predictions and tolerances); anchored places bit-identical (8 places, 3 reference bodies, 32 dumped camera fields each, composed screens **max abs pixel difference 0** over 2048×2048); A–D battery + `b24_equivalence` (120 bodies) + `b24_screen` green; md5 `03fbee59`/`545a51ef` in == out on every run. **THE TWO RIDERS STAY OPEN AND ARE MEASURED TO BE UNTOUCHED:** `get status position` answers the entry triple to every printed digit across the toggle, exactly as before (leaving `getPlace()` on the old conversion is what would have CHANGED it — see §11.153(e)); and `moveto lat/lon` now names one place in both modes, which restores the header's own stated contract without deciding whether free flight should give it a different meaning (§11.153(f), veto point at each). **Two channels this fix does NOT reach, both recorded with measurements and neither touched:** the shipped-flags 3.3 Mpx frame change is the environment's own free-mode gate (**new §5.106**; §11.144(i)'s attribution refuted — it survives the fix at 3 300 632 px while the observer moves 0.241 m), and the 90° between `moveto lon` and `orbit_lon` is §5.49's channel (sharpened there, fixed nowhere).
154. **TWO POST-ROUND RULINGS [vixy 2026-08-26] (Claude Fable 5, supervising; record-only).** (a) The F40 residual class (≤2.3 m/toggle, float32 ulp) is an ACCEPTED DIVERGENCE with structure: cause attributed, magnitude predicted, and a usage-path model — the mode switch serves INTERACTIVE navigation (human loop corrects compounding deltas), scripts don't use free movement ⇒ the residual's beyond-visual consequences land on no uncorrected path. Model recorded as design input on §5.106 (veto point: close-vs-annotate). (b) **§5.104 ANSWERED — scaling ownership is FORMAT-SCOPED**: legacy = config.ini (never generalizable), modular = the new FILE, which DEPRECATES config.ini whenever the new format serves the solar system. Reload mechanics follow [derived, veto]: legacy reload re-applies config; new-format reload re-applies by construction (D31). Created: B28 sign-off owed on the new-format scaling key BEFORE any emitter writes it; deprecation logged once (§2(f)); D30 hybrid absorbs it per-branch. […] → INTENT/11.154.md

81. **A body sitting exactly at the eye gets a NaN `screenPos`.** [measured 2026-08-09, F33 §11.143(l)] `ModularBody::update`'s centre-singularity guard covers `rq → 0` at a finite distance, but at `distance == 0` its own fallback is `1/(distance·halfFov)` = ∞ and `mat.r[12]·∞` = NaN `[observed: ModularBody.hpp:486-506]`. Newly REACHABLE from a shipped command: `camera action transition_to target point` puts the camera AT its anchor body (distance 0 by design, mirroring old's `setAltitude(0)`), so that body's dump carries `"screen":[-nan,-nan]`. Confined: the anchor body is hidden, hence outside the draw and pick sweeps (§11.111(f)), so nothing consumes the value — but it is a NaN in a member, and it broke a JSON reader on contact. — **OPEN (recorded, not fixed: F33's scope boundary is the transition surface, and "where is a body that is at the eye" is a projection question. The limit is not ambiguous — the centre, (0,0) — so the guard looks decision-free; what is owed is whether any consumer should instead be told "this body has no screen position".)** — **CLOSED 2026-08-09, F35 §11.145(a)(b)(f), code `c12ed803`.** The owed question is ANSWERED and the answer is that the marker already exists elsewhere: `screenPos` has **six** consumers `[observed]` — `drawHaloCore`, `HintModule`'s circle and label, `StarModule::drawBigHalo`, the selection pointer, the pick sweep `findBodyAt`, and `dumpTrace` — and **none needs one**, because *"does this body belong to the drawn surface"* has ONE authority already: the body/orbit sweeps `break` on the `distance == 0` tail (which the descending sort puts last) and the pointer + pick ask `operator bool` over a list that `propagateRenderHidden` **unregisters a hidden subtree from** `[observed: ModularBody.cpp:196-206]`. The point anchor is `relation` **1 = HIDDEN_ORBITING** `[measured]`, so `dumpTrace` is the only consumer that ever sees it, and a NaN sentinel would have been a second, silent authority (I2). The VALUE is forced rather than chosen: `distance` is the norm of (r[12],r[13],r[14]), so at 0 both numerators are exactly 0 and the answer is (0,0) for **every finite f**, independent of transfer, halfFov and the 1e-5 constant. **MEASURED**: `screen` **[nan,nan] → [0,0]** at `dist` 0, 120 other bodies finite and unchanged in kind; the branch **OBSERVED** at its own instruction — **0** hits over a non-degenerate scene, **1298** over the transitioned one, every hit `temp_point`. Recorded not minted (unreachable today): a registered, non-hidden body at distance 0 would now win the pick everywhere, `screenSize` being 2 against a unit rect.

82. **`camera action transition_to target point name <X>` DROPS its documented `name`.** [observed 2026-08-09, F33 §11.143(c)] The interface hard-codes the anchor name: `coreLink->cameraTransitionToPoint("temp_point")` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:4243]`. Every shipped use passes a name (`name Space`, 19 lines in 3 files) and none of them has ever had an effect; the anchor is always `temp_point`. Both paths receive the same hard-coded string from the dual seam, so they agree — the quirk is inherited, not introduced (measured: asked for `Space`, got `temp_point`, on both). — **OPEN (recorded, not fixed: honouring the argument would change what a shipped command DOES — 19 shipped lines would start creating differently-named anchors — so it is a product decision, not a repair. B28 also forbids minting around it.)** — **ANNOTATED 2026-08-23 (§11.147).** *"Every shipped use passes a name (19 lines in 3 files)"* is exact for `~/.spacecrafter/scripts/**` and over-broad as written: `doc/superscript.sts:175` and `:185` issue `camera action transition_to target point` with **no** `name` [verified 2026-08-23]. The defect claim is untouched; what changes is the product-decision framing — two reference-script call sites would be affected differently from the 19, since they name nothing today. Citation drift, pre-existing: `cameraTransitionToPoint("temp_point")` is at **:4241** (`:4243` is the `if (!result)`).

83. **`camera action move_to target body ... duration 0` produces a NaN position on the OLD path.** [derived 2026-08-09, F33 §11.143(f)] `AnchorManager::moveTo(anchor,time,alt)` has no `time == 0` branch (the point form at `:429` does), so `getTravelPosition` divides by `travelTime == 0` and `0/0` reaches `setCurrentAnchorPos` `[observed: anchor_manager.cpp:459-486, :406-418]`. The command accepts `duration 0` (only an EMPTY duration is refused, `:4306`). No shipped script uses it. The new path guards the degenerate case (a zero-duration travel IS its endpoint) because a NaN would poison the tree's cached frame state and the D8 barrier rejects NaN dates by design — a deliberate, recorded divergence. — **OPEN (old-path only, retire-with-old.)**

84. **`Body::getPositionAtDate` leaves the iterative solvers' Newton seed at the queried date.** [derived 2026-08-09, F33 §11.143(f)] `EllipticalOrbit::eccentricAnomaly` performs ONE Newton step per call seeded from the previous call's result `[observed: orbit.cpp:515-560]` — the mechanism §11.117's `useNow` re-converges with 1+4 iterations. `AnchorManager::moveTo(anchor,time,alt)` calls `getPositionAtDate(jd + travelTime)` on the target body `[observed: anchor_manager.cpp:477, body.cpp:1291]` and never re-converges, so the frames after a `move_to body` start their step from a future seed. Old-path only; the new path's counterpart re-converges at the frame's own date at the call site. — **OPEN (old-path only, retire-with-old; magnitude unmeasured — one Newton step from a seed at most a travel-duration away.)**

85. **`AnchorManager::alignCameraToBody` does not align anything — `camera action align_with body` computes a frame-scrambled angle.** [measured 2026-08-09, F33 §11.143(i)] `Navigator::alignUpVectorTo` builds `A = mat_local_to_eye · mat_earth_equ_to_local · mat_equ_to_vsop87ᵀ` (vsop87 → eye) and then applies its **TRANSPOSE** to the target axis, which its own comment says should be brought INTO the camera frame; and it feeds that transpose an axis converted **vsop87 → j2000** first, a frame the rest of the expression never speaks `[observed: navigator.cpp:455-479]`. The author's inline note says as much (*"I can't get the axis of the planet at the moment"*). **MEASURED through the live command channel, two black-box discriminators that need no frame algebra:** it is NOT IDEMPOTENT — a second `align_with Mars` moves the heading another **25.158°** (0 → −122.614 → −97.456) — and it is START-DEPENDENT — the same command from heading 40° lands **29.635°** away from where it lands from heading 0° (40 → −92.979). An alignment would be both. 0 shipped scripts use it. — **OPEN (this is B4(iv)'s recorded derivation gap: the member was NOT ported to the new path because the requirement its shape appears to encode is one old does not meet, and the two terms that fail — the transpose and the j2000 hop — have no camera-side expression to derive. Owed before it is judged: what the command is FOR, which only its author or a show that wants it can say.)** — **CORRECTED 2026-08-23 (§11.147): the "0 shipped scripts use it" clause is FALSE.** `doc/superscript.sts:187` reads `camera action align_with body selected duration 3` [verified 2026-08-23 at the byte]. The number is right for `~/.spacecrafter/scripts/**` (0 of 408); the sentence said *shipped*, and that includes the reference script which served for years as the documentation and the only functional test [vixy, §5.97]. This lands on the row's OWN owed item — *"what the command is FOR, which only its author or a show that wants it can say"*: there is now a first witness, a documented use, where the row recorded none. The corpus boundary, not carelessness, is the root: S1 was measured and "shipped" was written.

86. **`Camera::observedToBodyLocalPos` is not the inverse of `viewMat` either — the new path's RA/DE readout is computed in a scrambled frame.** [measured 2026-08-09, F34 §11.144(j)(1)] A DIFFERENT defect from §5.80, in a different member. The anchored branch applies `Y(latitude−π/2)` where `viewMat` composes `X(lat−π/2)`, `Z(−longitude)` where the inverse needs `Z(+lon)`, and subtracts `distance` where the inverse adds it, and it omits the surface fold entirely `[observed: Camera.hpp:264-274 vs Camera.cpp:183-197]`. **MEASURED by round trip through the project's own primitives** (`harness/f34_probe_inverse.cpp`): a body-frame point pushed through `viewMat` and back through this method comes back **133.9041° away** from itself, where the algebraic inverse recovers it to **8.135e-12 AU**. The FREE branch is exact when unbound (**2.058e-11 AU**) and off by the surface fold when bound (**38.5856°** at `axisRot` 0.7 rad), so only free+unbound is right. Reachable from the new path's object readouts — `observedPosToRaDe` and this method feed `ModularObject` `[observed: ModularObject.cpp:19, :47, :115, :152]` — hence a row by §5.79's criterion. `observedPosToAltAz` is NOT affected: it rides `observedToLocalPos` (`viewRotationᵀ`), which is the honest zenith-frame direction. — **OPEN (recorded by F34, out of its scope: F34 is record-only on the free-flight conventions. This is the precise mechanism of the caveat `Camera.hpp:258` already flags as open, the §11.4 absolute-calibration item — so the fix belongs with whoever closes §11.4, and the algebraic inverse is written out in the probe. Owed before it is judged: whether any consumer depends on today's answer, and what the old path's RA/DE says for the same body — a parity target this row does not assume.)**

87. **`select constellation_star <abbrev>` acts on the PREVIOUSLY selected constellation when the abbreviation does not resolve.** [measured 2026-08-09, F35 §11.145(e)] `ConstellationMgr::setSelected(const std::string&)` returns without touching anything when `findFromAbbreviation` gives nullptr `[observed: constellation_mgr.cpp:723-729]`, and `Core::selectObject` then reads `asterisms->getSelected()` on the very next line `[observed: core.cpp:1063]` — so a name the culture does not carry selects the brightest star of whatever was selected before, with no diagnostic. **MEASURED on F31's fixture culture**: `select constellation_star ZZNOSUCH` after `select constellation_star F3A` still answers `43 And` / `HP 5447` and `F3A` `[artifacts/f35/post/f35_result_post.json B3]`. Distinct from §5.79, which was the EMPTY case and is closed: this is the non-empty one, unchanged by that fix by construction (D8). — **OPEN (recorded, not fixed: the right answer is a user-visible choice among unselect / no-op / today's behaviour, with a §2(f) diagnostic question attached to each — product semantics, so Vixy's. Minted by §5.79's reachability criterion: a shipped command gives a wrong answer. Owed before it is judged: whether any show relies on the current forgiving behaviour, and whether the same shape exists on the `constellation` and `star` branches of the same switch.)**
88. **A star catalogue that is missing is reported NOWHERE — not the log, not even the console.** [measured 2026-08-09, §11.146(g)] `HipStarMgr::load_data` wraps the spectral-catalogue read in `try`/`catch` and would print `ERROR while loading data from …` `[observed: hip_star_mgr.cpp:489-493]`, but `StringArray::initFromFile` opens with `FILE *f = fopen(...); if (f) { … }` and **returns with `size == 0` when the file is absent** `[observed: string_array.cpp:33-45]` — no throw, so no catch, so no message. Measured on a default launch: `~/.spacecrafter/stars.ini` names `cat_hip_sp_file_name = stars_hip_sp_0v0_4.cat` (cited fetch, md5 `0aec3e72c25cd709abb46e37a6c924af`) while the data root carries only `stars_hip_sp_0v0_0.cat`, and `ERROR while loading data` appears **0 times** in the process's whole console output `[measured: artifacts/f36/f36.applog]` and 0 times in `spacecrafter.log`. The spectral-type array is silently empty for the run. This is the LIMIT CASE beyond §5.77 — not "reports only on stderr" but "reports nowhere" — and the same shape covers `cat_hip_cids_file_name`. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-09 by F36 while sizing §5.77; record-don't-fix: outside that task's boundary, and the repair is a choice between making `initFromFile` report, making it throw so the existing `catch` fires, or having the caller check — three different contracts, one of which changes control flow. Owed before it is judged: what an empty spectral array costs at draw time, which this task did not measure.)

89. **`Core::init` prints "Unknown viewing mode type" and then CARRIES ON, because the `assert(0)` under it is a no-op in the build type this project ships.** [measured + observed 2026-08-09, §11.146(h)] `core.cpp:487-492` reports an unrecognised `[navigation] viewing_mode` to `std::cerr` and follows it with `assert(0)`; the project builds `RelWithDebInfo`, whose flags are `-O2 -g -DNDEBUG` `[measured: build-claude/CMakeCache.txt]`, so the assert compiles to nothing and execution falls through with `navigation->setViewingMode` never called on that path — the app runs in whatever mode the member defaulted to. The guard's author plainly meant it to be fatal (there is no other handling), so this is a dead guard, not a lenient one. Reachable from a one-word typo in the user's `config.ini`. Compounding it, the only report is on `stderr`: `spacecrafter.log` says nothing, which is also a §2.0 D12 miss (an acting default that is not logged). — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-09 by F36 while sizing §5.77; record-don't-fix: the repair is a choice among abort, fall back to a NAMED default and log it (D12), or refuse to start — user-visible startup semantics, so Vixy's. Owed before it is judged: whether any other `assert` in `src/` is load-bearing in the same way, which F36 did not sweep.) — **INFORMED, NOT DECIDED, 2026-08-26 (D15(a) → §11.149(a1)):** [vixy] *"the new system avoid the use of up vector to avoid this edge case, so the VIEW_HORIZON is no longer usefull."* The repair fork here is untouched — but the answer removes one candidate: on the drawn path a fallback to the EQUATORIAL mount is now the only defensible *"named default"*, since the other shipped `viewing_mode` value is declared useless there. Note the boundary the answer does not cross: it is not authority to remove `VIEW_HORIZON` from the config vocabulary (§2.0 D9/D13), so this guard still has two values to recognise.

90. **The star-catalogue LIST is read from the user directory and the catalogue FILES from the data root, so a list naming versions the data root lacks loads a fraction of the sky, silently.** [measured 2026-08-09, §11.146(g)] `HipStarMgr::load_data` loads `getConfigDir() + "stars.ini"` `[observed: hip_star_mgr.cpp:452]` — `m_configDir` is `~/.spacecrafter/` `[observed: app_settings.cpp:51,132; main.cpp:185,257]` — while `ZoneArray::create` opens `getDataRoot() + "stars/" + fname` `[observed: zone_array.cpp:100]` = `/usr/local/share/spacecrafter/stars/`. Two roots, one name list, no one checking they agree (I2). On this install they do not: `stars.ini` asks for `stars_2_0v0_8.cat` and `stars_3_1v0_4.cat`, the data root carries `stars_2_0v0_5.cat` and `stars_3_1v0_3.cat`, and `~/.spacecrafter/stars/` — where the loader does NOT look — does carry the requested versions. **Measured consequence on a default launch**: both `fopen`s fail, `spacecrafter.log` reports `Loading catalog X` for each and no outcome, its own summary reads `finished, max_geodesic_level: 1`, and the app runs on **26 561 stars** (4 964 + 21 597) instead of the level-2/3 catalogues `[measured: artifacts/f36/farm_f36/.spacecrafter/log/spacecrafter.log:758-771, f36.applog]`. The failure text exists but goes only to `stderr`/`stdout` (§5.77's class, member 2 and 3). — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-09 by F36; record-don't-fix: which root is authoritative for a user-supplied catalogue list is a data-layout decision, and whether THIS install's mismatch is local or shipped is the same question §5.74 already put to Vixy. Owed before it is judged: whether the delivered `spacecrafter-data` ships a `stars.ini` whose names match the catalogues it installs, and whether `~/.spacecrafter/stars/` is meant to be a search path at all.)

91. **`suntrace pen on` does not aim the trace at the Sun — the pen branch reads the VALUE of a key spelled `sun` where its two siblings pass the literal.** [observed 2026-08-04, scedit sweep unit 1, supervisor-verified at source] `commandSuntrace`'s pen branch calls `coreLink->bodyTraceBodyChange(args[W_SUN])` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:1633]` — `W_SUN` used as a KEY, so the argument is the value of `sun …` on the line — while the `action clear` and `hide` branches pass the literal `W_SUN` (:1646, :1650). Any plausible use (`suntrace pen on`) carries no `sun` key ⇒ the argument is `""` ⇒ `bodyTraceBodyChange` logs `Unknown planet_name` `[observed: protosystem.cpp:468-475]` and the pen lands on whatever body was PREVIOUSLY traced. Rider, same handler: no terminal error message exists (compare `commandBodyTrace` :1625) — `suntrace pen banana` and `suntrace nonsense 3` both report success — and the pen/action/hide branches are not exclusive, so one line may run several. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-04 by the scedit per-handler sweep, unit 1, opus-xhigh executor dispatched by Claude Fable 5; fragment `util/scedit/grammar/args/unit-1.json` [code @ 15927a11], mirror ledger `claude/util/scedit/INTENT.md` journal 2026-08-04b; record-don't-fix: whether `sun <name>` is an undocumented retarget key or a typo for the literal decides OPPOSITE fixes, and scedit's doc line for the key is blocked on the same answer — its `doc` ships `null`+flagged until then. Owed before it is judged: ~~Vixy's reading of the intent~~ **[OWNER CORRECTED 2026-08-04, vixy: script-surface semantics belong to the main user/tester → SCRIPT_SURFACE.md SS-11]**, and whether any shipped show writes `suntrace sun <name>`.)

92. **One script line can kill the app: `dso3d` override path runs `std::stoi` on a raw argument and NOTHING on the execution chain catches.** [observed 2026-08-04, scedit sweep unit 1, supervisor-verified at source] With `maxobject` present and ≠ `"1"`, `commandDso3D` calls `coreLink->dsoNavOverrideCurrent(args["color_tex"], args["alpha_tex"], std::stoi(args["depth"]))` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:1525]`; `depth` absent or non-numeric ⇒ `std::invalid_argument`, and no try/catch exists in scriptModule, interfaceModule, appModule/app.cpp or main `[grep'd 2026-08-04; the only 'try' hits are `directory_iterator` substrings]` ⇒ `std::terminate`. Same class as §5.69's rider (`std::stoi(param[W_KEEPTIME])`, protosystem.cpp:1016) but REACHABLE from the shipped command surface: `dso3d action restart maxobject 2` with no `depth` is the two-key reproduction. Rider, same handler's downstream: `z_reflection` is parsed then DISCARDED (`it->second == "true";` — a statement with no effect, dsoNavigator.cpp:304-306) — §5.35's inert-capability class. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-04 by the scedit per-handler sweep, unit 1; fragment + mirror ledger as §5.91; record-don't-fix: the guard belongs to a POLICY for numeric arguments across the whole command surface — the sweep is enumerating every conversion site right now, and patching one contradicts the class fix. Owed before it is judged: the sweep's complete inventory of unguarded `stoi`/`stod` on the command surface, due when units 2–4 land.) **[INVENTORY DISCHARGED 2026-08-04, units 2–4 landed — the sweep's surfaced set, all supervisor-verified: `dso3d` `std::stoi(args["depth"])` :1525 (this row); `timerate duration` `std::stod` :3317; `media speed`/`speed_increment` `FixedPointI16_2::fromString` :3686/:3754/:3759 (throws by construction, `THROW_ON_FIXED_POINT_ERROR 1`, fixed_point.hpp:12). The sole `catch` in interfaceModule/scriptModule/mainModule/main.cpp is `app_command_eval.cpp:151`, another path — so all four sites terminate the process on a non-numeric or absent value, `media speed $var` included (no `$`-substitution on that path). Basis: the sweep read every handler line for domain extraction; sites beyond these four would be conversions outside the extracted key paths.]**

93. **A failed `color … r g b` line reports success and enters the recording — the error text dies in a by-value parameter.** [observed 2026-08-04, scedit sweep unit 1, supervisor-verified at source] `AppCommandColor::setClassicColor` takes `std::string debug_message` BY VALUE `[observed: app_command_color.hpp:81, .cpp:58]` while the ctor that calls it holds the caller's by REFERENCE (hpp:69, cpp:92); the two range errors (`negative value` :67, `value > 1.` :71) are the ONLY messages it writes, both into the copy, and on those paths `isOkay` stays false with the color unwritten ⇒ `commandColor`'s `if (!testColor) return executeCommandStatus();` `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:1871-1872]` returns with debug_message EMPTY ⇒ success is reported AND the line is recorded. `color property equator_line r 2 g 0 b 0` changes nothing, claims success, and persists into any recording. 94. **`configuration`'s module block falls through into its module-independent block — a star-catalogue save ALSO rewrites `config.ini`, and an unknown module re-runs `App::init()` mid-session before reporting failure.** [observed 2026-08-04, scedit sweep unit 2, supervisor-verified at source over the full extent :2296-2351] The `star_navigator` W_SAVE branch calls `starNavigatorSave` and does NOT return `[observed: app_command_interface.cpp:2330-2331]`; control falls into the `// as a command independent of argModule` block (:2339), whose W_SAVE arm calls `stapp->saveCurrentConfig(...)` — **§5.42's comment-destroying, non-atomic writer** — so `configuration module star_navigator action save name X` performs BOTH saves. `configuration module <unknown> action load` sets "unknown module argument" (:2335), falls through, **re-runs `stapp->init()`** (:2341), and only then reports the failure — the report is true and arrives after the action; with `action save` the destructive save runs and the command then CLAIMS failure. 96. **What a recording CONTAINS diverges from what the author wrote — the recorder persists the executor's internal rewrites, in at least four shapes.** [observed 2026-08-04, scedit sweep units 1/2/3, class row per §5.70's precedent] The recorder records `commandline` at status time (:1167), and handlers rewrite or re-enter before that point: (a) **`set a 1 b 2` records TWICE** — `evalCommandSet` calls `executeCommandStatus()` per pair (:2231), each recording the whole line; and `debug_message` is cleared only per LINE (:194), so one bad name makes every LATER pair report failure and go unrecorded while the earlier ones recorded; (b) **`clear` records as its ~35 nested commands** (:2479-2530, each nested `executeCommand` replacing `commandline`), never as `clear`, and records `zoom auto initial` twice (:2532 runs after the nested calls); (c) **`media` audio-only re-enters `executeCommand`** with a string-concatenated `audio …` line (:3511) — the recording keeps the rebuilt line, a filename with spaces is destroyed by re-tokenization (values are already unquoted), and when `loop` is absent the rebuilt line ends in a dangling key that survives only because pair extraction silently drops it; (d) **`flag` and `timerate increment/…` rewrite `commandline`** to their computed forms (:1192; :3341/:3358/:3374/:3390) — deliberate-looking (record the resulting state), but the same mechanism as (a)-(c) and the reason a replayed show can diverge from an authored one. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-04 by the scedit sweep; record-don't-fix: recording semantics are a product surface (R2 class, §5.70) — whether a recording should hold what was WRITTEN or what was DONE is Vixy's, and (d) suggests both answers already coexist. scedit consequence, already actionable: the editor must never round-trip a script through a recording and call it identity. Owed before it is judged: ~~Vixy's intended~~ the recording contract — **[OWNER CORRECTED 2026-08-04, vixy: the script-surface owner's, → SCRIPT_SURFACE.md SS-12]**.) — **ANNOTATED 2026-08-23 (§11.147).** (a)'s stated MECHANISM is imprecise: `:2121` is `returnValue = returnValue && evalCommandSet(...)`, so after the first failure the later pairs are **not evaluated at all** rather than evaluated-and-reported. Same observable (the line goes unrecorded), different cause — and it is the same short-circuit that makes §5.97's `set home_planet Mars duration 5` inert, which is worth having said once in both places. (d) records the same four lines as §5.76's rider — one facet, two citations, one from each diverged tree. — **ANNOTATED 2026-08-26 (§11.149(e)): UNTOUCHED by the tester's `doc/superscript.sts` rewrite (`f0c8ef83`)** — this row's witnesses are code-side (`AppCommandInterface`), so the rewrite reaches none of them and SS-12's question (*what should a recording contain — what you wrote, or what the engine did?*) is exactly as open as it was. Worth pairing with the fact that DID move: the shipped script the recorder would round-trip is now a different file, so any future recording-vs-source comparison must state which revision it used.
