99. **`b39_star.py`'s illumination criterion cannot report the Moon absent — it counts the whole frame, which the star field dominates.** [measured 2026-08-26 off the instrument's own committed artifacts, §11.149(d6); instrument defect, the §11.101(g)/§11.53(d) criterion-that-cannot-discriminate class] `harness/b39_star.py:67-68` defines `lit(tag)` as *every pixel of the 2048² frame whose max channel exceeds 16*, and the scene it judges runs `flag landscape off` + `flag atmosphere off` with the Moon at fov 30 — so the count is dominated by the star field and the milky-way glow: **15 462 px of background against ~1 850 px of Moon**. When `body name Sun hidden true` parks the whole Sun subtree and the Moon leaves the frame entirely, the number moves 17 302 → 15 462, an 11 % change that the entry read as *"the Moon stays lit"*. There is no body-local region of interest, no off-body background control, and **no state in which the criterion could report the subject absent**; the sibling `dpx()` metric was attributed to the Sun (*"the Sun's own disc and halo leaving the frame"*) when the Sun's own dump record reads `visible: false` in BOTH frames and 95.0 % of the differing pixels lie within 150 px of the Moon's centre. Consequence: **§11.117(k)(1)'s reading is wrong and D37 was opened on it** (corrected §11.149(d5); the counts themselves reproduce exactly). — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F37's premise probe; record-don't-fix: the repair is a disc-local ROI plus an off-body control, and it is only worth anything re-run, which needs a launch — outside a record-only task. The fix must also re-point the entry's claim, never loosen it. Owed before it is re-run: whether any OTHER harness that judges "is this body lit/visible" uses a whole-frame threshold — F37 did not sweep, and the two prior instances of this class were both found one at a time.)

100. **`zoom auto in` starts tracking on the OLD path only — the drawn path does not follow the body, and it zooms to a 0.0023° field pointing away from it.** [measured 2026-08-26, §11.150(k); `harness/f38_gaps.py`, artifacts `harness/artifacts/f38/gaps`] `Core::autoZoomIn` writes `navigation->setFlagTraking(true)` directly [observed: core.cpp:1324] with no `Camera::trackBody` beside it — while the B17 view-offset mirror IS there, two lines below, so the seam was visible at the site. Measured on the shipped command surface: `select planet Mars` then `zoom auto in duration 0` leaves **`old flagTraking = 1` and `camera.tracked = ''`**; old's screen position of Mars becomes exactly **(1024, 1024)** — dead centre of the 2048 render — and stays there across a 0.05 d sidereal advance, while the new path never centres it. The fov mirror (§11.40's `zoomToBothPaths`) DOES reach the Camera, `halfFov` **1.5708 → 0.00002 rad**, so the path that DRAWS ends at a **0.0023°** field aimed 0.2056 rect units off the body: it zooms in on empty sky. This is D15(c)'s own sentence — [vixy] *"it's hard to impossible to properly observe a body while the time continue to tick"* — reached through a different command than the one the answer's recommendation named, and it is the same old-only-write class §11.112(b) found on the sky-lock flag, one flag over. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F38 while mirroring the four sky-lock sites; record-don't-fix, and the reason is specific: the site cannot be routed through `Core::setFlagTracking(true)` the way the sky-lock sites were routed, because that setter uses `getAutoMoveDuration()` where this site passes its own `move_duration` — changing the OLD path's move duration is forbidden by construction (§11.52(b)). The one-line alternative, `Camera::instance->trackBody(ModularBody::findBody(selected_object.getEnglishName()))` beside the `armViewOffset(true)` already at that site, is a user-visible new-path behaviour change outside F38's four-site mandate. **Recommendation carried, decision not taken.** Owed with the fix: the census of the remaining old-only `setFlagTraking` writes — `core.cpp:399` is the init reset and is coherent by accident, since `loadCamera` clears `Camera::target` in the same init.)

101. **`zoom auto initial` returns only the OLD view to the init direction — after a shipped unzoom the two paths look 99° apart.** [measured 2026-08-26, §11.150(k); same instrument] `Core::autoZoomOut` calls `navigation->moveTo(InitViewPos, move_duration, true, -1)` at both of its branches [observed: core.cpp:1430, :1478] with no `Camera::lookTo` counterpart, so the command re-aims the old path and leaves the drawn view wherever it was. Measured with the date frozen so nothing else can move a view: from both paths pointing at Mars (reached through `flag track_object on`, which IS dual, then off), `zoom auto initial duration 0` moves the OLD look direction by **99.0318°** and the NEW one by **0.0214°** — the latter is not a move but the sidereal drift of the 6.366e-05 d the sample pair spans (predicted 0.0230°). Mars's screen position: old **(1024.15, 1024.00) → (278.58, 1868.70)**, new **(0,0) → (0.00031, 1.5e-07)**, i.e. the new path holds the body dead centre through a command whose whole purpose is to leave it. Consequence beyond parity: any harness that uses `zoom auto initial` to reset a scene is resetting ONE path (F38's own `f38_mirror.py` uses it as a state reset, never as a view assertion, for this reason). — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F38; record-don't-fix: mirroring it needs a `Camera::lookTo` in the camera's own convention — the `init_view_pos` frame conversion `SSystemFactory::loadCamera` performs at startup, `(x,y,z)_old → (y,−x,z)_camera` — and the parity of a snapped `lookTo` against old's eased `moveTo` ramp is a question of its own, the §11.92 arming precedent. The fov half of this same command was mirrored by §11.40; the view half was not, and the two were never separated in the record until now.)

102. **The two display-SCALED bodies carry NaN scaled radii while they are being drawn — and on the Moon it reaches the observer's own distance, the altitude readout and the drawn view matrix.** [measured 2026-08-26, §11.150(n); artifacts `harness/artifacts/f38/scenes_pre` and `scenes_post`, identical in both ⇒ **pre-existing, untouched by F38**; reproduced on the bit-reproducible `f0c8ef83` binary (md5 `f1c47308`) and on `b444d381` (md5 `497181da`)] In the A–D scene battery (shipped `[viewing] moon_scale = 5`, `flag_moon_scaled = true`, `sun_scale = 5`, `flag_sun_scaled = false`), **exactly two bodies — `Moon` and `Sun`, exactly the two the display-scaling path touches — dump `boundingRadius = nan`, `scaledDatumRadius = nan`, `scaledGroundRadius = nan` while `visible = true`** and while their own `dist`, `ecl`, `mat` rotation and `screenSize` (1.05882359 for the Moon) are finite. Since all three NaN members are `X * scaling` with three DIFFERENT raw radii [observed: `ModularBody.cpp:511-524`, `updateCache`], the common factor is **`scaling`**, the `ASmooth<AsyncHub, float, 5.f>` member [observed: `ModularBody.hpp:2087`] — which is the failure mode §11.16 already fixed once in this project (*"an ASmooth 0/0 (double set in one tick → permanent NaN)"*). **The consequence is not confined to a bounding volume**: `getAltitudeReference()` returns `scaledDatumRadius` verbatim [observed: `ModularBody.hpp:1440-1442`], so with the Moon as the camera's reference `Camera::moveTo`'s absolute snap (`distance = reference->getAltitudeReference() + pos[2]`) writes **`distance = nan`** — measured in scene C (`set home_planet Moon` + `moveto lat 10 lon 30 alt 100 duration 0`), which then propagates to `rootPos = [nan,nan,nan]`, `refAoI = nan`, `refDist = -nan`, `selDist = -nan`, the **translation of the camera's own dumped view matrix (`-nan,-nan,-nan`)**, `skyBrightness = -nan`, and the control surface's `altitude {reported: nan, new: nan}` beside `old: 100` — i.e. the ALTITUDE READOUT of the path that draws answers NaN on the Moon while the old path answers 100 m. 73 of the 153 records in that dump are affected. Conditions are recorded rather than dressed as a characterization: the NaN is NOT always present — the same two bodies read finite (`Moon 1.17299396e-05`, `Sun 0.00465247268`) in the F38 scenes where they are `visible: false`, and the Sun alone reads NaN in a bare startup dump on the field config (`artifacts/f38/cfg_default/startup.json`) — so what has not been established is the trigger, only that it correlates with the body being updated/drawn and that it survives a full scene drive. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F38 while running the A–D battery as a regression; record-don't-fix, and the addressee is named: this is **D21/F39's own subject** — display scaling as the presentation layer, `ssystem_factory.hpp:556/240/267`, the `SurfacePointOrbitLoader` latch and the 5 s `scaling` ramp — and F39 must not build "grounded children inherit scaling" on a factor that is NaN in the shipped configuration. **[FIXED 2026-08-26 by F39 — §11.152(a), `EntityCore 7ce5835` / code `fd98c8e9`.** Root: `ASmooth::set`'s FRESH-movement branch wrote `duration`, `nextDuration`, `a` and `b` and never `timer`, and the constructor wrote none of them [observed: `ASmooth.hpp:55-60` and `:11-13` at `224eba7`] — so the phase time of the very first movement was INDETERMINATE. Measured by in-binary read-back at `ModularBody::setScaling`: the Moon enters its first call with `timer = -nan`, and the ASmooth's own `operator T()` returns `-nan` **immediately after that first call**, before any retarget and before any tick; the second call (init applies the flag then the value, `core.cpp:618-621`) then NaNs the whole state through `c += timer*(b + timer*a)` — which **also defeats §11.16's `b == 0` guard**, since `b += timer*a*2` is NaN and never 0. So §11.16's *"0/0 on a same-tick retarget"* is NARROWED, not refuted: on this configuration the value is already non-finite one call earlier. **The dating question this row asked is answered and the answer is neither of the two offered**: the defect is ORIGINAL (`ca3205f`, the commit that added the file; only two commits ever touched it) and what varies is the GARBAGE — two runs of the same binary read `a = -2.18e-39` vs `1.12e-08` where only ctor-written `c` is stable, and this row's own conditions (`nan` here, finite `1.16138e-05` there) are two of the THREE outcomes an undefined phase time predicts, the third being the correct `5.80690103e-05`; `1.16138e-05` is not "finite and fine" but the Moon **stuck at scaling 1 with `moon_scale = 5` configured**. Fix: `timer = 0` in the fresh branch — forced by the same `p(t) = c + t*(b + t*a)` the coefficients are solved for, so decision-free — plus class-wide default initializers; the line is load-bearing beyond the first movement, because `update()` clears `mgr` but leaves a completed phase's `timer` in place. Post-fix, same config and driver: the A–D battery's five dumps carry **zero non-finite values** (was 73 of 153 records in scene C) and the Moon reads `scaledDatumRadius = 5.80690103e-05 AU`. The owed discriminating check is discharged with it: the dump now carries `scaling`, `scalingTarget` and `inheritedScaling`.]** Discriminating check owed first: dump `scaling`/`scalingTarget` themselves (neither is in the dump today, which is why this row cannot name the root), and bracket the first NaN in time — startup vs first `setScaling` vs the ramp's first tick. Note the interaction with §5.27/§11.100's `flag moon_scaled off` workaround: turning the scaling off is also the one state in which this cannot fire.)

103. **The dual dump writes bare `nan` / `-nan`, which is not JSON — a single non-finite value makes the whole record unreadable to every consumer.** [observed 2026-08-26, §11.150(n)] `Camera::dumpTrace` and `ModularBody::dumpTrace` stream floats with `operator<<`, which prints `nan`/`-nan`/`inf` in lower case; Python's `json` accepts `NaN`/`Infinity` (capitalised) and rejects these, and a strict JSON reader rejects all of them. Measured consequence, not hypothetical: **`harness/analyze.py` — the A–D battery's analyzer, §11.14/§11.16 — cannot parse a single scene-C dump** (`json.decoder.JSONDecodeError` at the header's `"distance":nan`), and dies on 2 body lines out of 153 in every OTHER scene of the battery because of §5.102's two bodies. So the instrument that certifies body-position parity is currently blocked by a formatting choice, on every scene, and the block is invisible until something goes non-finite. The failure mode is the wrong way round for an instrument: a value that is *the thing you most need to see* is the one that destroys the record containing it. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F38; record-don't-fix here because the fix is a dump-format change (`null` for non-finite, or a quoted token) that every landed analyzer's parser would have to be checked against — `analyze.py`, `quad.py`, `b*_*.py`, `f*_*.py` — and F38 worked around it locally by substituting `null` at read time in its own comparison, which is a workaround and is recorded as one. Note the existing precedent for how to do it, ALREADY IN THIS FILE PAIR: `Navigator::dumpTrace` routes its plan scalars through a `jnum()` helper [observed: `navigator.cpp:500-508`] which emits `"nan"` / `"inf"` as QUOTED tokens, and whose own comment states this row's argument verbatim — *"every consumer of this channel fails on the whole line. The value is PRESERVED as a quoted token rather than nulled, because 'this plan is instantaneous' is exactly the state a restore has to get right."* So the OLD path's half of the same dump line is NaN-safe by a deliberate decision, and the new path's half never adopted it: the fix is to apply an existing local convention, not to invent one.) **[FIXED 2026-08-26 by F39 — §11.152(j), code `7ef11aca`.** `src/experimentalModule/JsonNum.hpp` is that convention as a stream manipulator, applied to every numeric field of `ModularBody::dumpTrace`, `ModularBody::dumpHops` and `Camera::dumpTrace`; the value is preserved as a QUOTED token, because which of NaN/+inf/−inf it was is the finding. **Exact for finite values by construction** — the finite branch is `out << n.v` with the caller's own type, i.e. the same overload and the same stream state, so the concern this row raised (every landed analyzer's parser would have to be re-checked) does not arise: there is no reformatting. Confirmed anyway by the A–D battery run pre and post on one config — five scenes, 152 records each, **zero non-numeric differences** and no field changing shape beyond D21's four additions. The old path keeps its three local copies (navigator/observer/projector), unchanged by construction (§11.52(b)). Reader side closed with it: `harness/dumpread.py` now HOLDS the grammar `b24_equivalence.sanitize_nonfinite` used to hold (re-exported, so all fifteen importers are untouched — one authority, not a fourth copy) and adds `unquote_nonfinite`, so no consumer's arithmetic changes meaning; F38's null-substitute workaround is retired, and a second blockage found in the same reader is fixed with it — `analyze.py` raised `AttributeError` on any body present only in the NEW path, i.e. on every COMPOSED body, because it guarded `rec["new"] is None` and not `rec["old"]`.]**

104. **`body action reload` silently drops EVERY display scaling — a "state-preserving" command that does not preserve `moon_scale`, `sun_scale` or any `planet_scale`.** [measured 2026-08-26, §11.152(i); artifacts `harness/artifacts/f39/d21c` and `d21rare`, identical on BOTH binaries (`487cf096` and `3cbab8c5`) ⇒ pre-existing and untouched by F39] On the shipped config (`moon_scale = 5`, `flag_moon_scaled = true`), a single `body action reload` takes the Moon from `scaling = 5` / `scaledDatumRadius = 8687.00 km` to **`scaling = 1` / `1737.40 km`**, and it stays there: nothing re-applies the value. Source: `SSystemFactory::reloadCurrentSystem` captures the reference / tracked / selected body NAMES before the rebuild and re-seats them afterwards [observed: `ssystem_factory.cpp:812-850`], but the display scales are applied only at `Core::init` [observed: `core.cpp:618-621`] and by the `moon_scale` / `sun_scale` / `planet_scale` commands, so the rebuilt bodies come back at the constructor's `scaling(1)`. The header's own contract for this command is state preservation, and the observer's own altitude reference moves 6949.6 km with it (`getAltitudeReference()` returns `scaledDatumRadius`), so this is not confined to how big the Moon looks. **This is also what REFUTES §11.101(f)'s untested prediction** — the prediction was that startup-loaded and reload-loaded scenes latch different points of the 5 s ramp so a reload should MOVE a composed rover; measured, the rover does not move, because BOTH ends latch scaling 1 (startup builds the system at `core.cpp:101`, before the config block; the reload never re-applies anything at all). — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F39 while confirming that prediction; record-don't-fix: the reload/state-preservation seam is B16's, and WHAT should be re-applied — the config values, the D30 authored-override ledger, or both, and in which order relative to the ramp — is a design question rather than a repair. Owed with the fix: the same probe for every other seam `Core::init` sets and `reloadSystem` rebuilds.) **[ANSWERED-in-design [vixy 2026-08-26 → §11.154(b)]: ownership FORMAT-SCOPED — legacy: config.ini owns scaling (reload re-applies it [derived, veto]); modular: the new FILE owns it, deprecating config.ini whenever the new format serves the solar system (reload re-applies by construction, D31). Implementation rides B16's seam; ONE GATE: B28 sign-off on the new-format scaling key before any emitter writes it. The owed every-other-seam probe STAYS — the ruling covers scaling only.]** **[OPERATED [vixy 2026-08-26 → §11.154(c)]: derivation ratified verbatim (*"your derivation is exact - you can operate it"*); key name **`display_scale`** proposed WITH its delegated deducibility test (*"if it doesn't hold, it's not the right name"*) — the B28 gate closes conditionally at F41's check. Twin must emit it (reproduce-legacy contract, §11.113(f) precedent). Implementation = F41.]**

105. **The OLD path dumps uninitialized memory as `dist` for the bodies it never sorts — 8–9 bodies per battery dump, values differing run to run.** [measured 2026-08-26, §11.152(o); artifacts `harness/artifacts/f39/scenes_pre|scenes_post/gen_a.json`] `Goldilocks_Zone`, `Haumea`, `Juno`, `Makemake`, `Pallas`, `Pluto`, `Sedna` (+ `Hiiaka` in one leg) dump `old.dist` as denormals ≈ **4.6355830e-310**, and `Vesta` as **3.94156092e+180** in one leg, while the SAME bodies' new-path `dist` is finite (Vesta 2.90038562). The values differ per body, per run and per leg, and the affected SET differs between legs — uninitialized, not a computed value. Source: `double distance;` declared with no initializer [observed: `body.hpp:630`, its own comment calling it *"a temporary variable used to store the distance to a given point … for sorting while drawing"*], streamed verbatim by `Body::dumpTrace` [observed: `body.cpp:1365`]. Today's measured consequence is instrument-grade — `analyze.py`'s `dDist` column is garbage for those bodies on every A–D battery run, i.e. the parity instrument reports a number nothing produced. — **OPEN** (found 2026-08-26 by F39 while comparing two battery legs field-by-field; record-don't-fix — the old path is unchanged by construction, §11.52(b), and this is retire-with-old class. **One thing is OWED before anyone acts**: whether `Body::getDistance()` [observed: `body.hpp:254`] is read anywhere outside the dump for a body that was never sorted this frame, which would move this from an instrument defect to a live one.)

106. **Entering free flight changes what the ENVIRONMENT draws, not only where the observer stands — the landscape stops updating and the atmosphere floods the dome.** [measured 2026-08-26, §11.153(l); `harness/f40_env.py`, artifacts `harness/artifacts/f40/env_pre|env_post`, BOTH binaries `89d66cd3` and `98d09488` ⇒ pre-existing and untouched by F40] At the shipped place (43°18′N 5°22′E, 75 m) one `camera action free_mode state on` changes **3 300 632 px>8** of a 2048×2048 frame — 79 % of it — **while the observer moves 0.241 m**. Four-cell measurement, each cell with its own adjacent A/A floor: atmosphere off + landscape off **0 px** (post) — that cell is the converter's and §11.153 zeroed it; landscape ON **543 393 px** (lit 806 800 → 287 893: the landscape leaves); atmosphere ON **3 300 686 px** (lit 384 061 → **3 300 843**: the dome saturates). Mechanism, at source and deliberate: `onBody = !camera.isFreeMode() && !reference->isSystem()` `[observed: EnvironmentManager.cpp:81]`, whose own comment reads *"system references and free flight are the old anchor-point case"* — the anchor branch skips `reference->groundedEnvironment`, where `LandscapeEnv` (landscape + fog) lives `[observed: LandscapeEnv.hpp:8]`, and updates the atmosphere member with `pos = (0,0,getDistanceToObserver())` instead of the observer's own radius `[observed: EnvironmentManager.cpp:99-105]`. **This also REFUTES §11.144(i)'s reading** of the same pixels as the free-mode teleport's witness (*"day into night"*): the teleport is gone and the pixels are not. — **OPEN (record-don't-fix, and the reason is the row's own class: it is a port of the old path's `isOnBody()` semantics, so what free flight should SHOW is user-visible semantics — Vixy's, and adjacent to D15(b)/§11.151(a), whose *"brutal changes are disorienting"* covers a 79 %-of-frame change at least as well as it covered the position channel. Owed before it is judged: whether the atmosphere's anchor-branch flood is the intended anchor-point appearance or a second defect inside this one — the two cells separate them and this row does not.)** **[DESIGN INPUT [vixy 2026-08-26 → §11.154(a)]: the mode switch serves INTERACTIVE navigation (compounding deltas corrected by the human loop); scripts don't use free movement. De-urgents this row (interactive-only path). Veto point held open: if the ruling was meant to CLOSE it as accepted old-path behaviour, one sentence flips it; the atmosphere-flood sub-question stays owed either way.]**

## 6. Decisions — open and resolved (resolved entries keep their answer)

1. **Priority ladder naming** — **RESOLVED** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: `ResourcePriority` naming wins; legacy `NOW` renamed to `ACTIVE`; strategy.txt updated (verified in tree).
2. **Inline-in-header policy** — **OPEN, context added**: headers are the thinking medium [vixy: 2026-07-11], so implementation-in-header is partly drafting state; G10 justifies keeping *hot* paths inline. Still to decide: which inlines are load-bearing (stay) vs drafting residue (migrate to .cpp once parity is proven).
3. **`BodyType` residual role** — **DERIVED (2026-07-19, per §2(a2)'s gates-vs-parameters test — recommendation recorded, enum surgery awaits Vixy since the enum is header vocabulary)**: `MINOR_BODY` justified [vixy: 2026-07-11] AND consumer-confirmed — 3 capability sites (shadow exemption, ModularSystem.cpp:154/208/237). **SPHERICAL_BODY and SINGLE_BODY have ZERO consumers and ZERO producers** [measured: grep 2026-07-19 — `strToBodyType` never assigns them (Asteroid/KBO/Comet→MINOR, all named types→CUSTOM_BODY, ModularSystem.cpp:40-55); no site tests them]. Under §2(a2): SPHERICAL's "doesn't use self-shadowing" duplicates what module traits already express (BMT_* absence is the authority — I2 violation as a second home); SINGLE/CUSTOM's "single vs multiple shapes" duplicates the components list itself. All three are identity declarations, not capabilities → **recommendation: collapse to ONE default value** (keep the `CUSTOM_BODY` spelling or rename `DEFAULT`); the oblateness-fast-path speculation found no code basis (oblateness rides `getOneMinusOblateness()` per body, type-untested).
4. **Taskable / cross-thread handoff** — **ARCHITECTURE CONVERGED [vixy: 2026-07-17]**; §8's v2 shape confirmed at its load-bearing point. Vixy's statement, verbatim: *"the single chain is my intent for the finaliser/integration part, that is integrating the fully-processed part into the scene so that it can be rendered without race."* What this confirms, precisely (do not over-read it): (i) **§8.2.5's single-Taskable granularity is INTENT, not a chosen tradeoff** — so its consequences stand on intent now: non-atomic pins, the forced hand-off rule, application-lifetime Taskables, `TASKABLE_REFERENCE_COUNT`/`KEEP_REFERENCE` off; (ii) **§8.2.3's two protection domains are confirmed by the words "fully-processed"** — what reaches the chain is already finished, so §8.4's precondition 2 (integration tasks are O(small)) is *definitional*, not merely hoped: a chain task that does hard work violates the intent, not just a budget; (iii) the chain's role is **finalisation/integration into the scene, race-free** — i.e. C1 is discharged by chain serialisation as §8.2.1 states. **Still open (implementation, not architecture)**: §8.5's parameters (pool sizing, VRAM ladder + degradation values, preservationCycles, attachment-list mechanism, s_texture/registry dedup overlap, the Task memory-validity flag candidate); §8.2.10's screen-capture/video-render integration item (flagged, never designed); §8.6(f)'s KEEP_REFERENCE refcount underflow — **stays MOOT under single-chain (now intent-backed) but remains latent**, with §8.2.5's reopening trigger (sustained publish volume contending with frame-task latency → per-body split → the flag returns → (f) must be fixed first). **S4 is architecturally unblocked**; the frame-task-rides-the-same-chain half was already [vixy: 2026-07-11].
5. **EnvironmentModule scope** — **RESOLVED** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: everything camera-location-relative "outside" — milkyway 2D(earth-centered)/3D, atmosphere, landscape, etc. (§3.8). ~~Open remainder: the module's interface~~ implemented 2026-07-16 (§11.27).
6. **Milkyway root lifetime** — **RESOLVED (2026-07-17, §11.36)**: the universe root exists (`unique_ptr<ModularSystem> universe`, factory-owned, declared before the camera for destruction order); every node now has a parent for remnant-ModularBodyPtr delegation and the **universe is the single eternal node** (redirect-to-nullptr only at final teardown — ModularBodyPtr::redirect null-guarded for exactly that). The milkyway is an ordinary INNER child of the universe; MilkyWayEnv rides it unchanged.

8. **Orientation convention (P5)** — **RESOLVED [vixy 2026-07-17] + IMPLEMENTED/VERIFIED (§11.35)**: render tilt routed through the accumulated `accumulatedBodyPosToBody` frame — option (A), accumulated + precessed at both consumers, one authority for the body's equatorial frame; (B) closed, not deferred. Full record (premise, precondition resolutions, the refuted 7-body-closure claim) → INTENT/archive/6.8.md
7. **`deduceBodyModuleList` coverage** — **RESOLVED** [vixy, plan phase 2026-07-11]: deduction extended per ported family (existing .ini files keep working) PLUS explicit module/slot declaration params for overrides and customs; param syntax lands with the first multi-module body (D2). Contract documented on `loadModule`.
9. **Executor-mode dissolution — DIRECTION RESOLVED [vixy: 2026-07-17, on the §11.36 report], implementation open.** The modes (inSolarSystem/inGalaxy/inUniverse) are **not kept by the new path — they must be emergent through the system nesting** (G2 chain membership), *"unified and still doing what they were doing as efficiently as they did"*. This is the stated intent behind EnvironmentModule's existence — *"part of the reason why EnvironmentModule exists, although not limited to it"* — i.e. the environment members carry the camera-location-relative slice, and the rest of the mode content becomes tree content. **Tully shall be handled as well** [vixy, explicit].
   - **Mode-content inventory (the migration surface; observed in the three executor modules)**: solar mode: `oort` (altitude-gated draw, solarSystemModule:154/196) · inGalaxy: `milky_way` 3D regime, `dso3d`, `starLines`, `starNav`, `dsoNav`, `cloudNav`, `ojmMgr(IN_GALAXY)` · inUniverse: `tully`, `starGalaxy`, `dsoNav`, `ojmMgr(IN_UNIVERSE)` · mode-independent overlays riding every mode (VR360, skyDisplayMgr person) — presumably stay frame-level, not tree content. `OjmMgr::STATE_POSITION` is itself a mode-keyed selector — same dissolution applies (OJM content attached at its floor).
   - **The mapping rule [vixy]**: mode content becomes **ModularBody instances at their floor level** — oort at/around the SolarSystem node (*"the oort and maybe some other might be harder to implement, though, yet they shall be implemented as modular body ideally, at different floor level maybe"*), dso3d/3D-milkyway-class content at the MilkyWay node, tully at the Universe node — with camera-location-relative rendering as EnvironmentModule members (the milkyway 2D/3D switch is already designed as exactly that, §3.8).
   - **Efficiency parity is a requirement, not a hope**: the old mode dispatch was also a work gate (only the active mode's content updates/draws). The nesting must reproduce it structurally: G4 gating applied at system-node level — content attached at a floor updates/draws only when its floor is in the active chain / visibly resolved. The §11.36 skeleton already carries the shape (drawSystemBodies gates nested systems at 16px; chain membership gates environments).
   - **What §11.36 already provides**: update-half mode independence (updateExperimental from Executor::update — the camera/chain lives in every mode); chain transitions Earth↔Universe verified both directions; the nested-draw dispatch + star proxy (skeleton, runtime-unexercised). ~~**What remains**: draw-half mode independence (`ssystemFactory->draw` is still called only by the solar/stellar modules — the draw twin of the §11.36 update defect);~~ **draw-half DONE 2026-07-23 (§11.80, code `14931a18`): `drawExperimental()` called from the inGalaxy/inUniverse modules, drawNested/drawStarProxy live + verified (0-vs-nonzero counterfactual).** What remains: the content migration itself (point-cloud/instanced module families for oort/dso3d/tully — the row-15 VOLUMETRIC slot and the MINOR_BODY/cluster traits are the reserved carriers, choice open); ojmMgr state dissolution; per-floor efficiency verification; the old-content/new-dot double-representation observed live (starNav's Sol vs the proxy dot, §11.80) resolves with the content migration.
   - **Horizon note vs §11.33's spec-8 gap list**: tully/dsoNavigator/etc. must render correctly under all projection modes NOW (§11.33 resolution stands — they live until absorbed), AND their content migrates into the unified path LATER (this entry). Two horizons, both real; neither cancels the other.

## 8. Threading / handoff strategy — PROPOSAL v2 (2026-07-11, pending Vixy convergence)

Successor to the strategy.txt task-at-draw sketch (confirmed best-found-at-the-time [vixy: 2026-07-11]). v1 proposed a single global completion queue; the EntityCore/Executor inventory (§8.6) found that Vixy's in-progress `Taskable` primitive [observed: EntityCore/Executor/Taskable.hpp, written 2026-07-11] provides strict per-body ordering [vixy: 2026-07-11 — named requirement] and a *better* resolution of the MPSC transient-window than v1's defer-retry. v2 is the synthesis. Once converged and implemented, the authoritative home of each contract becomes the header of the component owning it; this section then reduces to a pointer.

### 8.1 Constraints it must satisfy
C1 no loading↔drawing race · C2 no body destruction with pending tasks · C3 draw thread never blocks · C4 loader threads may block · strict per-body task ordering · burst absorption without idle-case cost (D7) · shared-resource dedup + fan-out (D7) · minimal overhead · D5 hardware ranges · D6 texture/model asymmetry (all [vixy: 2026-07-11]).

### 8.2 Shape: per-body Taskable chains + global ready-list, boundary-localized lock-freedom
Lock-free machinery exists **only** where the render thread meets producers; loader-side internals may block (C4 — INT-2: complexity lives where the binding boundary is).

1. **The render chain is an "effective thread"** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: draw-visible state is protected by one render-side `Taskable`; everything touching that state runs as a Task on its chain. Sequential consistency comes from the Taskable ordering promise, **not from thread identity** — rendering itself may occasionally execute on a worker or events thread, and that is correct by design. C1 = chain serialization; per-body publish ordering is automatic (a single FIFO chain preserves per-key order). The chain **may and should go idle** — see the stack note in 4.
2. **Main loop** [vixy: 2026-07-11, corrected 2nd pass]: a pure cadencer at the target framerate — `execute()`s the **frame task** onto the render chain (inplace when idle, lowest latency; chained behind pending publishes otherwise) and waits until the next tick. **Update+draw live inside the frame task itself** — one Task, scheduled/executed every frame [vixy]: the residual synchronous update part is cheap, cache-local, and uses the exact elapsed time in non-recording mode.
3. **Two protection domains** `[inferred — effectively confirmed by the fence discipline below]`: work tasks/chains do the hard work and the async sequencing (completion events: the chain waits, no thread waits) and **never touch draw-visible state**; their final step schedules a lightweight subscribe/finalize/publish task onto the render chain [vixy: 2026-07-11 — "tasks on the Taskable for rendering only subscribe/finalize the render-thread-side part, not doing hard work"]. **Fence discipline** [vixy: 2026-07-11, 2nd pass]: fence waits happen as late as possible, only where the touched resource requires them, and **only the rendering part ever waits on a fence** — with multiple frames in flight, the acquire/present semaphore structure makes an actual block genuinely unlikely; the fence is the guarantee, not the expected wait. The separation restated with this: long holds belong to the work domain; the render chain carries only late, rarely-blocking guarantees. Starvation-free: publish execution is independent of whether the body is drawn (closes the D3 channel of the at-draw sketch).
4. **Cost profile**: idle = zero — no drain point exists; the render chain sleeps between frame tasks. Per completion = the work chain's exchanges + one publish push. Bursts chain behind the frame task and execute between frames as O(N) lightweight publishes (heavy work — decode, build, upload — already done in the work domain; a publish is a pointer/descriptor swap, O(small) by contract). **Stack note** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: synchronous `endTask` cascades nest on the stack (unless tail-call optimization applies), so chains going idle is *preferable* — it resets the depth; bounded bursts (D7) are the sizing assumption.
5. **Lifetime (C2) under the granularity decision** [vixy: 2026-07-11, 2nd pass]: synchronization goes through a **single Taskable** — the render chain — likely sufficient granularity, and cheaply revisable if it turns out wrong, thanks to how Task/Taskable compose [vixy]. **All Taskables live for the whole application duration** → `TASKABLE_REFERENCE_COUNT`/`TASKABLE_TASK_KEEP_REFERENCE` stay off. Two consequences:
   - **Pins need no atomics at all**: pin increments (request emission, inside the frame task) and decrements (publish/finalization tasks) both execute within the effective thread — a plain integer under chain serialization.
   - **The hand-off rule stops being discipline and becomes forced**: with a non-atomic pin, worker code *cannot* legally release — the only path is transferring the hold into the publish task. The earlier `[inferred]` rule is now enforced by the type, not by convention.
   `remove()` on a pinned body detaches from the tree immediately (world-consistent, ModularBodyPtr::redirect philosophy); memory follows when the pin reaches zero inside the chain. Reopening trigger (membership, not magnitude): sustained publish volume contending with frame-task latency → split to per-body chains — KEEP_REFERENCE returns, and §8.6(f) must be fixed first.
   - Pin counter deliberately **not** merged with render-local `pointerCount` (different synchronization domains; merging forces atomics onto the render-local path — I2: one info, one domain).
   - Module replacement during pending load needs no pin: integration validates `body->slot(id) == module` at drain — render-thread-local, race-free by construction, drops stale payloads.
6. **Requests, priorities, cancellation**: `AsyncBuilder`-style atomic priority field is the existing pattern ("prioritize on activation" = one atomic store); workers re-read at pop and re-check before expensive phases (drops uploads for bodies gone halo-only). Cancelled tasks still schedule a payload-less completion for the unpin. The `LoadPriority` fused state+priority encoding (one atomic carries DONE/COMPLETED/LOADING/LAZY→NOW) is kept — one-word lifecycle — but needs a header comment and the NOW→ACTIVE rename for consistency.
7. **Shared resources (D7)**: the loadable unit is a refcounted resource object (`AsyncBuilder::useCount` pattern exists). Dedup by resource identity at request time (render-side registry; s_texture already dedups by name — verify overlap at plan time). On completion, fan-out = schedule one integration task per attached (body, module) — each push lock-free, per-body order preserved. Attachment-list synchronization: either a small loader-side mutex (legal, C4) or the resource itself as a `Taskable` in `execute` mode (attachments serialized as tasks, no lock) — plan-mode choice.
8. **Texture residency (D6)**: `gc.hpp` is the existing eviction machinery — cycle-based grace (`preservationCycles`) is the hysteresis; `used()` at draw is the relevance mark. Eviction decisions stay render-side; VkImage destruction deferred by frames-in-flight. VRAM budget = D5 parameter with degradation ladder. Models: same pipeline, no eviction path (D6). `gc` cross-thread sync has a known open point (`deletable` non-atomic, flagged in-source) — verify at plan time.
9. **Task semantics — the contract** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: strict ordering applies **only inside `[start(), endTask())`**; a task's `start()` returning does *not* mean the task is done — `endTask` being called on it does; a task must hold nothing with side effects past its `endTask`; outside the window, no guarantee. Tasks/Taskables compose (a Task may hold another Task which calls the enclosing task's `endTask`).
   → **Asynchronous chain holds**: a task may start async work (GPU transfer, fence) and call `endTask` later from wherever completion fires — the *chain* waits, no *thread* waits. This is the natural carrier for the upload→integrate sequence with ordering preserved.
   → ~~Render-hop pattern~~ **SUPERSEDED same day** by the effective-thread model (§8.2.1/3): no thread affinity exists to restore — cross-thread continuation is correct because protection is per-chain, not per-thread; the "hop" is simply the work chain's natural final step of publishing onto the render chain. Kept as a record of the wrong frame (affinity-thinking) it came from.
10. **Thread model** [vixy: 2026-07-11, extended 2nd pass] (concretizes the draft inventory at ModularBody.hpp:131-137): 1 video-player thread (video frame loading) · 1 main thread as cadencer (frame-task `execute` at target framerate, opportunistic inplace execution) · 1 events/scripts thread (SDL event polling, scripts, non-recurrent actions: body building — heavy part lazy — with attachment published as a render-chain Task, orbits/hints display, detach/remove, move, selection) · 1 compute thread (shadow projection, owns a compute VkQueue) [initially forgotten — vixy] · 1 worker executing **only ACTIVE-priority** tasks (a guaranteed free lane for the "needed now" class — reactivity) · nproc−3 generic workers (default, configurable) executing all tasks. The dedicated threads are rarely stressed simultaneously (< 3 active at a time in real-world use) and don't compete with other programs → REALTIME FIFO scheduling shouldn't hurt latency by design [vixy]; the "< 3 active" property is the load-bearing claim, not the exact thread count. **Open integration item** [vixy, initially forgotten]: screen capture for video rendering must fit the task system — `[inferred sketch]` readback rides the render chain after the frame task, encode is worker-domain, file write on the capture path's own ordering.

### 8.3 Rejected alternatives (with rejecting precondition)
- Per-body mutex: C3 + D5-floor (preempted holder stalls render a scheduling quantum); unconditional hot-path cost.
- Task execution at the owning body's draw (strategy.txt sketch): D3 starvation — halo-only bodies never draw → tasks never run → pin/VRAM leak. Its ordering property is retained via Taskable; its execution trigger is replaced by the ready-list drain.
- v1 single global completion queue (2026-07-11, same day): no per-body ordering guarantee across producers (two loaders finishing LoD1/LoD2 of one body can interleave); transient-window handled by defer-retry (one frame latency) where Taskable's marker-exchange resolves it with zero latency and exactly-once continuation. Superseded by §8.2.
- v2.1 never-idle render chain (2026-07-11, same day): a self-rescheduling frame task keeping the chain permanently busy bought "affinity by construction" — at the price of **unbounded stack growth**: synchronous `endTask` cascades nest, and a chain that never ends never resets the depth (unless TCO) [vixy: 2026-07-11]. The stack was the uncounted resource in the "by construction" reasoning. Superseded by the effective-thread model, where idle chains are correct (inplace execution is serialized by the chain promise, no affinity needed) and going idle resets the stack.
- Per-module atomic payload slots: O(visible) per-frame checks for rare events; check cost dominates event cost.
- shared_ptr lifetime: G8. · Generation counters + indirection: same guarantee as pins, more machinery. · Blocking flush on removal: blocks whichever thread removes.

### 8.4 Preconditions (design invalid if violated)
1. **All draw-visible state mutation happens through Tasks on the render chain** — reformulated 2026-07-11 from "on the render thread": the effective-thread model makes physical thread identity irrelevant; what must hold is that no draw-visible state is touched outside the chain. Scripts/events build offline and publish via chain Tasks (e.g. body attachment) — **VERIFIED as the intended management path** [vixy: 2026-07-11].
2. Integration tasks are O(small); violations show as frame spikes at drain (measurable, attributable).
3. Loaders read bodies only via pinned references; mutable inputs are snapshotted into the request at emission (render-side).

### 8.5 Open parameters (plan-mode decisions, not architecture)
Pool sizing policy over 4–64 cores · VRAM budget detection + degradation ladder values · `preservationCycles`/hysteresis values · attachment-list mechanism (mutex vs resource-as-Taskable) · overlap between s_texture dedup and the resource registry · **Task memory-validity flag candidate** (per §8.6.d "maybe worth a new flag"): dual-hold count on Task — init 2 (execution + linkage); `endTask` releases the execution hold (both on CAS-success, since no successor can then exist); the successor's producer releases the linkage hold after its `next.exchange`; at 0 → `recycle()`. Under ifdef, zero overhead when the external guarantee (task embedded in refcounted resource object) is used instead.

### 8.6 EntityCore/Executor inventory (2026-07-11) — reuse map + draft defects
**Reusable as-is or with port**: `AsyncLoaderMgr` (worker pools, BigSave native cache, O_DIRECT once-reads, priority scan, minPriority gate) — its main-thread `update()` pump (O(all-pending) scan per frame, poll not push — I3) is what the ready-list drain replaces; `AsyncBuilder` (refcount + atomic priority + two-phase asyncLoad/postLoad = the resource object pattern); `gc` (eviction/grace); `Taskable`/`Task` (the ordering primitive, in-progress).
**Defects found in the 2026-07-11 `Taskable.hpp` draft** (statuses per Vixy review, 2026-07-11):
- (a) Local shadows member: **RESOLVED on disk** — `execute()` renamed by Vixy; `scheduleExecution()` applied by Claude (2026-07-11, authorized). GCC behavior clarified [vixy]: the new local wins (consistent with the standard point-of-declaration reading; earlier divergence note was a misparse on my side — dissolved).
- (b) Missing memory edge on the marker handoff: **RESOLVED on disk** — producer half (`acq_rel` lines 21/32) by Vixy; consumer half (`endTask` marker exchange → `acq_rel`) applied by Claude (2026-07-11, authorized).
- (c) `Task::start` undefined → link error: **FIXED by Vixy** (now pure virtual; verified on disk 2026-07-11).
- **Contract comments written** (2026-07-11, per Vixy's "specify the promises where they weren't made explicit"): Taskable.hpp carries the full promise block (effective-thread framing, ordering window, completion = endTask, no-affinity, memory validity/marker window, scheduleExecution == false meaning, stack-nesting caution); Task.hpp carries the completion-semantics pointer.
- (d) Node reclamation: **RESOLVED as contract, promises to be written into the headers** [vixy: 2026-07-11 — "definitely worth specifying the promises where they weren't made explicit"]: ordering guarantee only within `[start, endTask)`; task holds nothing with side effects past `endTask`; lifetime via `TASKABLE_TASK_KEEP_REFERENCE` or an external guarantee (ifdef = zero overhead when external); not pooling. Memory residual **confirmed as real and hard to determine externally** [vixy] — a Task's storage must stay valid until successor-linked or chain-empty (the successor's producer may write `task->next` after `endTask` returned); "maybe worth a new flag" [vixy] → candidate design in §8.5.
- (e) `delete this` on releasing thread: **RESOLVED BY STRUCTURE** [vixy: 2026-07-11] — see §8.2.5: lightweight render-chain finalization tasks carry the releases, so the last release lands on the render thread by construction; the checkable hand-off rule is `[inferred — pending convergence]`.
- (f) `TASKABLE_TASK_KEEP_REFERENCE` acquire/release asymmetry (found 2026-07-11, second pass): `execute()`'s empty-chain branch acquires but `scheduleExecution()`'s does not, while `endTask`'s CAS-success releases unconditionally → scheduled-mode chain start with both ifdefs on underflows the refcount → premature delete. **MOOT under the chosen granularity** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: single-chain synchronization, all Taskables application-lifetime, flags off. Remains **latent** — must be fixed before any per-body-chain split enables the flag (reopening trigger in §8.2.5).
**Defects in existing `AsyncLoaderMgr` (pre-dating, lower priority — the port supersedes them)**: `addLoad`/`addBuild` `push_front` without holding the list spinlock while workers iterate under it (list-head data race); `AsyncLoader::priority` is plain (non-atomic) but written by worker and main threads (`AsyncBuilder::priority` is atomic — the asymmetry looks unintended); `update()`'s lock-busy branch iterates the list without the lock while a worker may scan it.

## 9. SSystemFactory seam table (Phase C, 2026-07-11)

The external world reaches the body system exclusively through `SSystemFactory` (callers: coreLink.cpp, core.cpp, executorModule). Per category, where each call routes when the new path is active (`drawModularSystem`). "dual" = already routed to both paths today.

| Category | Old-surface examples | New-path route |
|---|---|---|
| Selection/search | setSelected, searchByNamesI18, searchAround, listMatchingObjectsI18n | `ModularBody::findBody*` + `ModularBodySelector` + `ModularObject` (B7 surface); pointer draw via ObjectBase::drawPointer over ModularObject (D2) |
| Queries | getSelectedAZ/ALT/RA/DE, getPlanetsPosition, getSunAltitude/Azimuth | `ModularObject::getAltAz/getRaDeValue` (Camera-conversion idiom); az convention verify at D2 |
| Scale/size | setScale, setPlanetSizeScale, set/getMoonScale, setSizeLimit | `ModularBody::setScaling` (ASmooth) + global params; moon/sun scale = hardcoded-body scaling; **setPlanetSizeScale now DUAL (T7 §11.45 — `planet_scale` was the §11.35 seam gap, now `findBody→setScaling`)** |
| Color | setBodyColor/getBodyColor, setDefaultBodyColor | **RUNTIME SETTERS DUAL (2026-07-22, §11.65, B29)**: per-body recolor (halo=body, label/orbit/trail=modules, self-select I4) + `"all"` broadcast + runtime DEFAULT (module statics) mirror to the new path; per-instance storage + broadcast override (§11.48(b)); getter old-authority pre-switchover (§11.46). Runtime colour PERSISTENCE across `body action reload` = B16 §11.55(i)'s suspended body-scoped-override decision, NOT closed here (old has no reload ⇒ no observable to reproduce; new rebuilds-from-file resets, measured) |
| Flags | setFlagHints/Axis/Trails/Orbits/Clouds, setPlanetHidden, toggleHideSatellites | module static `show` flags (HintModule etc.) + `ModularBody::hide/show`; per-name orbit flag = per-body module toggle |
| Textures | switchPlanetTexMap, createTexSkin, planetTesselation | **ALL DUAL (2026-07-19, §11.46)**: skin pair = `BodyModule::createTexSkin/switchTexSkin` broadcast (MESH modules consume, old tex_current parity) + factory mirrors; tesselation was ALREADY dual by shared `BodyTesselation` object (row 2 §11.26 — the T7 audit row claiming OLD-ONLY was wrong, correction at §11.45) |
| Body add/remove | addBody, preloadBody, removeBody, removeSupplementalBodies | `ModularSystem::loadBody` (dual for removeBody already); preload = `ModularBody::preload`. **New-path-only addition (2026-07-21, §11.55): `body action reload` → `SSystemFactory::reloadCurrentSystem` → `ModularSystem::reloadSystem`** — rebuild the current system from its data file, observer state kept; no old-path counterpart |
| Anchors/camera | cameraMoveToPoint/Body, transitionTo*, alignCameraToBody, saveCameraPosition, switchToAnchor | `Camera` (motion/reference layer, live) + `CameraAnchors` sketch (named/persistence/scripted layer — second pass); **bookmark fov (position save/load) now DUAL (T7 §11.45 — CoreBackup::loadBackup mirrors Camera::setHalfFov)**; ~~view_offset/zoom_offset still OLD-ONLY — B17 CHARACTERISED + SUSPENDED (§11.63): a Navigator/VIEW fov-coupled rotation (not a projector offset), the new path has no projection-stage hook; projection-space-vs-parity is Vixy's~~ **view_offset now DUAL (B17 LANDED 2026-07-24, §11.92 — D6 option (1), render-only Camera pitch; line de-staled §11.102(f); two enumeration residuals §11.102(b): fifth arm site + query-channel skew)** |
| Per-frame | computePositions, computePreDraw, draw, update | frame task on the render chain (D1): update+draw fused; computePreDraw's buckets → Renderer depth partitioning (D4) |
| System transitions | enterSystem/leaveSystem, changeSystem | `Camera::switchToBody`/`warpToBody` + `ModularSystem::systemOf` |
| Tools | bodyTrace pen (upPen/downPen/togglePen) | stays a coreModule-level tool (not a body-render concern) |
| Fonts | registerFont | `HintModule::setFont` at the seam |

## 10. Renderer pipeline-family API — requirements (2026-07-11) + design (§10.3)

D2's blocker. §10.1/10.2 are observed need, not invented design; the design itself now exists (§10.3 + `PipelineFamily.hpp` + the registry block in `Renderer.hpp`, 2026-07-11) — headers written, implementation deliberately absent, pending Vixy convergence. B3's contract already fixes the frame: modules DESCRIBE (traits + descriptor contract), the Renderer EXECUTES (owns every drawState).

### 10.1 What it must manage
- **Pipeline families** replacing `SHADER_USE`/`selectShader` [observed: bodyShader.hpp:41-60]: mesh-layered (the hard one: night/clouds/specular/bump/tesselation feature COMBINATIONS — the old encoded them as enum entries + `pipelineOffset` variants [observed: body_bigbody.hpp:92]), OJM/model (± shadowed), ring (+ instanced-asteroid + depth-trace variants), lines (orbit/trail/axis/grid — depth-tested and depth-free variants), screen-space 2D (hint circle, pointer), big-halo/star, translucent shell (AtmExt), tail.
- **Per-pass variants**: a family must provide its variant per pass kind it participates in — COLOR, NO-DEPTH, SELF-SHADOW, SHADOW-STENCIL, TRACE [BodyModule.hpp hooks] — keyed at least by (family, pass, feature-variant). Old path had explicit SHADOW/DEPTH_TRACE drawStates [observed: bodyShader.hpp].
- **Render-pass compatibility**: pipelines are bound to FrameMgr passes (`PASS_MULTISAMPLE_DEPTH` etc. [observed: Renderer.cpp beginDraw; body.cpp:1036-1068]); self-shadow targets variable-size attachments (8192 main / 2048 secondary [ModularBody.hpp constants]) → dynamic viewport or per-resolution builds.
- **Descriptor machinery**: set layouts per family; set allocation for modules (BasicMesh owns an EntityCore `Set` [observed: BasicMesh.hpp]); big-texture rebind generations (`bigTexRecap::binding` [observed: s_texture.hpp:97-108], old `bigSet` lazy rebuild [observed: body_bigbody.cpp:445-478]); **descriptor POOL sizing per family needs** — live evidence this is currently broken: one `vkAllocateDescriptorSets` validation error per run, pool created without `VK_DESCRIPTOR_TYPE_SAMPLED_IMAGE` (§11.2).
- **Batching as a Renderer service**, dissolving the global batchers (`Halo::global` beginDraw/nextDraw/endDraw [observed: halo.hpp:66-70], `Tail::global` [observed: tail.hpp:43-46]): begin/submit/flush per family at pass boundaries; `BMT_REPLICATED` is the declared hint.
- **Global config → specialization**: tesselation levels (`BodyTesselation`, shared static [observed: body_tesselation.hpp]) and dynamic-resolution (`BMT_DYNAMIC`) parameterize pipeline builds → config changes trigger REBUILDS (work-domain) with publish-swap, never in-frame stalls.
- **The two existing old-path borrows to absorb first** — **BOTH DISSOLVED (2026-07-12, S1; measurements §11.22)**: `BasicMesh` now draws through the MESH family (MeshFamilies.cpp; bodyShader include gone, globalVertProj/globalFrag re-homed to meshModules/bodyShaderInterface.hpp with bodyShader.hpp including back — old→new edge dying with the old path); `Renderer` halo drawing runs on the batching service (HALO service family, Halo::global untouched by the new path; texture via the `setTexHaloMap` seam mirror). ~~The hint DrawHelper seam entry remains~~ — **DISSOLVED (2026-07-12, §11.24)** with the HINT-family completion, as planned (that code was touched exactly once): DRAW_HINT_POS/s_hintPos/drawHintPos removed, hint circles ride the HINT batched service family.

### 10.2 Constraints it must satisfy
- **C1/effective-thread**: registry mutation (new pipeline published, variant swapped) = chain task; pipeline USE within the frame task is read-only. Creation is heavy (old path: static init + 4 dedicated shadow-pipeline build threads [observed: context.cpp:79-83]) → creation is WORK-DOMAIN (AsyncBuilder fits), publication is a publish task.
- **C3**: never build-on-first-use synchronously in the frame. Corollary — **base-variant-always-ready**: each family keeps one variant resident from init, mirroring D6's lowest-LoD-always-resident (something drawable always exists; a missing fancy variant degrades, never stalls). Same ladder philosophy, pipelines instead of textures.
- **D5 budget**: do NOT precompile the feature-combination cross-product (2 GiB floor); lazy variant builds via work domain + the EntityCore pipelineCache for fast rebuild [observed: "No changes in the pipelineCache" shutdown log].
- **Trait-driven routing**: the Renderer routes a module through exactly the passes its BMT_* traits declare (a trait not declared = a pass never received [BodyModule.hpp contract]); MINOR_BODY exemption applies at body level above module traits.
- **Frame structure**: per-frameIdx command buffers, 3 frames in flight [observed: Context, app.cpp:373-408]; teardown order deterministic (old `deleteShader` pattern).
- **Text is a delegation, not a family**: labels go through the Projector gravity-text path + shared s_font [observed: hints.cpp:72] — the API must expose access to that path (or the frame task's Projector), not reimplement text.

### 10.3 The design (2026-07-11, headers written — pending Vixy convergence)

Authoritative contract: `PipelineFamily.hpp` + the registry block in `Renderer.hpp` (headers are the medium; this section records decisions, evidence and opens — not the contract itself).

Evidence base: 3 file:line-backed sweeps (old-path pipeline inventory / EntityCore capabilities / new-path borrow surface). Scale being replaced: ~41 shared old-path graphics pipelines + 3 per Sun/Star instance + 3 per Ring instance + ≤255 shadow-blur ComputePipelines.

Decisions, each with the fact that forced it:
1. **Exactly four PassKinds = the four drawing types; NO-DEPTH is the reserved variant bit `VARIANT_NO_DEPTH`, not a pass.** All body-path color/trace/line/halo pipelines live in ONE subpass (PASS_BACKGROUND==PASS_MULTISAMPLE_DEPTH==0 [observed: context.hpp:43-49]); no-depth is pipeline state (old `pipelineNoDepth = pipeline+2` [observed: bodyShader.cpp:128]); ringed's legitimate no-depth non-support [observed: body.cpp:1055-1059] maps onto bit-drop fallback. Real separate render passes exist only for shadows and already exist [observed: app.cpp:347-363, renderSelfShadow/renderShadow].
2. **Bind-and-record**: a module calls `bind(family, wanted)` (resolved against the Renderer's current pass kind — a COLOR pipeline structurally cannot bind while TRACE records) then records its own geometry. Retained draw-description submission rejected: per-draw marshalling on the hot path for zero safety gain, forbids module-owned record patterns (ObjL::bind/draw), contradicts G10 + the normative `operator VkCommandBuffer`. Reopen only if a pass appears whose recording the render chain cannot serialize. [open #1]
3. **Variant model**: uint16 feature bits; axes declare effect (SPEC_CONSTANT | SHADER_SWAP | STATE_OVERRIDE) + dropPriority; base variant of every declared pass built synchronously at registration (base-always-ready ← C3, mirrors D6 lowest-LoD-resident); non-base variants lazy in the work domain, published as a chain task (← C1; precedent: 4-thread shadow builds + atomic publish [observed: context.cpp:79-100]; pipelineCache concurrent use is legal); never the cross-product (← D5). EntityCore's 3-update deferred pipeline destroy [observed: Pipeline.hpp:70] covers publish-swap across frames in flight. SHADER_SWAP axes port today's distinct shader files unchanged; a future layered mesh shader collapses them into SPEC_CONSTANT axes with no API change.
4. **Layout-invariance hard rule**: a variant axis never changes the descriptor contract — (family, pass) → layout is constant; layout-changing features are separate families. Evidence: artificial vs artificialShadowed set-count divergence [observed: bodyShader.cpp:240-285]; myEarthShadowed omits the global UBO set that shaderShadowedTes includes [observed: bodyShader.cpp:359-370 vs 385-395] — normalized at port, not reproduced.
5. **Declarative descriptor contract → pool aggregation**: the same SetDesc data builds the PipelineLayout and sizes Renderer-owned pools — a pool is always sized from the layouts it serves; structural closure of the §11.1 error class (new-path scope; the live old-path instance needs the EntityCore fix, open #2).
6. **Batching as a Renderer service under the occlusion contract** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: batched screen-space content (halo, hint) occluded as-if drawn farthest→closest against bodies — flush at the per-body command-buffer boundaries, the mechanism already live [observed: Renderer.cpp:36-59]; halo↔hint mutual order inside a segment is free whenever it improves performance. Dissolves `Halo::global` (+ the Renderer friend-poke into its pData), Tail's ride-on-halo coupling [observed: halo.cpp:55,72], and the DrawHelper hint batch at the seam. **Scope note** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: "per-category order" is EnvironmentModule frame-sequencing (§3.8), NOT a batch-flush rule.
7. **Frame context on the Renderer** (Projector/Navigator set before beginDraw) — **INVALIDATED** [vixy: 2026-07-11, review]: Projector/Navigator are old-path projection machinery (system-center reference); the new path doesn't use them at all — projection rides the parent↔child matrix chain routed through the closest common parent between Camera and target (better precision by construction). Delegating labels to Projector would carry old-path state into new-path drawing — the exact borrow class this API dissolves. The gravity-text machinery must be partially rewritten against the new chain (duplicate code between paths is possible); the module-facing channel is redefined by the projection-path investigation (§11.14). Header carriage removed. [open #3 → investigation delivered a proposal: Renderer text service as a pure function of `screenPos` + viewport + font — printGravity180's frame-matrix dependency was apparent, not real (projection-paths.md C7); pending Vixy convergence]
8. **Compute families in the same registry** (kind=COMPUTE; shadow-blur bank = one family, radius = variant, EAGER_ASYNC_ALL policy reproducing today's behavior); dispatch-side ergonomics deferred to G7's shadow implementation. [open #4]
9. **Describe-once → descriptor paradigm** [vixy: 2026-07-11, header review — supersedes the first C-shaped draft]: descriptions are one-time cold-path costs, so they use owning types (std::string/std::vector — lifetime correctness by construction, no externally-asserted `const char*`/pointer-array clauses); allocation returns a descriptor (integer index wrapped in `RegistryHandle<Tag>`: copies share via registry refcount, destruction releases implicitly); hot paths carry only the index; all enums are `enum class`, declared before use. Structural payoff: the SetRole enum (FAMILY_OWNED/GLOBAL_UBO/EXTERNAL) **dissolved** — all three were ownership cases of one mechanism, "a `SetContract` allocated once, referenced by handle" (global UBO = a pre-allocated contract via `globalUboContract()`; traceLayout sharing = the same handle held by two families; a desc holding handles keeps its references alive by construction). Root cause of the first draft's shape, traced: hot-path constraints (zero-alloc, static tables) misapplied to a cold path + matching existing style weighed over long-run optimality — the criterion "optimal in the long run (decades), code debt prevention is critical, not nice-to-have" [vixy: 2026-07-11] now explicit.

Coverage check (every §10.1 family walked against the inventory; the walk itself forced two contract additions — `FixedState::stripBreaks` [Tail's primitive-restart strips, observed: tail.cpp] and `SpecConstant` device/config-derived values [float64 id 7 across families, sun big-halo viewport height, artificialShadowed shadow resolution — the "global config → specialization" input, rebuilt-not-stalled on change]; after which every family is expressible without struct change): mesh-layered (SHADER_SWAP now → spec-const later) · OJM + OJM_SHADOWED (rule 4) · RING color+trace, RING_ASTEROID instanced+batched · orbit/trail/axis/grid as separate families where push-constant contracts differ · HINT (batched) / POINTER · SUN / SUN_HALO / STAR_VIEWER / CORONA · ATM_EXT (SRC_ALPHA + MAX blend-op expressible in FixedState) · TAIL (instanced batch, shared geometry via VertexArray instance-rate) · trace/self-shadow/shadow-shape = Renderer-owned service families (traceLayout sharing = one SetContract held by both families). Sun/pointer prebuilt static command buffers dissolve into frame-task recording at port time (C1 requires it anyway).

**Open convergence points** — (1)–(5) **RESOLVED [vixy: 2026-07-12, plan approval — the approved plan carried these as explicit recommendations; approval = acceptance; if any was not intended as such, flag and it reopens]**: (1) bind-and-record confirmed as the module contract. (2) SetMgr SAMPLED_IMAGE pool-table fix in EntityCore — authorized, applied uncommitted in the submodule (precedent: Taskable/ASmooth edits). (3) label/text channel: Renderer text service, pure function of `screenPos` + viewport + font (projection-paths.md C7) — accepted. (4) compute dispatch deferred to G7 — accepted. (5) variant budget uint16, bit 15 reserved — sufficient. (6) depth-slice margin policy — **RESOLVED** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: boundingRadius is DEFINED as the smallest sphere enclosing the whole traced body — inclusive by definition, which is exactly why one value serves both the visibility cone test and depth bounding; no margin factor (the old 1.1 compensated a non-inclusive radius). Documented at BodyModule.hpp member + Renderer.hpp clearDepth. Consequence: a module whose drawn extent exceeds its mesh radius (terrain, shells, rings) must RETURN that larger radius from update() — the contract, not a renderer guard, carries the safety.

135. **§5.47 CLOSED — THE QUEUE WAS DRAINED ALL ALONG, TO WHOEVER HAD ASKED FOR THE LOGS (2026-08-02, Opus 5 executor dispatched by Claude Fable 5, task F27; code `d13681eb`, harness `23f1833`/`7049558`/this entry).** The row's three hypotheses are all answered at source `[observed: src/tools/io.cpp @ d9de42ac]`: the queue **is** drained, on EVERY pass of `ServerSocket::run` (`checkDataToSend`, io.cpp:388, the loop's own wait being a 1 ms `SDLNet_CheckSockets`), and it is drained through `broadcast` (io.cpp:641-655), which addresses the clients whose `clientBroadcastTab` entry is true — a table set true in exactly ONE place, the **`$LOGON`** command (io.cpp:614). So an answer went to the FEEDBACK SUBSCRIBERS and never to the issuer as such, and with nobody subscribed it was popped off the queue and lost in silence; the client COUNT is irrelevant, so *"only when a second client is connected"* is REFUTED. The root is one hop earlier: the origin of a request was known at `computeNormalString(client, string)` and dropped on the next line, and `setOutput` had no addressee to name — a structure that does not carry what is asked of it (I6). Delivered: a request and an answer are a `ClientMessage` naming the SLOT **and** the connection id that slot held (slots are reused — an answer must never land on the next tenant, I5); `getInput` latches the request being served and CLEARS the latch when the batch is drained, so a script's or the TUI's output is not attributed to the last client that spoke; `setOutput` stamps; `deliver` sends to that connection **and** to the subscribers, minus the double copy. **The addressed copy is ADDITIVE — every byte that reached a client before still reaches it** (veto point; whether the log channel should keep carrying other clients' answers is §5.72, Vixy's). Measured on the wire, six legs, twelve fresh launches, `harness/f27_reply.py`: the §11.118(i) scenario reproduces **6.007 s / 6.006 s of silence** pre-fix and answers in **0.002 s / 0.006 s** delivered, with the six commands the row lists as working green on that same connection in both (select 1 203 563 px>8 on a 2 166 876-lit-px frame, 0 refusals); the SAME socket after `$LOGON` answers in **0.002 s pre-fix** — the positive map that makes the silence a routing fact and not an absent instrument; a subscribed listener + a plain issuer go from **0 / 1** to **1 / 1**, before AND after the issuer's connection is replaced in the same slot; an HTTP-issued answer (issuer already gone) falls back to the subscribers by the id check, and the next tenant of the slot inherits nothing; an answer nobody can receive is now REPORTED instead of vanishing (§2(f)). Content verified against `body action dual_dump`'s `control` object field by field in a scene where the authorities differ by **6.16°** in heading — the reply follows the path that DRAWS (B33's bar). §11.118(f)'s recorded cost is discharged: the heading pin is now readable with **3/3** samples in ONE launch across two `experimental_path` toggles, against **0/3** pre-fix. 137. **A 187 MiB DERIVATION SAT IN THE HISTORY FOR THREE WEEKS WHILE THE ENTRY RECORDING IT SAID IT WAS UNCOMMITTED (2026-08-22, Opus 5 (1M context), Vixy-directed; code `76ee38c7` UNTOUCHED — the code repo was not rewritten; four more ELFs (~555 MiB) hung off `refs/original` → `df34cff`, an aborted `add -A` sync commit **whose entire content was those four binaries**, left by a 2026-08-07 filter-branch that stopped one step short. **ROOT — a convention whose only enforcement was attention**: *"uncommitted per the artifacts convention"* is stated in a dozen delivery entries **including §11.132(j), which lists this very file among them**; the tree and the record disagreed for three weeks and no channel carried the disagreement, because git validates no claim written in prose and `add -A` reads only `.gitignore`. **THE GUARD IS THE DELIVERABLE** (`0fc5e4e`), three layers each assuming the previous failed: `.gitignore` name shapes (**proxy** — a new name walks past it); `githooks/pre-commit` on **CONTENT** (**the criterion**: ELF/PE/Mach-O/ar refused **at any size** — a 2 MiB stripped binary is as much a derivation as a 187 MiB one — plus any blob ≥ 8 MiB, a ceiling *read off the corpus*, whose largest legitimate blob is 3.06 MB, ~12× under GitHub's hard limit so it fires while the commit is still cheap to fix); GitHub's 100 MiB rejection as layer 3, the late and expensive one. `install.sh` wires `core.hooksPath` in BOTH repos and then **proves the hook fires** against a throwaway index — ELF refused, 9 MiB refused, **ordinary 12-byte file passed** (the negative control is load-bearing: a hook that refuses everything passes both positives). **Stated limit**: git config is per-clone and is not cloned, so a fresh clone runs no hook until `install.sh` runs; nothing inside a repository can close that. **CORRECTED SAME DAY (c1)** [vixy, structural]: the guard led with a **size ceiling** and hand-wrote four magic numbers for type — mistaking what made this instance *visible* (187 MiB) for what made it *wrong*. The criterion is the **one-way transformation**: the source determines the binary, the binary does not determine the source, so a 12 KiB `.pyc` is as much a derivation as a 187 MiB executable **and a size gate never sees it**. `file(1)` is now the primary channel (content, unconditional, ~15 compiled formats), magic bytes a second channel with a different failure mode, and size is demoted to the **residue** — a layer that explicitly decides nothing and hands the semantic question back. Measured: **3 175 tracked files of both repos staged as new → 0 refusals**; an 8-byte ELF refused identically to the 187 MiB one; a 12-byte `.pyc` (no magic-byte fallback) proves the `file(1)` channel is live. **THE PURGE** (`purge-path.sh`) makes rewrite and citation-repoint **ONE pass** — 20 tokens across 14 tracked files, 4 inside commit messages, **0 in the code repo** — since shas are load-bearing data here and a second pass re-shas whatever the first just repointed. Plus the measured surprise that reshaped the design: filter-branch's closing `reset --hard` **deletes the purged file from the working tree** — so the copies are saved, restored and md5-compared. **VERIFIED CROSS-REPO against a pristine baseline** (a clone whose `refs/original` still holds the original): **44/44 trees identical modulo the removed path, 44/44 author+committer identities and both dates identical, exactly 3 messages changed**, each a citation resolving to a commit with the **identical subject**, token lengths preserved. **PRUNED in the same pass** [vixy]: 519.52 MiB loose → **88.91 MiB packed**, 0 loose, fsck clean, all five blobs gone from the database — safe because each binary's md5 + source commit are recorded and §11.126 already rebuilt one **bit-identical**; what is lost is this operation's own rollback path. The clean-tree gate earned itself mid-run: **the tree was not inert** — Vixy was writing D21/D23 replies into `DECISIONS_PENDING.md`, and `reset --hard` would have destroyed them. RESIDUALS: the second tree still carries the blob across 84 commits, deliberately untouched [vixy: *"This tree only"*, the merge being manual on conflicting QUEUE ids] — `--repo=` takes it unchanged; 1 235 tracked `harness/artifacts/` files predate/bypass their own ignore rule, recorded not acted on. 146. **§5.77's OWED ENUMERATION — THE STARTUP FAILURES THAT REPORT ONLY ON THE CONSOLE ARE **37**, ON FIVE CHANNELS, AND THE UNIFORM ADDITIVE FIX DOES NOT EXIST (2026-08-09, Opus 5 executor dispatched by Claude Fable 5, task F36; NO product code changed — code tree clean at `04ae1d3e` at open AND close; harness `f36_enum.py`/`f36_probe.py`/`f36_reach.py`/`f36_class.py` + this entry).** The row owed *"an enumeration of the other startup paths that report only on `stderr`"* and gated a fix on the class being uniformly, purely-additively routable through `cLog`. **The enumeration is PAID: 37 startup failure reports the app's own log does not carry. A **fourth reports NOWHERE**: `StringArray::initFromFile` returns silently on a missing file, so the spectral catalogue is empty and nothing anywhere says so. The artifacts were read as comparable records across runs while every verification surface only ever compared within one. So *authoritative* is **ill-posed** for the accumulating fields and there was never a conflict to resolve. **No §5 row minted**: whether that path is meant to be idempotent at fixed jd needs a source read this session did not do, and a defect claim would assert more than was measured.
